If a panel is functionally open:

it is functionally open.

Visual transition may continue around it.


## 06.118 — NO FOCUS ON MOVING TARGET

When moving focus programmatically to a new heading/control:

that target should not simultaneously travel 300px.

Prefer static/settled focus target.


## 06.119 — ARIA LIVE + MOTION

Do not announce every intermediate animation state.

For proximity recommendation:

announce final result:

“Recommandation mise à jour : Boxing Center Portet.”

Not:

evaluating… 3 routes… 2 routes… Portet…

Assistive technology needs decision state, not choreography.


## 06.120 — MOTION AND COLOR

Remember:

selection is not merely animated red.

Selection state simultaneously uses:

- text;
- geometry;
- marker;

- route state;
- color.

Reduced-motion mode still fully communicates it.


## 06.121 — ANIMATION + CORE WEB VITALS

No effect is allowed to destabilize initial layout.

Reserve sizes.

Hero visualization gets known container dimensions.

Images get width/height/aspect ratio.

No animation that unexpectedly moves content after load.

Motion must not create CLS for artistic effect.


## 06.122 — LCP PRIORITY

Do not make a massive interactive hero asset the only meaningful hero content.

Text appears first.

If photography is LCP candidate:

optimize it.

If route graphic is advanced:

it initializes later.


## 06.123 — INP PRIORITY

User interactions must respond immediately.

Animation work cannot block:

- menu;
- selector;
- filter;
- form;
- recommendation.

A beautiful 700ms animation driven by a 300ms main-thread stall has failed.


## 06.124 — SCROLL PERFORMANCE

No JS listener doing expensive work on raw `scroll`.

Use:

- IntersectionObserver;
- passive listeners where appropriate;
- requestAnimationFrame scheduling if truly needed.

Never mutate hundreds of DOM nodes per wheel event.


## 06.125 — MOTION AUDIT MODES

Every major page must be QA-tested in four modes:

A. NORMAL DESKTOP B. NORMAL MOBILE C. REDUCED MOTION D. JS DISABLED / FAILED

Plus low-end performance testing.


## 06.126 — NORMAL DESKTOP PASS

Must feel:

precise.

Not underanimated.

Not overanimated.

Pointer effects subtle.

Page continuity useful.


## 06.127 — NORMAL MOBILE PASS

Must feel:

direct.

No lost effects.

No desktop leftovers.

No heavy graphics preventing immediate interaction.


## 06.128 — REDUCED MOTION PASS

Must feel:


### DESIGNED.

Not:

“the cool version but broken.”

Color, typography, geometry and state must carry the identity independently.


## 06.129 — JS-OFF PASS

Core journey remains:

- readable;
- navigable;
- crawlable;
- useful.

Selector becomes normal links.

Recommendation may become comparison/help page.

Planning remains accessible through authoritative links/content.


## 06.130 — MOTION HATER TEST

For every animation ask:


### Can I describe what changed?

No → delete.


### Does motion clarify why?

No → probably delete.


### Does it delay the user?

Yes → redesign.


### Does it survive interruption?

No → redesign.


### Is reduced motion genuinely equivalent?

No → fail.


### Does mobile have a deliberate version?

No → fail.


### Is this merely an agency-site cliché?

Yes → delete.


### Could CSS/platform APIs do this cheaper?

Yes → don't add a library.


## 06.131 — FORBIDDEN MOTION PATTERNS

Locked prohibition:

NO scroll-jacking NO Lenis by default NO perpetual cursor follower NO magnetic buttons NO per-letter body text reveals NO huge parallax NO spinning 3D glove NO looping particles NO continuous route animation NO every-section fade-up factory NO multi-second preload theatre NO fake loading NO full-screen wipes on every link NO shaking error fields NO bouncing controls NO animated counters from zero NO autoplay audio NO motion-only information NO global React/Motion dependency NO transition: all


## 06.132 — APPROVED SIGNATURE MOTION MOMENTS

The entire site needs only a few memorable moments.


### MOMENT 01 — ORIGIN

Colomiers establishes the route field.


### MOMENT 02 — INTENT

User selects what they want.

Possibilities narrow.


### MOMENT 03 — RESOLUTION

Proximity Engine converges.

Red is earned.


### MOMENT 04 — CONTINUITY

Selected route carries into the relevant discipline/club experience.


### MOMENT 05 — ARRIVAL

Final CTA reflects the resolved destination.

That's enough.


## 06.133 — THE MOTION +1

The project's visual +1 was already strong.

Motion makes it deeper.


### MOTION HAS MEMORY.

Definition:

The interface does not repeatedly perform unrelated entrance animations. Motion remembers the user's previous decision state and transforms the same route primitive across subsequent contexts.

Example:

HERO route = possibility ↓ SELECTOR route = preference ↓ PROXIMITY ENGINE route = computation ↓ DESTINATION route = recommendation ↓ PLANNING route = time ↓ FINAL CTA

```text
route = commitment
```

Same object.

Different semantic state.

This is significantly more coherent than:

custom transition library + fancy reveals.


## 06.134 — SECOND +1: ENERGY CONSERVATION

Another useful doctrine:


### THE MORE CERTAIN THE USER BECOMES, THE LESS THE INTERFACE MOVES.

At the beginning:

several possibilities have controlled movement.

During selection:

movement concentrates.

After resolution:

the interface becomes calm.

That means the behavioral experience mirrors cognition:

UNCERTAINTY movement / branching ↓

DECISION convergence ↓ CERTAINTY stillness

The end state should feel physically settled.

This is unusual and highly appropriate.


## 06.135 — MOTION INTENSITY CURVE

HERO ███████ TRUTH █ SELECTOR █████ DISCIPLINES ███ PROXIMITY ENGINE ████████ DESTINATIONS ███ FIRST SESSION ██ PLANNING ████ EVIDENCE █

FINAL ██ FOOTER ░

Motion intensity decreases after the engine.

The user has enough information.

We stop performing.


## 06.136 — REFERENCE FUSION — MOTION

**Mechanism Source layer Operation Colomiers result +1**

Cross-view continuity

Native View Transition API / Astro

technical graft real document navigation with selective continuity

route state persists conceptually without forcing SPA architecture

Controlled motion preference

W3C reduced-motion guidance

foundational constraint

static-first animation architecture

reduced motion remains branded, not fallback

Compositor-f irst animation

web.dev rendering guidance

technical compression

transform/opacity dominate frequent motion

paint-heavy route drawing confined to small meaningful events

Narrative spatial motion

Lusion / high-end interactive references

recontextualization route topology represents choices

topology progressively becomes verified recommendation

Truth-genera ted experience

Internal Ramonville

escalation movement encodes factual resolution

failure/no-match gets an honest visual state

First-session storytelling

Internal Portet/Minimes

subtraction quiet sequence after interactive climax

certainty intentionally reduces motion

Round rhythm

Boxing domain itself

abstraction bursts of interaction separated by visual rest

motion intensity follows cognitive rather than literal boxing rounds


## 06.137 — ASTRO IMPLEMENTATION DECISION TREE

```text
DOES IT NEED MOTION?
│
├── NO
│ └── static HTML/CSS
│
└── YES
│
├── simple UI state?
│ └── CSS
│
├── interruptible sequence?
│ └── WAAPI
│
├── DOM reorder/state continuity?
│ └── View Transition API
│
├── route path?
│ └── SVG first
│
└── SVG genuinely insufficient?
│
├── Canvas
└── WebGL only after gate
```

This decision tree is locked.


## 06.138 — IMPLEMENTATION DEFAULTS

ROUTING Astro MPA PAGE TRANSITIONS Native cross-document View Transitions where supported STATE TRANSITIONS CSS / WAAPI ROUTE VISUAL SVG SCROLL Native SCROLL OBSERVATION IntersectionObserver ANIMATION SCHEDULING Shared RAF only if necessary MOTION FRAMEWORK None by default REACT None solely for motion REDUCED MOTION Static-first + prefers-reduced-motion FALLBACK Immediate complete state


## 06.139 — MOTION IMPLEMENTATION CONTRACT

The implementation LLM may adjust:

- individual duration ± roughly 15% for optical tuning;
- SVG implementation details;
- exact transform distance inside defined bounds;
- native API fallback code;
- IntersectionObserver thresholds;
- route path geometry.

It may **not** reinterpret:

- native scroll;
- MPA-first architecture;
- no motion library by default;
- no React solely for motion;
- Red Is Earned timing;
- route state semantics;
- reduced-motion completeness;
- latest-intent-wins;
- interruptibility;
- no fake loading;
- no continuous decorative animation;
- motion intensity reduction after resolution;
- static-first content;
- mobile parity.


## 06.140 — MOTION QA CHECKLIST

Before release:


### Initial load

Content visible before JS.


### Hero

No input lock.


### Routes

No decorative meaningless movement.


### Selector

Immediate response.


### Rapid input

Latest choice wins.


### Recommendation

Result explainable before/without choreography.


### Planning

Filtering remains fast.


### Mobile

No clipped/pinned broken states.


### Reduced motion

All meaningful motion eliminated/replaced.


### Back/Forward

Normal browser expectations preserved.


### External links

No artificial portal delay.


### Keyboard

Focus does not land on moving targets.


### Screen reader

Final states announced, not animation stages.


### 200% zoom

Sticky/motion systems remain operable.


### Performance

No persistent dropped-frame mechanism.


### Hidden tab

Continuous work suspended.


### JS failure

Primary site remains useful.


## 06.141 — PART 06 LOCKED DOCTRINES

We now lock:


### CONTROLLED IMPACT

Motion is economical and meaningful.


### NATIVE SCROLL IS SACRED

No scroll-jacking.


### LATEST INTENT WINS

Animations obey humans, not queues.


### MOTION HAS MEMORY

The same route evolves through the journey.


### ENERGY CONSERVATION

More certainty = less movement.


### RED IS EARNED IN TIME AS WELL AS COLOR

The resolved route becomes red only after the decision is defensible.


### MPA FIRST

Astro stays document-native.


### PLATFORM BEFORE LIBRARY

CSS → WAAPI → View Transitions → SVG → heavier technologies only with evidence.


### STATIC FIRST

Motion adds meaning. It never provides basic meaning.


## 06.142 — SYSTEM IN ONE SENTENCE

**Boxing Center Colomiers moves only when knowledge changes: possibilities** **branch, intent narrows them, verified data resolves a route, red marks the** **earned decision, and once the visitor knows where to go, the interface** **becomes still.**

That is the motion system.


<a id="part-07-component-interaction-architecture"></a>

# PART 07 — Component & Interaction Architecture


## 07.0 — PURPOSE

Parts 01–06 established:

- entity truth;
- search architecture;
- user journey;
- homepage architecture;
- visual language;
- motion language.

Part 07 converts those principles into a **finite component grammar**.

The implementation LLM is no longer allowed to solve the same interface problem differently on every page.

The rule becomes:

**SAME INFORMATION JOB → SAME COMPONENT FAMILY → SAME STATES → SAME ACCESSIBILITY → SAME DATA AUTHORITY.**

This prevents:

- card drift;
- CTA drift;
- spacing drift;
- inconsistent mobile transformations;
- duplicated business facts;
- random ARIA;
- SEO/GEO inconsistencies;
- three different planning interfaces;
- five different external-link styles;
- arbitrary AI-generated UI.


## 07.1 — COMPONENT PHILOSOPHY

Components are not primarily visual objects.

**They are contracts between data, semantics, interaction and presentation.**

A component exists only when one of these is true:

1. the same information job recurs; 2. it owns meaningful behavior; 3. it owns important accessibility behavior;

4. it centralizes volatile facts; 5. it prevents implementation drift.

We do **not** componentize every `<div>`.


## 07.2 — COMPONENT HIERARCHY

The system has four levels.

LEVEL 0 — TOKENS color / type / spacing / motion / route LEVEL 1 — PRIMITIVES button / link / label / field / image / rule / node LEVEL 2 — INFORMATION COMPONENTS club / discipline / price / evidence / schedule result LEVEL 3 — PRODUCT SYSTEMS intent selector / proximity engine / schedule explorer LEVEL 4 — PAGE COMPOSITIONS home / MMA / children / pricing / first session

Pages compose systems.

Systems compose information components.

Information components compose primitives.


## 07.3 — NEVER CREATE PAGE-SPECIFIC DUPLICATES

Forbidden:

HomeClubCard

MmaClubCard PricingClubCard MobileClubCard

if all four represent the same entity.

Correct:

ClubDestination

with controlled variants.


## 07.4 — DATA FIRST

Every component displaying factual Boxing Center information receives structured data.

It must not contain factual business truth hard-coded inside its template.

Bad:

<p>MMA à Portet</p>

Good:

<ClubCapability capability={club.capabilities.mma} />

The distinction is fundamental.


## 07.5 — SHARED CONTENT MODEL

Conceptually:

```text
type VerificationState =
| 'verified'
| 'time-sensitive'
| 'pending'
| 'conflicted'
| 'prohibited';
interface Claim<T> {
value: T | null;
state: VerificationState;
source?: string;
verifiedAt?: string;
public?: boolean;
}
```

Then:

```text
interface Club {
id: 'minimes' | 'portet';
name: string;
canonicalUrl: string;
address: Claim<Address>;
openingHours: Claim<OpeningHours>;
disciplines: Claim<DisciplineId[]>;
facilities: Claim<FacilityId[]>;
audiences: Claim<AudienceId[]>;
schedules: Claim<ScheduleEntry[]>;
offers: Claim<OfferId[]>;
images: MediaAsset[];
}
```

Presentation components consume **claims**, not assumptions.


## 07.6 — COMPONENT TRUTH BEHAVIOR

If state = `verified`:

render.

If state = `time-sensitive`:

render + freshness where relevant.

If state = `pending`:

do not make affirmative public claim.

If state = `conflicted`:

do not silently choose one value.

If state = `prohibited`:

never render/infer.

This is how design and GEO remain connected.


## 07.7 — COMPONENT INVENTORY

Core system:

**ID Component Job**

C01 `SiteHeader` global orientation

C02 `PrimaryNavigation` expose primary tasks

C03 `MobileNavigationD`

`ialog`

mobile navigation

C04 `RouteGraph` visualization of relationship

C05 `RouteNode` state/place/decision

C06 `IntentSelector` capture visitor goal

C07 `DisciplineIndex` explain practice options

C08 `ProximityEngine` choose relevant real club

C09 `RecommendationRes`

`ult`

explain recommendation

C10 `ClubDestination` represent a physical venue

C11 `ClubComparison` compare verified differences

C12 `ScheduleExplorer` orient by time/discipline

C13 `ScheduleEntry` one session result

C14 `PricingExplorer` orient pricing

C15 `PriceFact` verified price representation

C16 `FirstSessionSeque`

`nce`

reduce first-visit anxiety

C17 `EvidenceLedger` factual trust

C18 `VerifiedFact` volatile factual datum

C19 `ProvenanceImage` real image + origin context

C20 `FreshnessLabel` source freshness

C21 `ContactOrientatio`

`nForm`

qualified human handoff

C22 `Breadcrumbs` page location

C23 `Disclosure` optional secondary detail

C24 `ActionLink/Button` command hierarchy

C25 `StatePanel` empty/error/success

C26 `SiteFooter` entity/knowledge map

C27 `NotFoundRoute` useful 404

C28 `ContentSection` editorial composition primitive


### C01 — SITE HEADER


## 07.8 — Responsibility

The header answers:

Where am I?

and:

What are my four primary options?

It must never become an ad bar.


## 07.9 — Anatomy

```text
SITE HEADER
├── Official Boxing Center logo
├── Context label: DEPUIS COLOMIERS
├── Primary navigation
└── Primary action: COMMENCER
```

Not:

`BOXING CENTER COLOMIERS` as a pseudo-sixth-club logo.


## 07.10 — Desktop semantic structure

```text
<header>
<a href="/" aria-label="Boxing Center — accueil depuis Colomiers">
<!-- official logo -->
</a>
<span>Depuis Colomiers</span>
<nav aria-label="Navigation principale">
...
</nav>
</header>
```


## 07.11 — States


### Hero

Visually integrated.


### Scrolled

Solid controlled surface.


### Focused decision mode

Reduced navigation noise.


### Mobile

logo/context + Menu.


## 07.12 — Forbidden

No:

- promo ticker;
- five club logos;
- phone number plus six icons plus nav;
- permanent giant offer;
- floating pill navigation;
- disappearing-on-scroll navigation.


### C02 — PRIMARY NAVIGATION


## 07.13 — Items

Locked:

DISCIPLINES TROUVER MON CLUB

PLANNINGS TARIFS COMMENCER

Disciplines may expose sub-navigation.

Others are direct links.


## 07.14 — Disciplines disclosure

If desktop Disciplines opens a submenu, implement it as a **disclosure/navigation pattern**, not an application `menu` widget.

Reason:

these are ordinary navigation links, not application commands.

Use a real button controlling a region/list.

WAI's disclosure pattern expects a button whose `aria-expanded` accurately reflects visibility, with Enter/Space activation.


## 07.15 — Keyboard

Tab reaches:

DISCIPLINES button

Then normal links.

Do not implement exotic arrow-key menubar navigation unless the component truly becomes an ARIA menubar—which it should not.

Native web navigation is simpler and more predictable.


### C03 — MOBILE NAVIGATION DIALOG


## 07.16 — Why dialog?

On mobile the menu becomes a deliberate full-screen navigation state.

When open:

background must not remain interactive.

This is a legitimate modal interaction.

WAI's current dialog pattern requires focus to enter the dialog, Tab/Shift+Tab to remain within it, Escape to close it, and focus normally to return to the trigger after closing.


## 07.17 — Anatomy

MENU BOXING CENTER DEPUIS COLOMIERS DISCIPLINES BOXE ANGLAISE MMA BOXING FITNESS BOXE ENFANTS TROUVER MON CLUB PLANNINGS TARIFS PREMIÈRE SÉANCE CONTACT CLUBS PHYSIQUES MINIMES ↗ PORTET ↗ [FERMER]


## 07.18 — Semantics

Prefer a robust native `<dialog>` implementation where cross-browser/AT testing validates behavior.

Otherwise implement the WAI modal-dialog contract explicitly.

Do not blindly copy APG example code; W3C itself warns that APG examples are illustrative and require real assistive-technology testing before production use.


## 07.19 — Opening

Trigger:

<button type="button" aria-haspopup="dialog" > Menu </button>

On open:

focus moves logically inside.

Large navigation content may focus its heading first rather than an arbitrary first link.


### C04 — ROUTE GRAPH


## 07.20 — Responsibility

Visualize:

- possibilities;
- relationships;
- progress;
- selection;

- destination;
- time.

It is **not** the source of business facts.


## 07.21 — Data model

```text
interface RouteGraphData {
nodes: RouteNodeData[];
edges: RouteEdgeData[];
activeNode?: string;
selectedPath?: string[];
mode:
| 'possibility'
| 'selection'
| 'recommendation'
| 'timeline'
| 'progress';
}
```


## 07.22 — SVG-first

Render as SVG.

Example structure:

```text
<svg aria-hidden="true">
<path ... />
<circle ... />
</svg>
```

If the graph simply duplicates surrounding textual information:

`aria-hidden="true"`.

If graph itself becomes an actual interactive selector:

the interactive controls must exist as normal semantic HTML controls either within or alongside it.

Never expose dozens of meaningless SVG geometry nodes to screen readers.


## 07.23 — States

LATENT AVAILABLE FOCUSED SELECTED COMPLETED UNAVAILABLE

Selected is never communicated by red alone.


### C05 — ROUTE NODE


## 07.24 — Node meanings

A node may represent:

- Colomiers origin;
- discipline;
- decision criterion;
- physical club;
- time;
- completion.

The component requires a semantic `kind`.

```text
type RouteNodeKind =
| 'origin'
| 'choice'
| 'criterion'
| 'destination'
| 'time'
| 'completion';
```

No generic decorative nodes.


### C06 — INTENT SELECTOR


## 07.25 — Product job

Translate:

“I want to do something”

into:

a meaningful Boxing Center practice intent.


## 07.26 — Initial options

APPRENDRE À BOXER ESSAYER LE MMA ME REMETTRE EN FORME TROUVER POUR MON ENFANT PASSER UN CAP JE NE SAIS PAS ENCORE


## 07.27 — Native controls first

These are mutually exclusive options.

Therefore use **native radio controls inside** `<fieldset>` **+** `<legend>` wherever possible.

Not custom `div role="radio"` unless native controls genuinely cannot achieve the design.

WAI recommends grouping related form controls and provides the Radio Group pattern where custom radios are required; the ARIA pattern includes Space and arrow-key behavior.

Our preferred solution:

native radios styled as large editorial rows.

This gives accessibility **without rebuilding radio behavior manually**.


## 07.28 — Semantic anatomy

```text
<fieldset>
<legend>Pourquoi veux-tu mettre les gants ?</legend>
<label>
<input
type="radio"
name="objectif"
value="boxe"
/>
Apprendre à boxer
</label>
...
</fieldset>
```


## 07.29 — Visual state

Label acts as full click/touch surface.

Selected:

- explicit native/check marker or controlled visual marker;
- stronger weight;
- route activation;
- red signal.

Focus:

full row gets strong focus treatment.


## 07.30 — State contract

```text
interface IntentSelection {
goal?: GoalId;
priority?: PriorityId;
audience?: AudienceId;
}
```

Do not overload the component with club recommendation logic.

It captures intent.

`ProximityEngine` interprets it.


## 07.31 — Persistence

May sync into:

- query parameters;
- session state.

The selector remains usable without persistence.


## 07.32 — No-JS fallback

Labels remain form controls.

A submit/link pathway can route toward corresponding discipline pages.

Critical functionality does not vanish.


### C07 — DISCIPLINE INDEX


## 07.33 — Responsibility

Represent the four main practice worlds without turning them into generic service cards.


## 07.34 — Data

```text
interface Discipline {
id: DisciplineId;
name: string;
summary: string;
beginnerStatus: string;
audiences: AudienceId[];
clubAvailability: ClubId[];
image?: MediaAsset;
canonicalUrl: string;
}
```

Availability is generated from verified location data.

Do not write it independently inside discipline content.


## 07.35 — Anatomy

DISCIPLINE NAME ONE-LINE HUMAN DEFINITION BEGINNER CONTEXT WHERE AVAILABLE IMAGE / EVIDENCE EXPLORE →


## 07.36 — Dynamic emphasis

Intent can alter visual priority.

It must not remove crawlable discipline content.

Search engines/static users still receive the full discipline set.


### C08 — PROXIMITY ENGINE


## 07.37 — This is the most important product component

It must be engineered like a **transparent decision system**, not a marketing quiz.


## 07.38 — Inputs

Only criteria that alter the recommendation.

Potential launch model:

```text
interface ProximityCriteria {
goal?: GoalId;
discipline?: DisciplineId;
audience?: AudienceId;
level?: 'first-time' | 'beginner' | 'experienced';
availability?: AvailabilityPreference;
}
```

Do not ask:

- age if it doesn't alter output;
- phone/email;
- arbitrary lifestyle questions;
- “what motivates you?” if unused.

Every question earns its existence.


## 07.39 — Engine data contract

```text
interface ClubMatch {
clubId: ClubId;
eligible: boolean;
score?: number;
reasons: MatchReason[];
conflicts: MatchConflict[];
}
```

But public presentation should avoid meaningless:

Portet score: 87/100.

The algorithm may use internal weighting.

The interface explains **reasons**.


## 07.40 — Recommendation transparency

Every match reason references a factual capability.

```text
interface MatchReason {
criterion: string;
claimId: string;
explanation: string;
}
```

Example:

Portet correspond à ton choix MMA car le programme vérifié inclut actuellement cette discipline.

Not:

Notre intelligence recommande Portet.


## 07.41 — No opaque AI needed

The recommendation system does **not** require an LLM.

Deterministic verified rules are preferable.

Advantages:

- predictable;
- explainable;
- fast;
- testable;
- cheap;
- accessible;
- stable.

This is product intelligence, not an excuse to add AI.


## 07.42 — Output scenarios


### One clear match

Present recommendation.


### Two valid matches

Present comparison.


### No exact match

Present closest verified alternatives.


### Conflicted data

Do not compute on conflicted criterion.

Either omit it or surface inability to confirm.


## 07.43 — Accessibility

Criteria use native:

- radios;
- checkboxes;
- selects where appropriate.

Recommendation update:

use a polite status region containing only the **final meaningful update**.

Example:

```text
<p role="status">
Recommandation mise à jour : Boxing Center Portet.
</p>
```

Do not announce animation stages.


### C09 — RECOMMENDATION RESULT


## 07.44 — Anatomy

OPTION LA PLUS COHÉRENTE BOXING CENTER PORTET WHY • reason 1 • reason 2 • reason 3 SOURCE/FRESHNESS if relevant [VOIR LE CLUB ↗] [VOIR LES CRÉNEAUX] [CHANGER MES CRITÈRES]


## 07.45 — It must contain a sentence

Do not rely on icons.

Example:

Selon tes critères actuels — MMA, débutant, soir — Portet correspond le mieux aux données de programme disponibles.

This is excellent for:

human comprehension screen readers GEO/extractability.


## 07.46 — Result state

Red can now dominate the resolved route.

This is one of the few components where **Red Is Earned** reaches maximum intensity.


### C10 — CLUB DESTINATION


## 07.47 — Responsibility

Represent a **real physical Boxing Center location**.

This component has higher truth requirements than nearly any other.


## 07.48 — Required data

```text
interface ClubDestinationProps {
club: Club;
context?: DisciplineId | GoalId;
showAddress?: boolean;
showCapabilities?: boolean;
showSource?: boolean;
}
```


## 07.49 — Anatomy

BOXING CENTER PORTET PORTET-SUR-GARONNE [REAL PHOTO] RELEVANT VERIFIED DIFFERENTIATOR ADDRESS RELEVANT DISCIPLINES RELEVANT FACILITY [VISITER LE SITE OFFICIEL ↗]


## 07.50 — No symmetry requirement

This component may display different quantities of evidence for different clubs.

Do not pad facts for visual balance.


## 07.51 — External-link semantics

Use a real anchor.

```text
<a href="https://...">
Découvrir Boxing Center Portet
<span aria-hidden="true">↗</span>
</a>
```

The accessible name already communicates destination.

Do not add meaningless:

aria-label="opens external link"

to every external anchor unless new-tab behavior makes extra notice necessary.


### C11 — CLUB COMPARISON


## 07.52 — Responsibility

Answer:

What meaningful difference could change my choice?

Not:

Which card has more checkmarks?


## 07.53 — Criteria selection

Display only criteria that:

1. are verified; 2. differ meaningfully or genuinely aid decision; 3. belong to the current intent.

For MMA query:

MMA availability matters.

For children:

age/programme availability matters.

Do not display:

“friendly atmosphere: yes / yes”.


## 07.54 — Semantic structure

If the comparison is truly tabular:

use a real `<table>`.

That gives HTML semantics for free.

Example:

```text
<table>
<caption>Comparaison de Minimes et Portet pour le MMA</caption>
<thead>...</thead>
<tbody>...</tbody>
</table>
```

Visual layout can become highly editorial while retaining the underlying table.


## 07.55 — Mobile transformation

Do not force horizontal 800px tables.

Options:

1. retain accessible horizontal table with explicit scroll affordance; 2. transform visual presentation into criterion-by-criterion comparison while preserving

semantic structure; 3. provide a stacked summary before the detailed table.

Preferred:

**decision summary first → detailed table second.**


## 07.56 — Absence

Explicit:

Non proposé

or verified equivalent.

Do not use an unexplained `—` as the only semantic answer.

Visual dash can accompany text.


### C12 — SCHEDULE EXPLORER


## 07.57 — Responsibility

Answer:

When can someone with my need actually train?

The PDF explicitly requires planning navigation toward Boxing Center Minimes, but our system makes that surface useful before the outbound handoff.


## 07.58 — Source rule

Schedule data belongs to physical clubs.

Colomiers **consumes** those facts.

It does not become a third schedule authority.


## 07.59 — Filters

Potential:

DISCIPLINE DAY TIME WINDOW CLUB LEVEL

Only expose filters supported by actual data.


## 07.60 — Default state

Never an empty interface asking user to configure everything.

Show useful upcoming/general options first.

Filtering refines them.


## 07.61 — Native inputs

Use native controls wherever possible.

Desktop visual treatment can feel custom without replacing semantics.


### C13 — SCHEDULE ENTRY


## 07.62 — Anatomy

18H30 MMA BOXING CENTER PORTET TOUS NIVEAUX [VOIR LE PLANNING OFFICIEL ↗]

Only fields actually confirmed in source data.


## 07.63 — Data

```text
interface ScheduleEntry {
id: string;
clubId: ClubId;
disciplineId: DisciplineId;
day: Weekday;
start: string;
end?: string;
level?: string;
audience?: string;
source: string;
verifiedAt: string;
}
```


## 07.64 — Time semantics

Use `<time>` where appropriate.

Example:

```text
<time datetime="18:30">18h30</time>
```


## 07.65 — Empty state

If selected combination yields none:

PAS DE CRÉNEAU VÉRIFIÉ POUR CE CHOIX. Le prochain créneau compatible : ...

Never fabricate availability.


### C14 — PRICING EXPLORER


## 07.66 — Responsibility

Explain the real financial options relevant to someone coming from Colomiers.

The PDF explicitly requests a Tarifs route linked toward Minimes.

But the Colomiers page cannot merely be an outbound button.


## 07.67 — Data authority

All prices:

from `offers/`.

Never hard-coded in prose and a component separately.


## 07.68 — Pricing interface

Possible dimensions:

- audience;
- subscription duration;

- relevant physical club;
- what is included.

No artificial:

Basic / Pro / Elite

unless those are actual product names.


### C15 — PRICE FACT


## 07.69 — Data

```text
interface Offer {
id: string;
name: string;
price: Claim<number>;
currency: 'EUR';
billingPeriod?: string;
duration?: string;
eligibility?: string;
included?: string[];
destinationUrl?: string;
}
```


## 07.70 — Display

259 € 12 MOIS Cours / accès / conditions…

Conditions always close to price.

No orphaned price separated from eligibility terms by five sections.


## 07.71 — Structured data

Any pricing structured data later implemented must match **visible pricing**.

If public page says 259 and structured data says 400:

release fails.


### C16 — FIRST SESSION SEQUENCE


## 07.72 — Responsibility

Remove emotional uncertainty.

It is a narrative component, not a technical “stepper” unless the user is actually progressing through a task.


## 07.73 — Anatomy

01 ARRIVER explanation 02 ÊTRE ORIENTÉ explanation 03 S'ÉCHAUFFER explanation 04 APPRENDRE explanation 05 DÉCIDER explanation

Actual wording derives from verified process.


## 07.74 — Context variant

```text
interface FirstSessionProps {
clubId?: ClubId;
disciplineId?: DisciplineId;
}
```

If context exists:

specific verified facts.

If not:

only network-safe general facts.

Do not invent a generalized process from one club's local process.


### C17 — EVIDENCE LEDGER


## 07.75 — Responsibility

Replace vague marketing claims with factual trust.


## 07.76 — Evidence types

Potential:

QUALIFICATION AFFILIATION HISTORY FACILITY PROGRAMME REVIEW NETWORK FACT

Only verified.


## 07.77 — Anatomy

FACT supporting explanation source/freshness where needed

No icon-plus-buzzword benefits.


## 07.78 — Numbers

Semantic HTML contains final value from first response.

Never:

<span>0</span>

then JS counts to 259.

This is permanently banned from our own-work audit.

If numeric reveal desired:

animate a duplicate/decorative layer.

Accessible/static fact stays final.


### C18 — VERIFIED FACT


## 07.79 — Tiny but important component

For volatile claims:

6 JOURS / 7 VÉRIFIÉ · 07.09.2026

But only after the current conflict around opening-hour data is resolved.

The client brief currently states 6 days/week from 10:00 to 21:15.

Until the external data conflict identified earlier is resolved, that exact closing time remains **conflicted**, not silently publishable as canonical.


### C19 — PROVENANCE IMAGE


## 07.80 — Responsibility

Make imagery truthful.

This is especially important because the brief supplies Minimes photography for a Colomiers-targeted site.


## 07.81 — Data

```text
interface MediaAsset {
id: string;
src: string;
alt: string;
width: number;
height: number;
locationId?: ClubId;
subject?: string;
role:
| 'evidence'
| 'orientation'
| 'emotion'
| 'instruction'
| 'decorative';
focalPoint?: {
x: number;
y: number;
};
provenance?: string;
}
```


## 07.82 — Rendering rule

If `locationId` exists and geographic interpretation matters:

render location caption.

Example:

BOXING CENTER MINIMES · TOULOUSE


## 07.83 — Alt

Alt is semantic description.

Not SEO stuffing.

The PDF requires descriptive ALT and web optimization; those requirements remain, but filename/alt optimization does not justify inaccurate “Colomiers” descriptions on photos taken elsewhere.


## 07.84 — Performance

Component owns:

- <picture>;
- AVIF/WebP;
- sizes;
- srcset;
- width/height;
- lazy/eager strategy;
- focal crop.

Pages should not rebuild image optimization manually.


### C20 — FRESHNESS LABEL


## 07.85 — Responsibility

Expose the recency of volatile information without overwhelming the page.

Use for:

- planning;
- opening hours;
- prices if volatile;
- perhaps reviews.

Not:

static discipline descriptions.


## 07.86 — Display

VÉRIFIÉ · 07.09.2026

or:

MISE À JOUR · 07.09.2026

depending semantic meaning.

Do not say “live” unless data is genuinely live.


### C21 — CONTACT ORIENTATION FORM


## 07.87 — Formal requirement

The brief asks for:

- first name;

- surname;
- phone;
- email;
- desired activity;
- message;
- desired club or geographical zone;

with submissions directed to `bc.combat31@gmail.com`.

We preserve the requested information territory while reducing unnecessary mandatory data.


## 07.88 — Product philosophy

The form should first help the visitor express **need**.

Then collect contact details.

Sequence:

1. WHAT ARE YOU LOOKING FOR? 2. DO YOU ALREADY KNOW THE CLUB? 3. HOW CAN WE CONTACT YOU? 4. ANYTHING ELSE?


## 07.89 — Accessibility

WAI's current form guidance recommends keeping forms simple, asking only for information needed to complete the process, explicitly labeling controls, grouping related controls, and providing clear instructions.

Therefore:

native `<label>` for text inputs.

```text
<fieldset> + <legend> for grouped choices.
```

Instructions before/near relevant controls.

No placeholder-only labels.


## 07.90 — Proposed minimum required fields

Product recommendation:

PRÉNOM ACTIVITÉ / BESOIN ONE CONTACT METHOD

Other requested fields may remain optional where business/legal requirements permit.

If operations insist on all original fields being required, implementation follows that requirement—but the blueprint flags the increased friction.


## 07.91 — Contact method

We can either require:

- email; or
- telephone.

If we support “at least one of two”, native `required` alone is insufficient; server/client validation handles the relationship.

Error:

Indique au moins un numéro ou une adresse e-mail pour qu'on puisse te répondre.


## 07.92 — Autocomplete

Use correct browser autocomplete tokens:

given-name family-name tel email

Do not disable autocomplete for ordinary contact data.


## 07.93 — Validation

Client validation improves immediate usability.

Server validation remains authoritative.

WAI explicitly notes that client-side validation alone is insufficient and data should also be validated server-side.


## 07.94 — Error summary

After failed submission:

show concise summary at top.

Move focus appropriately to summary.

Each field also receives local error.

Example:

IL RESTE 2 CHOSES À CORRIGER • Ton e-mail semble incomplet. • Choisis l'activité recherchée.

Then field-level messages.


## 07.95 — Error associations

Use:

- aria-invalid="true";

- aria-describedby linking the field to error/help text.

Visible label remains.


## 07.96 — Success

No dead-end:

ON A CE QU'IL FAUT. Ta demande concerne : MMA · Recommandation de club L'équipe peut maintenant te répondre. [VOIR LE MMA] [COMPARER LES CLUBS]


### C22 — BREADCRUMBS


## 07.97 — Responsibility

Reinforce page context without clutter.

Example:

ACCUEIL → MMA

WAI's current breadcrumb pattern recommends a labeled navigation landmark and `aria-current="page"` for the current linked page.


## 07.98 — Structure

```text
<nav aria-label="Fil d'Ariane">
<ol>
<li><a href="/">Accueil</a></li>
<li aria-current="page">MMA</li>
</ol>
</nav>
```

Decorative separators come from CSS and do not need screen-reader announcement, aligning with the WAI example.


### C23 — DISCLOSURE


## 07.99 — Responsibility

Hide **secondary detail**, never primary answers.

Approved use:

- extensive FAQ;
- detailed equipment guidance;
- secondary terms;
- explanation methodology.

Rejected:

- hiding every SEO paragraph;
- entire prices;
- core club availability;
- “does Boxing Center exist in Colomiers?” truth statement.


## 07.100 — Native preference

Use `<details><summary>` for simple disclosure where visual/behavioral needs permit.

This provides strong built-in semantics with little JS.

For custom disclosure:

button + `aria-expanded`.

WAI's disclosure pattern defines this contract directly.


## 07.101 — Accordion

Only use a true accordion when several related disclosures form one coherent set.

WAI's accordion pattern requires headings containing buttons whose expanded state is programmatically exposed.

Avoid region-landmark proliferation.


### C24 — ACTION LINK / BUTTON


## 07.102 — Most important rule


### LINKS NAVIGATE. BUTTONS ACT.

Never:

```text
<div onclick="location.href='/mma'">
```

Never style an `<a>` as a button and then give it fake button behavior if it simply navigates.


## 07.103 — Types

```text
type ActionTone =
| 'primary'
| 'secondary'
| 'quiet'
| 'external'
| 'destructive';
```

Destructive probably rare on public site.


## 07.104 — Primary

One dominant primary per local decision group.

Not six red buttons in one viewport.


## 07.105 — External

External venue handoff uses clear destination naming:

DÉCOUVRIR BOXING CENTER PORTET ↗

Not:

EN SAVOIR PLUS.


### C25 — STATE PANEL


## 07.106 — Unified state grammar

States:

EMPTY ERROR SUCCESS PENDING UNAVAILABLE CONFLICTED

Each uses the route visual language.

No random illustrations.


## 07.107 — Empty

PAS DE CRÉNEAU POUR CE CHOIX. Voici l'option vérifiée la plus proche : ...


## 07.108 — Error

ON N'A PAS PU CHARGER LES DONNÉES ACTUELLES. [VOIR LE PLANNING OFFICIEL ↗]

Never present stale data as current merely to avoid an error state.


## 07.109 — Conflicted

Internal/public behavior differs.

Internal build QA:

hard failure for critical fact.

Public production fallback:

omit disputed claim and present safe alternative.

We should generally avoid exposing:

“Our internal data is conflicted”

to ordinary users.

Resolve/omit before release.


### C26 — SITE FOOTER


## 07.110 — Responsibility

Close:

- navigation;
- entity truth;

- club relationships;
- legal information.


## 07.111 — Required contextual statement

Visible:

Boxingcenter-colomiers.fr aide les habitants de Colomiers à s'orienter vers les clubs Boxing Center pertinents. Il n'existe pas de salle physique Boxing Center à Colomiers.

This is intentionally not hidden in legal text.


## 07.112 — Physical club section

Explicitly labeled:


### CLUBS PHYSIQUES

Then:

Minimes ↗ Portet-sur-Garonne ↗

This is both excellent UX and entity disambiguation.


### C27 — NOT FOUND ROUTE


## 07.113 — HTTP truth

Real 404 response.

Not a 200 page saying “not found.”


## 07.114 — Component anatomy

404 MAUVAISE DIRECTION. PAS MAUVAIS DÉPART. Cette page n'existe pas. [TROUVER MON CLUB] [VOIR LES DISCIPLINES] [ACCUEIL]

Route graphic:

dead-end → valid branches.


## 07.115 — No search box by default

A tiny ten-page site doesn't need fake site search on its 404.

Direct high-value routes are better.


### C28 — CONTENT SECTION


## 07.116 — Purpose

Prevent every page section from being randomly structured while still allowing strong art direction.


## 07.117 — Data/props

Conceptually:

```text
interface ContentSectionProps {
```

id?: string; eyebrow?: string; title: string; lead?: string; tone?: 'ink' | 'paper' | 'bone' | 'navy'; width?: 'reading' | 'standard' | 'wide'; intensity?: 'quiet' | 'editorial' | 'immersive'; }

But it must **not** generate a universal:

eyebrow → title → paragraph → cards

layout automatically.

The primitive establishes:

- section semantics;
- spacing;
- tonal surface;
- heading relationship.

Composition remains intentional.


## 07.118 — HEADING OWNERSHIP

Every section specifies its heading level according to page hierarchy.

Do not let components hardcode every heading as `<h2>`.

Possible API:

<SectionHeading as="h2" ... />

But the page composition owns the semantic level.


## 07.119 — FAQ / ANSWER COMPONENT

We should distinguish:


### AnswerBlock

Visible direct answer.


### Disclosure

secondary detail.

This is important for GEO.

Critical questions should often have visible direct answers, not be buried in collapsed accordions.


## 07.120 — ANSWER BLOCK

Potential:

Y A-T-IL UN BOXING CENTER À COLOMIERS ? Non. Boxing Center ne possède actuellement pas de salle physique à Colomiers...

HTML:

heading + paragraph.

Extremely crawlable.

Extremely extractable.

No special AI component required.


## 07.121 — TABS POLICY

Tabs are **not** a default architecture component.

Why?

They hide alternative content and often encourage designers to cram too much into one section.

Use only when:

- content is genuinely parallel;
- the same context needs one panel at a time;
- first panel is not artificially privileged for SEO;
- all content remains available in HTML/accessibility.

If used, follow the full WAI tabs behavior rather than styling three buttons above hidden `<div>`s.

For Minimes vs Portet comparison, a comparison matrix is usually better than tabs.


## 07.122 — CAROUSEL POLICY

No generic Carousel component in the core system.

If later an asset set genuinely requires a carousel:

it needs its own accessibility specification.

The absence of a default carousel prevents the implementation model from putting everything into sliders.


## 07.123 — MODAL POLICY

Only `MobileNavigationDialog` is pre-approved.

Other modal use requires a reason.

No:

- promo modal;
- exit-intent modal;
- “subscribe” popup;
- first-visit offer popup.

The formal brief explicitly excludes promotional popups and aggressive marketing mechanics.


## 07.124 — TOOLTIP POLICY

No essential information in tooltips.

Tooltips may clarify uncommon icon controls.

But our icon system already minimizes icon-only controls.

Therefore tooltips should be rare.


## 07.125 — BADGE POLICY

Approved:

VÉRIFIÉ DÉBUTANT ACCEPTÉ AUJOURD'HUI

where factual/interaction status matters.

Not:

PREMIUM TOP POPULAR HOT BEST

without real meaning.


## 07.126 — COMPONENT RESPONSIVE CONTRACT

Every non-trivial component documents a **transformation**, not merely breakpoints.

Example:


### ClubComparison

Desktop: side-by-side evidence matrix.

Mobile: decision summary → criterion sequence → full details.

That is a transform.


## 07.127 — BREAKPOINT POLICY

Components do not invent random breakpoints.

Use system-level layout breakpoints informed by actual content failure.

Conceptually:

mobile tablet desktop wide

But CSS container queries may be appropriate for components whose transformation depends more on available component width than viewport width.


## 07.128 — CONTAINER QUERIES

Good candidate:

ClubComparison

EvidenceLedger

ClubDestination

because they may appear in different page widths.

Use where browser support/implementation remains robust.

This reduces page-specific breakpoint hacks.


## 07.129 — COMPONENT SEO CONTRACT

Every factual/indexable component must answer:


### Is its important text server-rendered?

Yes.


### Are links real anchors?

Yes.


### Does it duplicate a canonical fact?

Generated from same source.


### Does hidden interaction conceal essential search content?

No.


### Are IDs/headings meaningful?

Yes.


### Could the component generate multiple competing URLs?

No unless route architecture explicitly permits it.


## 07.130 — COMPONENT GEO CONTRACT

Every component displaying a business fact needs one of:

1. source through centralized entity data; 2. source through centralized claim registry.

Never:

free-form component prop invented by page author.

Example forbidden:

<ClubCard description="Le meilleur club MMA près de Colomiers" />

That phrase includes a superiority claim with no authority.


## 07.131 — COMPONENT ACCESSIBILITY CONTRACT

Default to native HTML before ARIA.

W3C's own APG examples repeatedly warn that robust accessibility is best served by semantic HTML and that poorly applied ARIA can be worse than using native semantics.

Therefore hierarchy:

NATIVE HTML ↓ native HTML + small ARIA relationship ↓ custom ARIA widget only when genuinely necessary

Never start from:

`role=""`.


## 07.132 — CUSTOM INTERACTION GATE

Before creating a custom widget ask:

Does HTML already provide this?

Examples:

choice → radio.

multiple choice → checkboxes.

expand → details/disclosure.

navigation → anchor.

action → button.

comparison → table.

form group → fieldset.

modal → dialog.

The answer is often already available.

That is good engineering.


## 07.133 — COMPONENT PERFORMANCE CONTRACT

A component does not get client JS simply because it has states.

CSS states often suffice.

Static components:

zero hydration.

Interactive systems:

localized hydration.


## 07.134 — CLIENT COMPONENT BUDGET

Likely hydrated components:

IntentSelector ProximityEngine ScheduleExplorer Contact form enhancement Mobile navigation

Everything else should strongly prefer:

Astro + HTML + CSS.


## 07.135 — COMPONENT CSS RULE

Each component uses:

- global semantic tokens;
- local component custom properties only where necessary.

No component invents its own palette.

No hard-coded:

```text
color: #e82331;
```

if `--color-signal` exists.


## 07.136 — STATE NAMING

Shared state vocabulary:

data-state="idle" data-state="focused" data-state="selected" data-state="resolved" data-state="pending" data-state="error" data-state="unavailable"

Avoid component-specific synonyms:

`active-ish`, `picked`, `done-red`.

This gives styling/debugging consistency.


## 07.137 — DATA ATTRIBUTES

Use semantic data attributes for presentation states where appropriate.

Example:

```text
<div
data-state="resolved"
data-club="portet"
>
```

But business logic does not live in CSS selectors.


## 07.138 — COMPONENT IDENTITY

Interactive controls require deterministic IDs.

Useful for:

- labels;
- aria relationships;
- hydration consistency;
- testing.

Avoid random client-generated IDs that differ between server/client where possible.


## 07.139 — TEST SELECTORS

Testing should prefer semantic queries:

- role;
- label;
- text;
- form name.

Not fragile CSS classes.

Where needed:

data-testid

may exist, but should not become implementation structure.


## 07.140 — ANALYTICS CONTRACT

Components emit semantic events.

Examples:

intent_selected recommendation_generated club_destination_clicked schedule_filter_applied price_destination_clicked

contact_form_started contact_form_submitted

Not:

red_button_clicked card_3_hover

Analytics follows business meaning.


## 07.141 — PRIVACY CONTRACT

No component silently injects:

- tracking pixels;
- third-party embeds;
- map scripts;
- video trackers

without site-level privacy approval.

Embeds get deliberate consent/loading strategy.

Noble Art's privacy-aware map loading remains a useful internal reference mechanism.


## 07.142 — THIRD-PARTY COMPONENT POLICY

Do not pull in a full component framework simply for:

- accordion;
- dialog;
- radio;
- tabs.

Native primitives + carefully implemented components are preferred.

If an accessibility-focused library later materially lowers risk, evaluate its:

- JS weight;
- styling constraints;
- SSR compatibility;
- Astro compatibility;
- accessibility quality;
- maintenance health.

No library by aesthetic default.


## 07.143 — COMPONENT DOCUMENTATION FORMAT

Every component implementation file should be documented with:

PURPOSE DATA SOURCE SEMANTIC ROOT STATES INTERACTIONS MOBILE TRANSFORMATION ACCESSIBILITY NOTES MOTION REDUCED MOTION SEO/GEO NOTES PERFORMANCE NOTES ANALYTICS EVENTS FORBIDDEN USAGE

Not necessarily in every source file as huge comments.

Could live centrally in:

`COMPONENT-SYSTEM.md`.

But the contract exists.


## 07.144 — STORYBOOK?

Not mandatory.

Astro component previews or a lightweight internal `/dev/components` route may be more appropriate.

If Storybook adds disproportionate dependency/configuration weight, skip it.

The important thing is **isolated component review**, not the brand name of the tool.


## 07.145 — DEV COMPONENT GALLERY

Recommended development-only route:

```text
/__dev/components
```

never deployed/indexed publicly.

Displays:

- every component;
- all states;
- mobile widths;
- error;
- empty;
- long copy;
- short copy;
- missing image;
- reduced motion;
- dark/light surface.

This is extremely valuable for system QA.


## 07.146 — STRESS CONTENT

Every component must be tested with:


### Short

MMA


### Long French

BOXING CENTER PORTET-SUR-GARONNE


### Long CTA

CONSULTER LE PLANNING OFFICIEL DE MINIMES


### Missing optional data

No image.


### Multiple evidence rows


### 200% text.

If the design only survives perfect demo data:

fail.


## 07.147 — COMPONENT “NO PLACEHOLDER LIES” RULE

If content is unavailable:

do not insert convincing placeholder facts.

Dev may use visibly marked fixtures:

[DONNÉE DE TEST]

Production build must reject unresolved critical fixtures.


## 07.148 — COMPONENT BUILD VALIDATION

We should eventually add automated/static checks for things like:

ClubDestination requires canonical URL physical club requires real address if address shown price requires currency ProvenanceImage requires alt unless decorative FreshnessLabel requires date external club link cannot point to Vercel/staging RecommendationResult requires ≥1 reason

Turn design rules into code rules wherever practical.


## 07.149 — THE COMPONENT +1

The system-wide innovation is:


### COMPONENTS KNOW WHY THEY ARE TRUE.

Traditional design-system component:

ClubCard title image description button

Our component:

ClubDestination entity verified claims

provenance context recommendation relationship freshness canonical destination

That is a significant conceptual upgrade.

The component is not simply visually reusable.

It is **epistemically reusable**.


## 07.150 — WHY THIS MATTERS FOR GEO

Consider an AI/crawler encountering:

Portet offers MMA.

That sentence should exist because:

PORTET ENTITY ↓ MMA CLAIM ↓ VERIFIED SOURCE ↓ CLUB COMPONENT ↓ VISIBLE HTML ↓ JSON-LD / MACHINE DOCS

Not because a page designer typed it.

That is how factual coherence scales.


## 07.151 — COMPONENT HATER TEST

For every new component:


### Why isn't native HTML enough?

If no answer → simplify.


### Does this component own a recurring job?

If no → page composition.


### Does it duplicate factual data?

If yes → refactor.


### Does mobile require a different information order?

If yes → document it.


### Can keyboard users operate it?

If no → fail.


### Does it work without animation?

If no → fail.


### Does it require JS?

If no → don't hydrate.


### Could it accidentally manufacture a Colomiers location?

If yes → fail.


### Can an AI implementation model misuse it easily?

If yes → make API stricter.


## 07.152 — FORBIDDEN COMPONENTS AT LAUNCH

Unless a later part explicitly approves them:

GenericCard GlowCard BentoGrid StatsCounter TestimonialCarousel PromoModal Countdown FloatingChatBubble CustomCursor InfiniteMarquee LogoCloud ParticleBackground GenericTabs GenericSlider VideoBackground MegaMenu

Some of these patterns may eventually have legitimate uses.

They do not enter the system without a named job.


## 07.153 — COMPONENT API PRINCIPLE

Make the correct usage easy.

Make the wrong usage difficult.

Bad:

<ClubCard title="Whatever" description="Whatever" url="whatever" />

Allows hallucination.

Better:

<ClubDestination clubId="portet" context="mma" />

Component resolves authorized data itself.

That is implementation-proof architecture.


## 07.154 — SAME PRINCIPLE FOR PRICES

Bad:

<Price amount="259" />

Better:

<PriceFact offerId="season-259" />

The number comes from the offer registry.


## 07.155 — SAME PRINCIPLE FOR IMAGES

Bad:

<img src="/gym.jpg" alt="Club de boxe Colomiers">

Better:

<ProvenanceImage assetId="minimes-bags-01" />

The component already knows:

- source;

- true location;
- alt;
- dimensions;
- crop;
- role.


## 07.156 — SAME PRINCIPLE FOR EXTERNAL CLUB LINKS

Bad:

```text
<a href="https://bc-portet-old.vercel.app">
```

Better:

<ClubExternalLink clubId="portet"> Découvrir Portet </ClubExternalLink>

Canonical URL comes from central registry.

This directly prevents the stale sister-domain problem discovered in our previous work.


## 07.157 — COMPONENT VARIANT LIMIT

Each component should have very few variants.

If:

```text
variant="one"
variant="two"
variant="three"
variant="dark"
variant="light"
variant="hero"
variant="mobile"
variant="compact"
variant="premium"
variant="new"
```

we have failed to create a system.

Prefer semantic props:

```text
context="mma"
tone="inverse"
density="compact"
```

Only where they genuinely affect the job.


## 07.158 — PAGE COMPOSITION OWNS DRAMA

Components stay coherent.

Pages create unique compositions.

This is important because otherwise a very reusable component system produces a generic-looking website.

Baffled Bar lives in the **composition of strong primitives**, not in making every primitive bizarre.


## 07.159 — COMPONENT QUALITY GATE

A component is considered locked only when all of these exist:

✓ normal state ✓ hover/focus ✓ keyboard path ✓ mobile state ✓ reduced-motion state

✓ empty state where relevant ✓ error state where relevant ✓ long-copy state ✓ missing-data behavior ✓ semantic HTML ✓ data authority ✓ analytics contract ✓ performance behavior ✓ screenshot review

No component is “done” because its default screenshot looks good.


## 07.160 — PART 07 LOCKED ARCHITECTURE

We now have three distinct classes of implementation:


### STATIC SEMANTIC COMPONENTS

ClubDestination ClubComparison PriceFact EvidenceLedger ProvenanceImage FreshnessLabel Breadcrumbs Disclosure Footer ContentSection FirstSessionSequence

Prefer:

**Astro + HTML + CSS.**


### LIGHT INTERACTION COMPONENTS

MobileNavigation ContactForm enhancement Disclosure

Use:

**minimal JS / native browser APIs.**


### PRODUCT SYSTEMS

IntentSelector ProximityEngine ScheduleExplorer

These legitimately own client-side state.

Even here:

semantic server-rendered foundations remain.


## 07.161 — COMPONENT SYSTEM IN ONE SENTENCE

**Every Boxing Center Colomiers component is simultaneously a visual object, an interaction contract, an accessibility pattern, and a controlled projection of verified network truth.**

That is the component architecture.


## 07.162 — LOCKED DOCTRINES


### NATIVE BEFORE CUSTOM

HTML semantics first.


### DATA BEFORE COPY

Business facts come from structured authority.


### COMPONENTS KNOW WHY THEY ARE TRUE

Claims retain provenance.


### LINKS NAVIGATE. BUTTONS ACT.

No semantic ambiguity.


### ONE COMPONENT, ONE INFORMATION JOB

No giant god-components.


### PAGE COMPOSITION OWNS DRAMA

Reusable primitives stay disciplined.


### NO PLACEHOLDER LIES

Missing truth remains missing.


### MOBILE TRANSFORMS

It never merely shrinks.


### STATIC FIRST

Hydration must be earned.


### THE API PREVENTS DRIFT

Component APIs should make hallucination and hard-coded factual divergence difficult.


<a id="part-08-individual-page-architectures"></a>

# PART 08 — Individual Page Architectures


## 08.0 — PURPOSE

The homepage owns the broad question:

**“Where can I practise boxing or combat sports when I live in Colomiers?”**

The remaining canonical routes must each solve **one narrower problem substantially better** **than the homepage can**.

The client brief explicitly requires dedicated coverage for:

- boxe anglaise;

- MMA;
- Boxing Fitness & Femme;
- boxe enfants;
- planning;
- pricing;
- contact.

We additionally introduced:

- /premiere-seance/;

- /quel-club/;

because they solve genuinely distinct user problems rather than merely targeting keyword variants.

The final canonical architecture remains:

```text
/
├── boxe-anglaise/
├── mma/
├── boxing-fitness/
├── boxe-enfants/
├── premiere-seance/
├── quel-club/
├── plannings/
├── tarifs/
└── contact/
```

Part 08 specifies every non-homepage route deeply enough that implementation should not need to invent its own page logic.


## 08.1 — UNIVERSAL PAGE CONTRACT

Every indexable content page must contain, in this order conceptually:

1. ORIENTATION What page am I on? 2. DIRECT ANSWER Answer the searcher's main question quickly.

3. EXPLANATION What does this actually involve? 4. PERSONAL FIT Is it suitable for someone like me? 5. BOXING CENTER REALITY What does the network actually offer? 6. DESTINATION Where does the physical activity happen? 7. PRACTICALITY When / how / equipment / price where relevant. 8. TRUST Why should I believe these facts? 9. NEXT ACTION What should I do now?

Not every page requires nine visually distinct sections.

Several may combine.

But the informational progression remains.


## 08.2 — UNIVERSAL ENTRY CONTRACT

A visitor may arrive from:

- Google;
- Bing;
- ChatGPT;
- Gemini;
- social media;
- an internal link;
- a shared URL.

Therefore **no route may depend on homepage context**.

Within the first meaningful section, every route should establish:

1. the practice/topic; 2. the Colomiers context; 3. the fact that the physical training location is elsewhere; 4. what the page will help the visitor decide.


## 08.3 — UNIVERSAL “COLOMIERS TRUTH” MODULE

Not necessarily the same box visually every time.

But every major discipline page must contain an explicit factual sentence equivalent to:

**Boxing Center ne possède pas de salle physique à Colomiers. Ce site aide les pratiquants venant de Colomiers à identifier le club du réseau adapté à leur discipline.**

This protects:

- user trust;
- local SEO honesty;
- AI extraction;
- entity disambiguation.

The brief makes this factual distinction mandatory.


## 08.4 — UNIVERSAL PAGE METADATA OBJECT

Every route must eventually resolve:

```text
route:
primaryIntent:
secondaryIntents:
title:
```

metaDescription: h1: canonical: robots: breadcrumbs: primaryEntity: relatedEntities: schemaTypes: og: internalLinks: externalDestinations: claimDependencies: imageRequirements: geoAnswers: conversionGoal:

There should be **no route-level metadata improvisation inside** `.astro` **templates**.


### PAGE P01 — /boxe-anglaise/


## 08.5 — SEARCH JOB

Owns the cluster:

boxe anglaise Colomiers cours de boxe Colomiers cours de boxe anglaise Colomiers club boxe anglaise près Colomiers boxe débutant Colomiers — secondary

Broad `boxe Colomiers` remains owned by `/`.


## 08.6 — USER'S ACTUAL QUESTION

Not:

What is boxing?

But:

**“If I live in Colomiers and want to learn real English boxing, where can Boxing Center take me and what will it actually be like?”**

That is the page.


## 08.7 — CURRENT DESTINATION EVIDENCE

As of the current live destination check, Minimes presents English boxing as its core identity, with several rings, beginner courses, competition boxing, equipment lending and coach-guided first sessions. It also publishes a dedicated beginner course pathway.

Portet also currently lists English boxing among its disciplines and publishes a dedicated ring/boxing space.

Therefore the page should **not automatically send all English-boxing users to Minimes**.

Instead:

explain why Minimes may be particularly relevant for certain English-boxing profiles while still acknowledging verified Portet availability.

The claim registry determines the final comparison.


## 08.8 — TITLE

Recommended:

**Boxe anglaise près de Colomiers | Cours Boxing Center**


## 08.9 — META DESCRIPTION

Recommended territory:

Tu habites Colomiers et veux apprendre la boxe anglaise ? Découvre les cours Boxing Center, l’accueil des débutants et le club adapté entre Minimes et Portet.

Final character length gets tuned later.


## 08.10 — H1


### FAIRE DE LA BOXE ANGLAISE QUAND ON HABITE À COLOMIERS

Alternative more conversational visual title may coexist, but semantic H1 remains clear.


## 08.11 — FIRST ANSWER

Immediately beneath H1:

Boxing Center ne possède pas de salle physique à Colomiers, mais les pratiquants venant de Colomiers peuvent rejoindre les clubs du réseau proposant la boxe anglaise. Cette page t'aide à comprendre la discipline, débuter sereinement et choisir le club réellement adapté à ton niveau et tes disponibilités.


## 08.12 — PAGE SEQUENCE

B01 HERO / ANSWER B02 WHAT ENGLISH BOXING REALLY IS B03 IS IT FOR ME? B04 WHAT A SESSION LOOKS LIKE

B05 MINIMES / PORTET FOR BOXING B06 BEGINNER PATH B07 SCHEDULE PREVIEW B08 EQUIPMENT B09 PROOF / COACHING B10 NEXT ACTION


## 08.13 — B01 HERO

Visual:

real boxing imagery.

Image must identify its actual physical location if location-specific.

Core action:

**TROUVER OÙ BOXER**

Secondary:

**JE N'AI JAMAIS BOXÉ**


## 08.14 — B02 — WHAT IS ENGLISH BOXING?

Do not write a generic Wikipedia definition.

Explain through actual beginner questions:

- hands only;
- guard;
- movement;
- distance;
- defence;
- combinations;
- bag/pads/technical work;
- controlled opposition where appropriate.

The page should distinguish:

**learning boxing**

from:

**being punched.**

That distinction matters for beginners.


## 08.15 — B03 — WHO IS IT FOR?

Profiles:

- absolute beginner;
- fitness-driven;
- technical learner;
- experienced boxer;
- competitor.

The section answers:

“Do I belong?”

No fake promise:

for absolutely everyone regardless of everything.

Instead clarify pathways.


## 08.16 — B04 — SESSION ANATOMY

Potential visual sequence:

ÉCHAUFFEMENT ↓ GARDE / DÉPLACEMENTS ↓ TECHNIQUE

↓ SACS / PATTES D'OURS ↓ APPLICATION ↓ RETOUR AU CALME

Only exact steps supported by source data get presented as universal facts.

Location-specific variations stay labeled.


## 08.17 — B05 — WHERE?

Headline:


### MINIMES OU PORTET POUR LA BOXE ANGLAISE ?

Use `ClubComparison`.

Criteria may include, when verified:

- English-boxing programme;
- facilities;
- beginner pathway;
- competition pathway;
- schedule;
- relevant equipment.

No subjective winner.


## 08.18 — B06 — BEGINNING

CTA into:

```text
/premiere-seance/?discipline=boxe
```

Main copy should answer:

- what to wear;
- whether gloves are needed;
- whether contact is compulsory;
- how coaches introduce first-time practitioners.

Current Minimes copy explicitly states borrowed equipment and no imposed contact for beginners, but the Colomiers route should only generalize facts validated across the destination context.


## 08.19 — B07 — SCHEDULE

Embed a filtered preview from `ScheduleExplorer`.

Not an image of a timetable.

CTA:

**VOIR TOUS LES CRÉNEAUX DE BOXE ANGLAISE**


## 08.20 — B08 — EQUIPMENT

Short decision-focused section:


### First time

what is genuinely required.


### Later

what someone typically buys.

Do not turn into ecommerce unless relevant.


## 08.21 — B09 — EVIDENCE

Real:

- coach qualification;
- facility;
- equipment;
- real photo.

No:

expertise / passion / excellence.


## 08.22 — B10 — CONVERSION

If recommendation state exists:

**TA ROUTE BOXE ANGLAISE → [CLUB]**

Otherwise:

**COMPARER MINIMES ET PORTET**

Secondary:

**PRÉPARER MA PREMIÈRE SÉANCE**


## 08.23 — GEO ANSWERS

Page should directly answer:


### Y a-t-il un Boxing Center de boxe anglaise à Colomiers?

No physical club.


### Où pratiquer la boxe anglaise avec Boxing Center depuis Colomiers?

Answer using verified destinations.


### Peut-on débuter?

Answer with evidence.


### Faut-il déjà être en forme?

Clear response.


### Faut-il faire du combat?

Clear factual distinction.


## 08.24 — FORBIDDEN

No:

- “meilleur club de boxe de Colomiers”;
- fake Colomiers facility imagery;
- generic history of boxing taking 800 words;
- separate SEO block titled “Boxe anglaise Colomiers”;
- forced Minimes recommendation without criteria.


### PAGE P02 — /mma/


## 08.25 — SEARCH JOB

Owns:

MMA Colomiers club MMA Colomiers MMA près de Colomiers club MMA près de Colomiers cours MMA Colomiers

Potential supporting concepts:

grappling JJB lutte striking

only where contextually relevant.


## 08.26 — COMPETITIVE REALITY

This is the route where geographic honesty matters most.

There are genuine MMA offers physically located in Colomiers. Current search results include Maison du Fight's MMA/grappling/pieds-poings programme, while current association listings also show MMA registration in Colomiers.

Therefore:


### WE DO NOT TRY TO PRETEND BOXING CENTER IS THE LOCAL PHYSICAL MMA CLUB.

Our page must instead answer:

**“Why would someone living in Colomiers choose the Boxing Center MMA ecosystem despite travelling outside Colomiers?”**

That is a real decision problem.


## 08.27 — CURRENT NETWORK EVIDENCE

Portet currently publishes:

- MMA;
- grappling/JJB;
- dedicated MMA cage;

- English boxing;
- kick-boxing;
- beginner accessibility.

This makes Portet a strong current candidate for MMA-oriented Colomiers visitors.

But final publication still depends on registry truth.


## 08.28 — TITLE

**MMA près de Colomiers | Où s'entraîner avec Boxing Center**


## 08.29 — H1

fitness programme availability women-specific programmes contact/no-contact format schedule


### Children

Requires:

age bands programme type contact rules schedule prices coaching qualification


### First Session

Requires:

arrival process equipment contact expectations trial procedure club differences


### Quel Club

Requires:

**all matching criteria facts.**


### Planning

Requires:

**schedule source + freshness.**


### Pricing

Requires:

**offer source + validity dates.**


### Contact

Requires:

**phone + email + form routing.**

This list becomes part of build validation.


## 08.144 — CONTENT PROVENANCE

Every non-trivial factual paragraph should be traceable internally.

The writer may make prose beautiful.

The writer may **not change the underlying fact**.

Example:

Registry:

```text
Portet:
MMA = verified
cage = verified
```

Editorial output may say:

Le MMA se pratique à Portet dans un espace équipé d'une cage.

Fine.

It may not say:

Portet possède la plus grande cage de MMA de Toulouse.

No source.


## 08.145 — NO CROSS-CLUB LEAKAGE

A frequent failure mode:

an image, coach, schedule or equipment detail from one Boxing Center gets copied onto another route.

Build/data architecture should make this difficult.

Example:

`ProvenanceImage` knows its club.

`ScheduleEntry` knows its club.

`Coach` knows club assignments.

No manual “looks close enough.”


## 08.146 — SEO PAGE DIFFERENTIATION TEST

Compare any two routes.

If more than roughly **60% of their narrative content and section jobs are substantially** **equivalent**, investigate consolidation.

Not an automated Google rule.

Our internal quality heuristic.

Examples:

`/boxe-anglaise/` vs /mma/

should be materially different.

`/boxe-enfants/` vs /boxing-fitness/

obviously different.

If they're all same template with nouns switched:

Baffled Bar failure.


## 08.147 — VISUAL DIFFERENTIATION WITHOUT DESIGN DRIFT

Each page may emphasize a different facet of the same visual grammar.


### Boxe anglaise

Precision / line / distance.


### MMA

Convergence of several combat ranges.


### Fitness

Rhythm / repetition.


### Children

Progression / scale.


### Première séance

Sequence / threshold.


### Quel Club

Branch / resolution.


### Planning

Time / axis.


### Tarifs

Value / terms.


### Contact

Open route / unresolved question.

Same primitives.

Different semantics.

This is much stronger than giving every page a different visual theme.


## 08.148 — MOTION DIFFERENTIATION

Same rule.

Boxe:

precision.

MMA:

multiple paths converging.

Fitness:

controlled rhythmic repetition.

Children:

step progression.

First session:

quiet sequential reveal.

Quel Club:

strongest convergence.

Planning:

fast temporal transitions.

Pricing:

almost static.

Contact:

minimal.

Motion follows page job.


## 08.149 — ROUTE-PAGE HERO RULE

Not every page gets a giant homepage-scale hero.

Hero heights vary by informational need.


### MMA / Boxe

strong editorial hero.


### Children

parent question first.


### First Session

photographic/narrative.


### Quel Club

product UI almost immediately.


### Planning

utility immediately.


### Pricing

answer/price context immediately.


### Contact

short.

This preserves momentum.


## 08.150 — NO HERO VIDEO FACTORY

Video only if a page-specific real asset materially helps.

Not:

every route automatically needs background footage.


## 08.151 — STRUCTURED DATA DIRECTION

Detailed schema is Part 10/SEO-GEO System.

At page level:


### Discipline pages

primarily `WebPage` connected to Boxing Center organisation and relevant entities.


### First Session

`WebPage`.


### Quel Club

`WebPage` / interactive tool context.


### Planning

`WebPage`.


### Pricing

`WebPage`, with offer/product schema only where genuinely eligible and exact.


### Contact

`ContactPage`.

Do not assign `LocalBusiness` to Colomiers route pages.


## 08.152 — GEO ENTITY RELATIONSHIP

Each route needs to make this machine-readable concept obvious:

USER CONTEXT Colomiers ≠ PHYSICAL LOCATION ↓ RELEVANT BOXING CENTER VENUE Minimes / Portet

The visible page should already communicate this without needing schema.

Schema confirms.

It does not repair bad content.


## 08.153 — PAGE-SPECIFIC OG SYSTEM

Each route gets an OG composition.

Examples:


### MMA

MMA DEPUIS COLOMIERS COLOMIERS

- ────────
- PORTET


### Children

BOXE ENFANTS DEPUIS COLOMIERS 3+ / ÉDUCATIVE / ADOS

Only verified age bands.


### Quel Club

MINIMES ╲ COLOMIERS

- ╱ PORTET

Brand/context clearly different from physical-location branding.


## 08.154 — PAGE ACCEPTANCE GATE

A route cannot ship until:


### SEARCH

It owns a distinct intent.


### HUMAN

It answers the searcher's question early.


### TRUTH

No fake Colomiers physical presence.


### DEPTH

It provides standalone value.


### DIFFERENTIATION

Not a noun-swapped sibling template.


### FACTS

Every volatile claim resolves from registry.


### DESTINATION

Real physical club clearly identified.


### INTERNAL LINKS

Logical next questions linked.


### GEO

AI can describe the entity relationship correctly.


### ACCESSIBILITY

Interaction is semantic and keyboard-usable.


### MOBILE

Primary answer and action arrive quickly.


### PERFORMANCE

Critical content ships server-side.


### CONVERSION

One sensible next action exists.


### BAFFLED

The page contains a mechanism or composition growing specifically from its user's problem.


## 08.155 — PART 08 LOCKED ROUTE JOBS

/boxe-anglaise/ LEARN THE PRACTICE + CHOOSE WHERE TO BOX /mma/ UNDERSTAND BOXING CENTER'S REAL MMA OPTION /boxing-fitness/ FIND BOXING WITHOUT COMPETITION PRESSURE /boxe-enfants/ MAKE A SAFE, AGE-APPROPRIATE PARENT DECISION /premiere-seance/ REMOVE FEAR OF WALKING THROUGH THE DOOR /quel-club/ TURN NEEDS INTO A TRANSPARENT CLUB RECOMMENDATION /plannings/ TURN AVAILABILITY INTO A REAL SESSION /tarifs/ TURN PRICE QUESTIONS INTO VERIFIED OPTIONS /contact/ TURN UNRESOLVED NEED INTO HUMAN HELP


## 08.156 — THE PAGE-SYSTEM +1

The nine pages are not nine independent landing pages.

Together they form:


### A DECISION GRAPH

A visitor may enter at any node:

MMA CHILD PRICE TIME BEGINNER

and the site can route them logically toward:

DISCIPLINE ↓ REAL CLUB ↓ TIME ↓ FIRST SESSION ↓ ACTION

This means the website does not have one funnel.

**It has multiple truthful entry points into one shared decision system.**

That is significantly stronger than a traditional SEO sitemap.


## 08.157 — PART 08 IN ONE SENTENCE

**Every Boxing Center Colomiers route exists to answer one distinct local** **decision completely, then pass the visitor—not blindly, but knowingly—to the** **next question or the correct real-world Boxing Center destination.**

This completes the canonical page system.


<a id="part-09-content-editorial-system"></a>

# PART 09 — Content & Editorial System


## 09.0 — PURPOSE

The brief asks for a tone that is:

- clear;
- local;
- reassuring;
- sporty;
- accessible;
- professional;
- suitable for beginners and experienced practitioners;

and specifically says the site should avoid an overly aggressive or competitive tone.

That is the baseline.

Our standard goes much further.

The content system must make Boxing Center Colomiers sound like:

**someone who knows combat sports extremely well, understands why the visitor hesitates, refuses to exaggerate, and can tell them exactly what to do next.**

The website must not sound like:

- an SEO writer;
- a motivational coach shouting on Instagram;
- an institution;
- a fight promoter;
- an AI assistant;
- a luxury agency trying to impress another agency.

The editorial language must support the same conceptual system as the interface:


### PRECISION UNDER PRESSURE


### THE RESOLVING ROUTE


### RED IS EARNED

In language, this becomes:


### CERTAINTY IS EARNED.

We never sound more certain than our evidence allows.


## 09.1 — EDITORIAL THESIS

The core voice is:


### DIRECT. KNOWLEDGEABLE. REASSURING. UNSENTIMENTAL.

We explain rather than sell.

We prove rather than proclaim.

We guide rather than pressure.

We speak like someone standing beside the visitor saying:

“Here is what you need to know.”

Not:

“TRANSFORM YOUR LIFE TODAY 🔥”


## 09.2 — THE FIVE VOICE AXES

Every piece of copy should sit somewhere on these five axes:

DIRECT ←────────────→ FORMAL HUMAN ←────────────→ INSTITUTIONAL PRECISE ←────────────→ PROMOTIONAL CALM ←────────────→ AGGRESSIVE EXPERT ←────────────→ TECHNICAL-JARGON

Our position:

DIRECT ████████░░ HUMAN ████████░░ PRECISE █████████░ CALM ████████░░ EXPERT ████████░░

Formal enough to inspire trust.

Human enough to reduce fear.


## 09.3 — TUTOIEMENT OR VOUVOIEMENT?


### LOCKED RECOMMENDATION: TUTOIEMENT

For the main site experience.

Reasons:

- the brand is sporting and direct;
- the interface asks the visitor to make personal decisions;

- the journey is conversational;
- it reduces institutional distance;
- it works naturally with commands such as:

○ Trouver mon club

○ Préparer ma première séance

○ Quand peux-tu venir ?

But the tutoiement must remain **respectful and adult**.

Not adolescent.

Not slang-heavy.


## 09.4 — TUTOIEMENT EXAMPLES

Good:

Tu n'as jamais boxé ? Aucun problème. Commence par comprendre ce qui se passe pendant une première séance.

Good:

Tu cherches du MMA depuis Colomiers ? Le club pertinent dépend de ce que tu veux réellement pratiquer.

Bad:

T'es chaud pour envoyer du lourd ?

Bad:

Viens casser la salle avec nous !

Bad:

Alors champion, prêt à monter sur le ring ?

Absolutely not.


## 09.5 — EXCEPTIONS TO TUTOIEMENT

Legal/privacy language may use neutral/formal constructions.

Example:

Les données transmises via ce formulaire sont utilisées pour répondre à votre demande.

This is acceptable because legal clarity outranks stylistic uniformity.

Transactional email language can be decided separately based on existing Boxing Center practices.


## 09.6 — THE VOICE IN ONE FORMULA

Every paragraph should ideally combine:

FACT + INTERPRETATION + NEXT STEP

Example:

Portet propose actuellement du MMA et du grappling. Si ton objectif est de travailler à la fois debout et au sol, c'est donc l'une des options pertinentes depuis Colomiers. Voir les créneaux MMA.

This is excellent.

Compare:

Notre salle ultra-moderne vous propose des cours de MMA de qualité dans une ambiance conviviale et dynamique.

Generic.


## 09.7 — THE FACT / INTERPRETATION SEPARATION

This distinction is critical.


### FACT

Portet propose du MMA.


### INTERPRETATION

Cela en fait une option pertinente si ton objectif principal est le MMA.


### OPINION

Portet est le meilleur club MMA de la région.

The first two may be publishable with supporting data.

The third requires a completely different evidence burden and is generally forbidden.


## 09.8 — THE CLAIM CONFIDENCE SCALE

Editorially, claims live at one of five confidence levels.

LEVEL 1 — VERIFIED FACT "Portet propose actuellement du MMA." LEVEL 2 — DIRECT CONSEQUENCE "Si tu recherches du MMA, Portet fait partie des options pertinentes." LEVEL 3 — QUALIFIED INTERPRETATION "D'après tes critères, Portet semble être l'option la plus cohérente." LEVEL 4 — SUBJECTIVE CLAIM "Portet est excellent pour le MMA." LEVEL 5 — SUPERLATIVE "Portet est le meilleur club MMA près de Colomiers."


### Allowed by default

1–3.


### Requires explicit evidence/review

4.


### Forbidden by default

5.


## 09.9 — BANNED SUPERLATIVES

Do not use unless objectively verified and useful:

- meilleur;
- n°1;
- incontournable;
- référence absolue;
- le plus grand;
- le plus complet;
- leader;
- unique;
- exceptionnel;
- incomparable;
- premium;
- élite;
- haut de gamme;
- révolutionnaire.

Even when legally possible, these words are usually weaker than evidence.


## 09.10 — REPLACE ADJECTIVES WITH FACTS

Instead of:

Une salle ultra-équipée.

Write:

Ring, sacs de frappe, cage MMA et espaces d'entraînement dédiés — selon les équipements vérifiés du club concerné.

Instead of:

Des coachs très expérimentés.

Write:

Les séances sont encadrées par des coachs dont les qualifications sont présentées avec le club concerné.

Instead of:

Des horaires très larges.

Write the actual verified schedule/opening information.


## 09.11 — EDITORIAL HIERARCHY

The site uses four copy levels:

1. THESIS big memorable statement 2. ANSWER direct factual resolution 3. EXPLANATION context and detail 4. MICROCOPY action/state/support

Every copy unit should know which role it plays.


## 09.12 — THESIS COPY

Purpose:

memory.

Characteristics:

- short;
- strong;
- human;
- usually one idea;
- not stuffed with keywords.

Examples:

**TU PARS DE COLOMIERS. LE BON CLUB DÉPEND DE CE QUE TU CHERCHES.**

**PAS DE SALLE À COLOMIERS. UNE VRAIE RÉPONSE POUR SAVOIR OÙ ALLER.**

**BOXER POUR LA FORME. PAS BESOIN DE MONTER SUR UN RING.**

**JAMAIS MIS UN GANT ? VOILÀ CE QUI VA SE PASSER.**


## 09.13 — ANSWER COPY

Purpose:

certainty.

Characteristics:

- direct;
- factual;
- 1–3 sentences;
- answer begins immediately;
- limitations stated explicitly.

Example:

Boxing Center ne possède pas de salle physique à Colomiers. Les pratiquants venant de Colomiers sont orientés vers les clubs du réseau adaptés à la discipline recherchée, notamment Minimes et Portet-sur-Garonne.

This is excellent GEO content because it is also excellent human content.


## 09.14 — EXPLANATION COPY

Purpose:

depth.

Characteristics:

- readable paragraphs;
- specific;
- no artificial keyword repetition;
- one factual theme per paragraph;
- contextual links near relevant information.
