If a coach medically/technically reviews a specialist article later:

add real reviewer/author only with consent and accurate credentials.


## 10.116 — DISCIPLINE ENTITY MODEL

Do we need schema objects for MMA/boxing?

Possibly:

simple `Thing`/`DefinedTerm` relationships where genuinely beneficial.

But default:

the page title/H1/content already communicates topic.

Do not create a giant custom taxonomy graph without a consumer need.


## 10.117 — KEYWORDS META

No meta-keyword architecture.

Search intent is stored internally for planning:

primaryIntent secondaryIntents

but not dumped into an obsolete visible meta-keyword system.

Intent data supports:

- editorial decisions;
- reporting;
- cannibalization audits.


## 10.118 — GEO INTERNAL FIELDS

Each route internal object may carry:

geoQuestions: [ "...", "..." ], entitiesMentioned: [...], claimDependencies: [...], spatialContext: "colomiers"

These are **editorial/QA metadata**.

They do not all become `<meta>` tags.


## 10.119 — NO MADE-UP META TAGS

Forbidden:

<meta name="geo-keywords"> <meta name="ai-summary"> <meta name="chatgpt-description"> <meta name="llm-context"> <meta name="aeo-score">

unless a documented standard/platform actually uses them.

Baffled Bar means knowing what **not** to implement.


## 10.120 — AI CRAWL POLICY FILE

We can generate `robots.txt` dynamically from environment/config.

Concept:

```text
const botPolicy = {
indexPublicWeb: true,
allowOpenAISearch: true,
allowOpenAITraining: BUSINESS_DECISION
};
```

One location controls policy.

No hand-edited production robots surprises.


## 10.121 — SEARCH ENGINE DISCOVERY

Primary discovery signals:

crawlable internal links XML sitemap external Boxing Center links Search Console Bing Webmaster IndexNow for participating engines

No submission-to-500-search-engines services.


## 10.122 — GOOGLE SEARCH CONSOLE SETUP

Before launch:

verify domain property via DNS.

Then:

- submit sitemap;
- inspect homepage;
- inspect major routes;
- monitor indexing;
- monitor enhancements;
- monitor manual actions/security;
- monitor Search performance;
- monitor generative-AI performance.


## 10.123 — BING WEBMASTER SETUP

Verify domain or import from Google Search Console.

Bing currently supports importing verified Search Console sites into Webmaster Tools.

Then:

- sitemap;
- IndexNow;
- URL Inspection;
- backlinks;
- SEO issues.


## 10.124 — BING URL INSPECTION

Bing's current URL Inspection provides:

- index status;
- crawl information;
- SEO errors;
- markup information;
- live URL view.

Use it during launch QA for canonical routes.


## 10.125 — SEARCH MONITORING BY INTENT

Do not report only:

ranking average.

Group Search Console terms:

BROAD boxe Colomiers club boxe Colomiers sport combat Colomiers MMA MMA Colomiers club MMA Colomiers ...

CHILD boxe enfant Colomiers ... FITNESS ... BEGINNER ...

Then monitor which canonical page receives each group.


## 10.126 — CANNIBALIZATION ALERT

If:

/

and

```text
/boxe-anglaise/
```

repeatedly alternate for the same exact high-value query:

investigate.

Possible causes:

- overlapping content;
- weak H1 differentiation;
- internal-link ambiguity;
- titles too similar;
- insufficient page-specific authority.

Do not “fix” by stuffing the keyword harder.


## 10.127 — AI VISIBILITY REPORTING

Dashboard should include:


### Google generative visibility

Search Console generative AI reporting.


### ChatGPT

referrals with `utm_source=chatgpt.com`.


### Bing/Copilot

Bing indexing/grounding-facing performance signals available through Webmaster Tools/analytics.


### Other agents

referrals where identifiable.


## 10.128 — NO FAKE “GEO SCORE”

We do not invent:

GEO score 93/100.

There is no universal authoritative metric.

Our GEO success indicators:

correct citations relevant referral traffic AI search impressions entity-correct answers high-quality indexed pages brand/entity query growth

Real outcomes.


## 10.129 — AI ANSWER QA

Before launch, manually test representative questions across major systems.

Examples:

Y a-t-il un Boxing Center à Colomiers ?

Correct expected:

No physical location; site directs toward nearby network clubs.

Not:

Yes, Boxing Center Colomiers is located at...

If machine answers incorrectly:

inspect the content/entity signals that might cause ambiguity.


## 10.130 — GEO TEST QUERIES

Test:

Where can I do boxing near Colomiers? Does Boxing Center have a club in Colomiers? Which Boxing Center is best for MMA from Colomiers? Does Boxing Center Portet offer MMA? Where can children box near Colomiers? How much is Boxing Center near Colomiers? What happens during a first boxing session?

We do **not** expect perfect citations immediately after launch.

We are testing semantic clarity.


## 10.131 — MACHINE DIFFERENCE TEST

Ask:

Could a crawler confuse Colomiers with a physical venue?

Inspect:

- title;
- H1;
- JSON-LD;
- footer;
- contact;
- photos;
- filenames;
- OG;
- schema.

One misleading surface = fix system-wide.


## 10.132 — ENTITY NAME TEST

Search codebase for:

"Boxing Center Colomiers"

Each occurrence must be reviewed.

Some may be legitimate domain/site shorthand.

Any occurrence that sounds like a physical location is rejected.

Preferred contextual naming:

Boxing Center depuis Colomiers Boxingcenter-colomiers.fr


## 10.133 — CONTENT CONSISTENCY TEST

For each factual claim:

compare:

visible HTML JSON-LD llms metadata where relevant recommendation engine schedule pricing

Mismatch:

release blocker.


## 10.134 — PRICING CONSISTENCY TEST

Search all rendered output for:

€

Every public amount must map to a valid Offer/Claim ID.

No raw price strings outside controlled exceptions.

This is a powerful static QA rule.


## 10.135 — HOURS CONSISTENCY TEST

Opening hours may appear only through registry-backed components.

No prose:

ouverts jusqu'à 21h15

manually typed in Markdown.

This would reintroduce the exact conflict we identified.


## 10.136 — CONTACT CONSISTENCY TEST

Phone/email from contact registry.

Search built output for alternate numbers/emails.

Any unexpected address:

fail.

The formal brief currently supplies:

05 62 24 46 82

and:

`bc.combat31@gmail.com`.

These remain source requirements subject to pre-launch current verification.


## 10.137 — STRUCTURED IMAGE PROVENANCE TEST

If schema says:

```text
image = Portet
```

asset must be tagged Portet.

Prevent media cross-location leakage programmatically.


## 10.138 — OG PROVENANCE TEST

If an OG image uses a Minimes photo:

card must not visually read:

Boxing Center Colomiers gym.

Add contextual labels.

Same truth standard applies outside body HTML.


## 10.139 — SITEMAP BUILD TEST

Assert:

- only 200 URLs;
- no redirects;
- no noindex;
- no query strings;
- no staging URLs;
- canonicals match;
- expected canonical-route count.

For this launch:

expected core content count should be known.


## 10.140 — ROBOTS BUILD TEST

Automatically request production:

```text
/robots.txt
```

Assert:

- public pages not globally disallowed;
- sitemap URL correct;
- no staging hostname;
- OAI-SearchBot not blocked if search visibility enabled;

- GPTBot matches chosen business policy.


## 10.141 — HTTP HEADER TEST

Check:

Content-Type Cache-Control X-Robots-Tag where intended Location redirects

No accidental global noindex header.

This has destroyed launches before across the web.

Treat it as critical.


## 10.142 — RENDERED HTML TEST

Fetch without JS.

Assert presence:

<title> meta description canonical H1 main content crawlable nav key internal links JSON-LD

If a crawler needs hydration to understand the page:

fail.


## 10.143 — OG TEST

Use live social debugger/testing tools after deployment.

Check:

- image loads;
- absolute URL;
- correct title;
- correct Colomiers context;
- no stale cached staging card.


## 10.144 — IMAGE CRAWL TEST

Ensure optimized media is not blocked by:

- robots;
- auth;
- hotlink security;
- incorrect CDN ACL;
- broken lazy-loading implementation.

Googlebot-Image must be able to access meaningful images.


## 10.145 — JAVASCRIPT AND SEO

Interactive UI may exist.

But critical:

discipline copy club facts direct answers links page headings

must exist in server-rendered HTML.

Recommendation personalization may be client-side.

The factual alternatives it relies on remain crawlable elsewhere on page/routes.


## 10.146 — PROXIMITY ENGINE INDEXING

The engine result does not generate indexable output combinations.

Google doesn't need to crawl every possible result.

Search pages provide canonical semantic content.

Engine serves the human.


## 10.147 — PLANNING INDEXING

`/plannings/` contains enough baseline text/data to be useful when crawled.

Filtered combinations are user states.

No:

```text
/plannings/mma/lundi/soir/portet/
```

page factory.


## 10.148 — SEARCH RESULT QUALITY

Google's current search appearance documentation reminds us that result presentation includes:

- site name;
- favicon;
- URL/breadcrumb;

- title;
- snippet.

We therefore treat the entire SERP result as a **mini interface**.

Not only the title.


## 10.149 — SERP BRANDING TARGET

Ideal result:

[BC favicon] Boxing Center depuis Colomiers boxingcenter-colomiers.fr › mma MMA près de Colomiers | Où s'entraîner avec Boxing Center Tu habites Colomiers et veux essayer le MMA ? Découvre où Boxing Center le propose réellement...

This immediately communicates:

- source;
- context;
- topic;
- truth.


## 10.150 — NO DATE IN EVERGREEN TITLES

Do not add:

2026

to discipline titles merely for freshness.

Only where year is genuinely relevant.

Avoid annual SEO churn.


## 10.151 — CONTENT DATE

Where a page contains time-sensitive facts:

visible:

Données vérifiées le…

where useful.

Static educational content does not need a fake update date.


## 10.152 — FRESHNESS IS CLAIM-SPECIFIC

One page can contain:

discipline explanation → static schedule → verified today price → verified last week address → verified months ago

A single giant:

Page updated today

doesn't communicate this nuance.

Freshness belongs near volatile claims.


## 10.153 — GEO CONTENT NON-COMMODITY TEST

Google's 2026 AI-search guidance specifically emphasizes unique, non-commodity content.

For us:

Generic:

MMA combines striking and grappling.

Commodity.

Non-commodity:

For someone starting from Colomiers, Portet currently emerges in the Boxing Center network for MMA because [verified programme/facility/schedule evidence].

That is proprietary decision context.


## 10.154 — GEO CITABILITY TEST

Ask of each page:

Is there a sentence an answer engine could confidently cite without needing to reinterpret us?

Example:

Boxing Center ne possède pas de salle physique à Colomiers.

Excellent.

Nos clubs vous accueillent à proximité.

Ambiguous.

Direct facts win.


## 10.155 — LOCAL ENTITY CONSISTENCY

Across:

homepage footer contact schema OG machine files club comparison

Colomiers must always remain:


### ORIGIN / CONTENT CONTEXT

Minimes/Portet remain:


### PHYSICAL DESTINATIONS

This is probably the single most important GEO quality gate.


## 10.156 — NO NEIGHBORING-CITY EXPANSION FACTORY

Do not create:

/boxe-tournefeuille/ /boxe-pibrac/ /boxe-blagnac/

through data templates.

Part 02 already rejected this because it risks doorway architecture.

Part 10 enforces:

no dynamic city route generator in code.


## 10.157 — IF FUTURE CITY SITES ARE REQUESTED

Each requires:

- independent local research;
- standalone value;
- unique decision problem;
- its own claim context;
- non-duplicated content;
- anti-doorway review.

Not cloning Colomiers and replacing the place name.


## 10.158 — ENTITY-GRAPH VERSIONING

Entity records are source controlled.

Meaningful changes:

club address club canonical URL organisation logo location existence

receive explicit commit history.

No mysterious configuration edits.


## 10.159 — SEARCH CONTRACT TESTS IN CI

Recommended automated tests:

✓ exactly one H1

✓ non-empty title ✓ non-empty description ✓ canonical absolute ✓ canonical host correct ✓ robots expected ✓ all internal href targets resolve ✓ no staging domains ✓ no accidental noindex ✓ JSON-LD parses ✓ no LocalBusiness Colomiers ✓ sitemap canonical only ✓ OG image exists ✓ image alt rules ✓ one primary intent per route

Turn SEO from checklist into build integrity.


## 10.160 — BROKEN LINK CI

Crawl generated output.

Check:

internal 4xx/5xx.

External club links can be checked separately with sensible timeout/retry so a temporary external outage doesn't constantly break deploys.

But stale canonical sister links must be surfaced.


## 10.161 — REDIRECT CI

No loops.

No chains if avoidable.

Ideal:

old URL → final canonical

not:

old → intermediate → non-www → slash → final

Normalize at infrastructure layer.


## 10.162 — SEARCH PERFORMANCE BUDGET

SEO is affected indirectly by performance/user experience.

Our Part 12 performance system will lock actual budgets, but Part 10 requires:

- server-rendered meaningful content;
- stable layout;
- optimized images;
- minimal unnecessary JS;
- fast mobile experience.

The brief explicitly requires good speed and mobile performance.


## 10.163 — CORE WEB VITALS MONITORING

Post-launch:

monitor field data where volume permits.

Do not optimize only Lighthouse lab numbers.

Search/UX quality requires real-device behavior.

Detailed budgets come later.


## 10.164 — LOG ANALYSIS

Server/CDN logs can eventually verify:

- Googlebot access;
- Bingbot access;
- OAI-SearchBot access;
- crawl failures;
- 404 crawl patterns;
- unexpected bot-blocking.

This is particularly useful because WAF rules can contradict robots intent.


## 10.165 — BOT ACCESS QA

Simulate/request with relevant user agents only as part of controlled QA.

Verify:

public page returns:

200

not:

403 429 challenge page CAPTCHA

for legitimate crawlers.

OpenAI explicitly notes these infrastructure blocks as common crawler issues.


## 10.166 — SECURITY VS CRAWLABILITY

Do not weaken WAF globally to allow bots.

Use:

- verified bot mechanisms;
- documented user agents;
- provider bot verification;
- published ranges where needed.

Security remains real.

Search accessibility remains intentional.


## 10.167 — INDEX REMOVAL

If a page is permanently removed:

- 404/410;
- remove from sitemap;
- remove internal links;
- send IndexNow removal update for participating engines.

Bing specifically recommends IndexNow and sitemap updates to accelerate recognition of removals.


## 10.168 — TEMPORARY CONTENT REMOVAL

No SEO tricks.

Use correct status/index directives according to actual business state.

If maintenance:

503.

If page temporarily unavailable but returning:

sensible temporary handling.

Do not 301 temporary issues to homepage.


## 10.169 — LEGAL PAGES

Recommended:

/mentions-legales/ /confidentialite/

They remain crawlable to users.

Indexation policy can be:

noindex, follow

if we want to keep SERP inventory focused.

This is an editorial/index choice, not a security measure.


## 10.170 — CONTACT THANK-YOU PAGE

If a separate URL exists:

```text
/contact/merci/
```

recommended:

noindex, follow

No sitemap.

Analytics conversion event.


## 10.171 — FORM-ENDPOINT INDEXATION

API endpoints:

not search pages.

Do not expose them in sitemap.

Return appropriate non-HTML/API responses.

Protect against spam/server abuse independently of robots.


## 10.172 — GENERATED ASSET INDEXATION

CSS/JS/fonts/images need crawl access as required for rendering.

Do not block:

```text
/_astro/
```

globally if crawlers need those assets to render pages.

Search engines should see the same functional page.


## 10.173 — PAGE SOURCE QUALITY

View Source should reveal:

real title real description canonical real H1/content crawlable internal links JSON-LD

This is a quality test.

The HTML should look like a competent website even before JavaScript.


## 10.174 — HEAD COMPONENT

One controlled Astro component:

SeoHead

responsible for:

title description canonical robots OG Twitter favicons JSON-LD injection

But it consumes route metadata.

It doesn't invent metadata.


## 10.175 — SCHEMA COMPONENT

Potential:

```text
StructuredData.astro
```

receives already-built graph object.

It serializes safely.

No page pastes raw JSON strings.


## 10.176 — ASTRO site

Set production origin in:

```text
astro.config.mjs
```

Astro's official sitemap integration uses this configured site origin when generating canonical sitemap URLs.

This same origin should inform helper utilities.


## 10.177 — ENVIRONMENT SAFETY

Production hostname is not a random environment variable overwritten by preview deployment.

Use explicit configuration:

production preview development

Preview must never generate production-sitemap submissions automatically.


## 10.178 — SEARCH SUBMISSION DEPLOY HOOK

Production deploy successful:

1. build tests pass 2. deploy 3. verify health 4. identify changed canonical URLs 5. submit changed URLs to IndexNow

Do not submit preview URLs.

Google continues through sitemap/internal discovery, with manual recrawl only when appropriate.


## 10.179 — INDEXNOW CHANGE DETECTION

Best implementation:

Git/content hash compares prior deploy.

Only changed relevant URLs are submitted.

No need to send all ten URLs every five minutes.


## 10.180 — CONTENT UPDATE TRIGGER MATRIX


### Price changed

Update:

registry HTML pricing route relevant comparison JSON-LD if applicable llms sitemap lastmod IndexNow


### Schedule changed

Update:

planning recommendation availability llms if summarized lastmod IndexNow


### Address changed

High-priority entity update everywhere.


### New discipline

Potentially recommendation and route strategy review.

Not automatically new SEO page.


## 10.181 — METADATA UPDATE TRIGGER

Metadata changes only if:

- content intent changed;
- SERP performance shows title/description problem;
- entity naming changed;
- page purpose materially changed.

Not weekly SEO tinkering.


## 10.182 — SEARCH CONSOLE TEST WINDOWS

Post-launch observations should be done over meaningful windows.

Do not rewrite titles after three impressions.

Small local properties need sufficient data.

Use:

- query grouping;
- directional trends;
- destination click behavior;
- indexing status.


## 10.183 — RANK TRACKING

Optional supplementary rank tracking is useful for:

- priority Colomiers clusters;
- competitor movement;
- SERP composition.

But Search Console remains authoritative for actual Google impressions/clicks.

Do not optimize obsessively to one anonymous rank-checker.


## 10.184 — LOCAL SERP REVIEW

Periodically manually review:

boxe Colomiers MMA Colomiers

boxe enfant Colomiers boxing fitness Colomiers

Look at:

- result types;
- maps/local packs;
- organic sites;
- videos;
- images;
- AI answers;
- new competitors.

Search environment changes.

Blueprint stays principled.

Tactics evolve.


## 10.185 — COMPETITOR SCHEMA IS NOT OUR TEMPLATE

We may inspect competitor structured data to understand SERP mechanics.

We do not copy incorrect markup merely because it ranks.

Ranking does not validate markup quality.


## 10.186 — STRUCTURED DATA DOES NOT GUARANTEE RICH RESULTS

Google explicitly describes structured data as helping it understand page content and potentially making pages eligible for supported presentation features, but implementation does not guarantee display.

Therefore no project report says:

schema installed = rich results secured.


## 10.187 — SEO SUCCESS CRITERIA

Successful SEO means:

- correct canonical indexation;
- impressions for intended local clusters;
- right page ranking for right query;
- qualified destination clicks;
- lead actions;
- no geographic misunderstanding;
- useful organic growth.

Not:

100/100 plugin score.


## 10.188 — GEO SUCCESS CRITERIA

Successful GEO means:

- machines identify no physical Colomiers location;
- relevant nearby physical clubs are correctly attributed;
- answers cite/mention the correct route/entity;
- volatile facts stay fresh;
- AI search impressions/referrals grow;
- no hallucinated address;
- no club-fact cross-contamination.


## 10.189 — GEO FAILURE EXAMPLES


### CRITICAL

AI answer:

Boxing Center Colomiers is located at [invented address].

Critical failure.

AI answer:

Boxing Center Colomiers offers MMA at its Colomiers gym.

Critical failure.

AI answer:

Portet offers MMA.

Correct only if current registry confirms.

AI answer:

Minimes offers MMA.

Failure if registry does not.


## 10.190 — ENTITY QA PROMPTS

Manual QA:

What is Boxing Center Colomiers?

Expected:

an orientation/local-information site for people from Colomiers.

Where is Boxing Center Colomiers located?

Expected:

there is no physical Boxing Center location in Colomiers.

Which Boxing Center is relevant from Colomiers?

Expected:

Minimes and/or Portet based on verified needs.

Where is the MMA cage?

Expected:

real club attribution.


## 10.191 — SOURCE PROXIMITY TEST

For volatile answer:

“Portet offers MMA.”

Can human see source/club context close by?

If not:

improve.

Machines should not need to connect a fact to an entity five sections away.


## 10.192 — ENTITY COLLISION TEST

Never place:

COLOMIERS [address of Portet]

without explicit label.

Visual context can mislead even when HTML technically says the right thing.

SEO/GEO QA includes screenshot review.


## 10.193 — MAP POLICY

If a map appears:

pins only at **real physical clubs**.

Colomiers may appear as:

- user origin/context;
- city label;
- region.

Not a business pin.

The map visually reinforces entity truth.


## 10.194 — MAP STRUCTURED DATA

Do not derive location schema from the map UI.

Location entities come from registry.

Map consumes those entities.

Again:

data → interface.

Not interface → data.


## 10.195 — ORGANIZATION LOGO

Use the official asset.

Google Organization markup can use logo information to help understand/display organisation identity.

Stable URL.

Correct dimensions.

No Colomiers remix.


## 10.196 — ORGANIZATION ADDRESS

Do not add Colomiers.

If Boxing Center has an authoritative administrative/network address and it is appropriate:

registry may contain it.

Otherwise omit.

Google Organization markup has no requirement that we invent irrelevant properties.


## 10.197 — areaServed

We do **not** need to stuff:

Colomiers Tournefeuille Pibrac Blagnac ...

into organization markup.

The site's content `spatialCoverage` already communicates its geographic focus truthfully.

Use service-area properties only where they accurately model a real service relationship.


## 10.198 — LOCAL KEYWORD TO ENTITY RELATIONSHIP

Search phrase:

MMA Colomiers

Entity interpretation:

content focus = Colomiers discipline = MMA physical destination = verified Boxing Center club

Not:

local entity = Boxing Center Colomiers

This model is the heart of the site.


## 10.199 — MACHINE-READABLE ANSWER IN HTML

Example:

```text
<section aria-labelledby="mma-colomiers-answer">
<h2 id="mma-colomiers-answer">
Y a-t-il un Boxing Center MMA à Colomiers ?
</h2>
<p>
```

Non. Boxing Center ne possède pas de salle physique à Colomiers. ... </p> </section>

No special JSON needed.

Semantic HTML already says a lot.


## 10.200 — SEARCH + DESIGN ALIGNMENT

Our visual concept:

**route resolves toward real location.**

Our structured-data concept:

**entity graph resolves toward real location.**

Our content concept:

**answer resolves toward real location.**

Our SEO concept:

**query resolves toward canonical page.**

This is why the architecture is strong.

Every layer is performing the same logic.


## 10.201 — PART 10 +1

The technical +1 is:


### THE CLAIM GRAPH IS THE SEO SYSTEM.

Traditional local SEO:

keyword → page → schema

Our model:

QUERY ↓ INTENT ↓ PAGE OWNER ↓ ENTITY RELATIONSHIP ↓ VERIFIED CLAIMS ↓ VISIBLE ANSWER ↓ MACHINE GRAPH ↓ ACTION

That is much deeper.


## 10.202 — SECOND TECHNICAL +1


### GEO IS BUILT AROUND NON-CLAIMS TOO.

Most systems only encode what is true.

Ours also explicitly encodes what must **not be inferred**.

Example:

Colomiers physicalGym = false forbiddenInference: - no address - no LocalBusiness - no map pin - no “our Colomiers club”

This gives the implementation layer guardrails against hallucination.

That's unusually strong.


## 10.203 — MACHINE UNDERSTANDING ACCEPTANCE GATE

Before launch:


### ENTITY

Is Boxing Center the organisation?

Yes.

Is Colomiers represented as a physical Boxing Center location?

No.


### ROUTE

Does each intent have one canonical owner?

Yes.


### CANONICAL

Do redirects, `<link canonical>`, sitemap and links agree?

Yes.


### STRUCTURED DATA

Does markup match visible content?

Yes.


### LOCATION

Are Minimes/Portet the only physical location entities when relevant?

Yes.


### CLAIMS

Are volatile facts registry-backed?

Yes.


### IMAGES

Do filenames/alts/captions tell location truth?

Yes.


### ROBOTS

Are public pages crawlable?

Yes.


### NOINDEX

Are non-search pages properly controlled?

Yes.


### OPENAI

Is OAI-SearchBot able to crawl intended public content?

Yes.


### BING

Is sitemap + IndexNow configured?

Yes.


### GOOGLE

Is Search Console + sitemap configured?

Yes.


### AI

Can a machine answer “is there a gym in Colomiers?” correctly?

Yes.


### STAGING

Can staging enter search?

No.


### URLs

Any Vercel/localhost/stale sister domains?

Zero.


### BAFFLED

Are we implementing real machine clarity or merely installing SEO tags?

Real clarity.


## 10.204 — LOCKED MACHINE ARCHITECTURE

We now lock:


### ONE FACT, ONE AUTHORITY

No duplicated truths.

```text
COLOMIERS = CONTENT CONTEXT
```

Not physical location.


### REAL CLUBS = PHYSICAL ENTITIES

Minimes/Portet only when verified.


### NO LOCALBUSINESS FOR COLOMIERS

Permanent until reality changes.


### CANONICAL OWNERSHIP

One intent → one page.


### VISIBLE HTML FIRST

JSON-LD confirms, never repairs.


### SCHEMA MINIMALISM

Only meaningful entity types.


### SPATIAL COVERAGE, NOT FAKE ADDRESS

A very important semantic distinction.


### NO FAQ RICH-RESULT STRATEGY

Current Google FAQ rich results are deprecated.

```text
SITEMAP = CANONICAL URL LIST
```

No junk.

```text
LASTMOD = ACTUAL CHANGE
```

Not build date.


### ROBOTS ≠ SECURITY

Never confuse crawl control with access control.


### OAI-SEARCHBOT ALLOWED

For ChatGPT Search discoverability.


### GPTBOT SEPARATE

Business policy decision.


### INDEXNOW FOR PARTICIPATING ENGINES

Especially Bing/Copilot freshness.


### LLMS FILES ARE SUPPLEMENTAL

Not canonical and not magical.


### NO PUBLIC MCP WITHOUT A PRODUCT NEED

Avoid complexity theatre.


### AI VISIBILITY IS MEASURED

Google generative reporting + ChatGPT referrals + Bing monitoring.


### NON-CLAIMS ARE DATA

The system knows what it must never infer.


## 10.205 — PART 10 IN ONE SENTENCE

**Boxing Center Colomiers becomes machine-readable not by adding more** **SEO markup, but by ensuring that every URL, sentence, image, entity,** **schema node, crawler directive and AI-facing representation tells exactly the** **same geographically truthful story: Colomiers is where the search begins;** **real Boxing Center clubs are where the training happens.**


<a id="part-11-accessibility-system"></a>

# PART 11 — Accessibility System


## 11.0 — ACCESSIBILITY THESIS

Accessibility is not:

“make sure axe gives no errors at the end.”

It is:


### THE EXPERIENCE MUST SURVIVE THE LOSS OF ANY SINGLE MODE OF PERCEPTION OR INTERACTION.

Remove:

- mouse;
- animation;
- color;
- fine vision;
- high bandwidth;
- JavaScript;
- perfect motor precision;
- large screen;
- audio;

and the decision system must remain intelligible.

Our target is:


### WCAG 2.2 LEVEL AA AS THE RELEASE BASELINE

plus selected stronger internal rules taken from AAA where they materially improve the experience.

W3C recommends WCAG 2.2 as the current target and WCAG 2.2 adds, among other things, minimum target-size, dragging alternatives and focus-not-obscured requirements.


## 11.1 — ACCESSIBILITY + BAFFLED BAR

The accessible version is **not**:

the simple fallback.

It must preserve:

- identity;
- hierarchy;
- confidence;
- editorial quality;
- decision usefulness.

If reduced-motion mode looks like the site broke:

fail.

If keyboard focus looks like an engineering debug outline pasted onto the design:

fail.

If mobile zoom destroys The Resolving Route:

fail.

Accessibility itself has to reach Baffled Bar.


## 11.2 — THREE CONFORMANCE TIERS


### TIER A — REQUIRED WCAG 2.2 AA

No exceptions for artistic ambition.


### TIER B — INTERNAL ENHANCED STANDARD

We voluntarily adopt selected stronger behaviors.

Examples:

- focus indicator designed toward WCAG 2.4.13 AAA geometry/contrast;
- 44×44px practical primary control target where layout permits;
- no important focus partially hidden even though AA technically only requires it not be

fully obscured;

- non-essential interaction animation removed when reduced motion is requested.


### TIER C — CONTEXTUAL EXCELLENCE

Sport-specific accessibility.

Examples:

- schedules understandable without spatial diagrams;
- club comparison does not rely on color;
- no drag requirement;
- no swipe requirement;
- real address/club name remains available in text;
- first-session content works without photography.


## 11.3 — LANDMARK ARCHITECTURE

Every page follows a clear landmark structure.

```text
HEADER
│
├── NAV
│
└── optional context controls
│
MAIN
│
├── H1
├── content sections
└── product interfaces
│
FOOTER
```

Semantic:

```text
<header>
<nav aria-label="Navigation principale">
...
</nav>
</header>
<main id="main-content">
```

... </main> <footer> ... </footer>

No `<div role="main">` when `<main>` exists.

Native first.


## 11.4 — SKIP LINK

First interactive item:


### ALLER AU CONTENU PRINCIPAL

Normally visually hidden.

On keyboard focus:

immediately visible.

Target:

```text
<main id="main-content">
```

If the page contains a major decision tool before long content, a second contextual skip may sometimes be justified:

Aller au sélecteur de club

but we do not create a skip-link menu unless needed.


## 11.5 — HEADING CONTRACT

Each page:

ONE H1 ↓ H2 page sections ↓ H3 subsections/questions

No visual sizing determines semantic heading level.

A tiny heading can be `h2`.

A huge display phrase may remain a paragraph if it is not structurally a heading.

W3C requires headings and labels to describe their topic or purpose at AA.


## 11.6 — NO HEADING SKIPPING BY COMPONENT ACCIDENT

Components never blindly emit:

`h2`.

Page owns hierarchy.

Example:

<SectionHeading as="h2" />

Then nested question:

<AnswerHeading as="h3" />

No:

H1 H4 H2 H6

because typography happened to look right.


## 11.7 — DOCUMENT LANGUAGE

Primary:

```text
<html lang="fr">
```

or appropriately localized `fr-FR` implementation where desired.

Isolated non-French passages receive correct language metadata where material.

Brand names do not need artificial language switching.


## 11.8 — KEYBOARD ABSOLUTE RULE


### EVERY FUNCTION MUST BE COMPLETABLE WITHOUT A POINTER.

W3C requires functionality to remain keyboard-operable and prohibits keyboard traps.

This covers:

- navigation;
- discipline disclosure;
- Intent Selector;
- Proximity Engine;
- planning filters;
- club comparison;
- form;
- cookie preferences;
- menu/dialog;
- every CTA.


## 11.9 — TAB ORDER

Tab order follows:


### MEANING, NOT VISUAL POSITION.

Never use positive:

```text
tabindex="5"
```

to repair DOM architecture.

Permitted:

```text
tabindex="0"
tabindex="-1"
```

only for legitimate behavior.

CSS layout does not rearrange logical interaction order into nonsense.


## 11.10 — FOCUS STYLE

Focus receives a dedicated token:

--focus-width: 3px; --focus-offset: 3px;

Conceptual:

:focus-visible { outline: var(--focus-width) solid var(--focus-color);

outline-offset: var(--focus-offset); }

The visual system should approach WCAG 2.4.13's enhanced focus guidance: an indicator roughly equivalent to a 2 CSS-pixel perimeter with at least 3:1 contrast between focused and unfocused states.


## 11.11 — FOCUS = ROUTE NODE LANGUAGE

We should not merely “brand” focus red.

Instead focus shares geometry with the route system:

ordinary node ○ focused ◎ selected


But:

focus ≠ selection.

Keyboard users must be able to distinguish:

where I am

from:

what I chose.


## 11.12 — FOCUS MUST REMAIN VISIBLE

Sticky header.

Mobile bottom action.

Cookie panel.

Dialogs.

Nothing may entirely cover the focused element.

WCAG 2.2 specifically introduced **Focus Not Obscured (Minimum)** at AA.

Our internal target is stronger:

important controls should remain fully visible where practically possible.


## 11.13 — STICKY UI OFFSET

For anchored/focused content:

scroll-margin-top: calc(var(--header-height) + var(--space-4));

Bottom sticky controls must not hide form actions or schedule rows.

Test short viewport heights.

Not only iPhone screenshots.


## 11.14 — POINTER TARGET STANDARD

WCAG 2.2 AA requires pointer targets to satisfy a 24×24 CSS-pixel minimum or its defined spacing/equivalent exceptions.

Our internal standard:


### 44×44 CSS PX PREFERRED MINIMUM

for standalone interactive controls.

Primary mobile CTAs:

approximately:

52–60px height.

Inline text links are naturally exempt from that visual requirement.


## 11.15 — DO NOT PACK FILTER CHIPS

Bad:

[MMA][BOXE][JJB][K1][LADY][ENFANT]

at tiny sizes.

Use:

large rows or sufficiently separated controls.

Motor accessibility and premium composition point in the same direction.


## 11.16 — DRAGGING

No function may require dragging.

WCAG 2.2 AA now requires a single-pointer non-drag alternative when dragging is not essential.

Therefore:

If future schedule UI supports dragging:

also provide buttons/selects.

If club slider supports swipe:

also provide explicit controls.

If route graphic reacts to dragging:

the underlying decision must still use ordinary controls.


## 11.17 — SWIPE


### SWIPE IS AN ENHANCEMENT, NEVER THE ONLY COMMAND.

No:

Swipe left for Portet.

without:

PORTET →

button.

No hidden mobile gesture grammar.


## 11.18 — DEVICE MOTION

No shake.

No tilt-to-select.

No motion-actuation requirement.

The site has no legitimate need for accelerometer interaction.


## 11.19 — ROUTE GRAPH ACCESSIBILITY

The Resolving Route is primarily a **visual explanation**.

When its information already exists in visible controls/text:

```text
<svg aria-hidden="true">
```

Screen readers do not need:

circle, line, path, line, line...


## 11.20 — WHEN ROUTE GRAPH IS INTERACTIVE

If visual nodes correspond to selectable options:

semantic HTML controls remain the real interaction mechanism.

Example:

```text
<label>
<input
type="radio"
name="club"
value="portet"
>
Boxing Center Portet
</label>
```

SVG state mirrors selection.

The SVG never becomes the only access path.


## 11.21 — ROUTE INFORMATION EQUIVALENCE

Visual:

COLOMIERS ↓ MMA ↓ PORTET

Text equivalent:

Pour le critère MMA sélectionné, Boxing Center Portet correspond actuellement au programme vérifié.

This is the actual semantic answer.


## 11.22 — COLOR ACCESSIBILITY

Color never owns state.

Selected:

RED + filled node + weight + label/status

Unavailable:

muted +

dash + "Non proposé"

Error:

red + symbol + message


## 11.23 — CONTRAST

Locked minimum:


### Normal text

4.5:1


### Large text

3:1


### Meaningful UI/non-text graphics

`3:1` where WCAG requires.

We target above minimum rather than designing against its exact edge.


## 11.24 — MUTED COPY

No fashionable:

#999 on #F4F1EA 12px / 300 weight

for meaningful metadata.

Source labels and freshness information matter.

They must remain readable.


## 11.25 — RED SIGNAL

Because red represents resolution, it must remain distinguishable in:

- grayscale;
- common color-vision deficiencies;
- forced-colors mode.

Therefore shape/status language always accompanies it.


## 11.26 — FORCED COLORS

Test Windows High Contrast/forced colors.

Do not write:

```text
outline: none;
border: none;
```

and rely exclusively on backgrounds.

Controls retain recognizable boundaries.

SVG route states that disappear under forced colors cannot be essential.


## 11.27 — REFLOW

WCAG requires ordinary horizontally written content to reflow at a width equivalent to **320 CSS** **pixels**, except content whose meaning genuinely requires two-dimensional layout such as some tables or maps.

Therefore:


### 320PX IS A RELEASE VIEWPORT.

Not an edge case.


## 11.28 — 400% ZOOM

Desktop testing:

1280 CSS px viewport → 400% browser zoom → equivalent narrow content environment.

Expected:

- no page-level horizontal scroll;
- navigation usable;
- route graphics adapt;
- text wraps;
- CTA remains visible;
- forms usable.

A genuinely two-dimensional comparison table may have local horizontal scrolling.

The **whole page may not**.


## 11.29 — 200% TEXT RESIZE

No clipped headings.

No fixed-height button labels.

No labels disappearing.

No overlapping schedule times.

No truncated form errors.


## 11.30 — TEXT SPACING RESILIENCE

Interface must survive user CSS approximating WCAG text-spacing conditions:

- increased line height;
- increased paragraph spacing;
- increased letter spacing;
- increased word spacing.

Therefore:

no component's height depends on exact font metrics.


## 11.31 — SCREEN ORIENTATION

No experience requires landscape.

Mobile portrait works.

Landscape works.

If visual route graph gains extra richness landscape:

fine.

Function remains available portrait.


## 11.32 — REDUCED MOTION

Part 06 remains authoritative.

W3C explicitly identifies non-essential interaction-triggered motion as potentially problematic and recommends mechanisms such as `prefers-reduced-motion`.

Rule:

```text
@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
/* immediate/still state */
}
```

Identity survives through:

- type;
- geometry;
- color;
- layout.


## 11.33 — NO FLASH

No flashing visual treatment.

No rapidly pulsing selection.

No strobe video.

Combat energy does not justify seizure risk.


## 11.34 — FORM ACCESSIBILITY

Every field:

VISIBLE LABEL CONTROL HELP IF NEEDED ERROR IF NEEDED

Not:

placeholder = label.

Grouping uses:

```text
<fieldset>
<legend>
```

for related choices.


## 11.35 — FORM REQUIRED STATE

Use both:

required

and clear visual/text indication.

Do not communicate required fields only by red `*`.

If most fields are required, it may be cleaner to label the few optional fields instead.


## 11.36 — ERROR MODEL

On invalid submission:

1. preserve entered data; 2. show error summary; 3. move focus to error summary where appropriate; 4. link/identify affected fields; 5. show field-specific error; 6. use `aria-invalid`;

7. connect description with `aria-describedby`.

No full-form reset.


## 11.37 — ERROR COPY

Bad:

Erreur.

Good:

Il manque un numéro ou un e-mail pour que l'équipe puisse te répondre.

The error describes resolution.


## 11.38 — LIVE STATUS

Recommendation update:

```text
<p role="status">
Recommandation mise à jour :
Boxing Center Portet.
</p>
```

Do not announce visual choreography.

Only outcome.


## 11.39 — ASSERTIVE ANNOUNCEMENTS

Reserve assertive live announcements for genuinely urgent/errors requiring immediate attention.

Do not make every selector change interrupt a screen reader.


## 11.40 — MOBILE DIALOG

When menu opens:

- focus enters;
- background inert;
- Escape closes;
- Tab cycles within;
- close action visible;
- focus returns to trigger.

When closed:

the route resumes where user was.


## 11.41 — COOKIE PREFERENCES ACCESSIBILITY

Cookie UI follows the same standard.

Buttons:

**TOUT REFUSER**

**TOUT ACCEPTER**

**PERSONNALISER**

all keyboard accessible.

No tiny:

continuer sans accepter

link.

Privacy cannot be an accessibility dark pattern.


## 11.42 — SCHEDULE ACCESSIBILITY

Visual agenda does not rely on pixel position alone.

Each session has textual:

DAY TIME DISCIPLINE CLUB LEVEL/AUDIENCE if relevant

Example:

<article> <h3>MMA</h3> <p> Mercredi, <time datetime="18:30">18h30</time>, Boxing Center Portet. </p> </article>


## 11.43 — TABLE ACCESSIBILITY

Where genuinely tabular:

use `<table>`.

Include:

- caption;
- <thead>;

- <th>;
- appropriate row/column scope.

Do not create a visual grid of `<div>` elements and attempt to reconstruct table semantics with ARIA.


## 11.44 — MOBILE TABLES

Provide a textual decision summary before complex comparison.

If table must scroll horizontally:

make scrolling obvious.

Do not hide scrollbar intentionally.

No information visible only by hover.


## 11.45 — IMAGE ACCESSIBILITY

Part 09 image decision tree remains authoritative:


### Informative

meaningful alt.


### Decorative

`alt=""`.


### Functional

alt communicates action.


### Complex

surrounding content provides full equivalent.


## 11.46 — VIDEO

If video contains meaningful spoken information:

captions required.

If information is not otherwise available in the page:

transcript/appropriate equivalent.

Autoplay sound:

forbidden.

Meaningful video controls:

keyboard accessible.


## 11.47 — MAPS

Map is always secondary to text.

Address/location information exists outside the map.

No one must manipulate a map to discover where the club is.

Real club CTA:

Voir Boxing Center Portet

remains.


## 11.48 — EXTERNAL LINKS

External ↗ is a visual cue.

Accessible link text already says:

Découvrir Boxing Center Portet.

If a link intentionally opens a new window—which should be rare—the user should be informed.

Default:

same-tab navigation.


## 11.49 — NO-JAVASCRIPT MODE

Must retain:

- navigation;
- discipline content;
- physical club links;
- pricing facts;
- contact details;
- first-session guidance;
- authoritative planning links.

The full Proximity Engine may degrade into:


### transparent manual comparison

not blank space.


## 11.50 — ACCESSIBILITY TEST MATRIX

Minimum manual matrix:


### Windows

- Chrome + NVDA
- Firefox + NVDA


### macOS

- Safari + VoiceOver


### iOS

- Safari + VoiceOver


### Android

- Chrome + TalkBack

Plus:

- keyboard-only;
- touch;
- zoom;
- reduced motion;
- forced colors;
- JS off.

No single AT/browser combination proves accessibility.


## 11.51 — AUTOMATED TESTING

Automated:

- axe-core;
- semantic linting;
- broken ARIA relationship checks;
- color-contrast test where tool reliability permits;
- heading checks;
- form-label checks.

But:


### ZERO AXE ERRORS DOES NOT MEAN ACCESSIBLE.

Automated tools cannot reliably judge:

- reading logic;
- alt usefulness;
- keyboard experience quality;
- understandable labels;
- screen-reader verbosity;
- cognitive clarity.

Manual testing remains mandatory.


## 11.52 — ACCESSIBILITY CI BLOCKERS

Build/release should fail for:

missing form label duplicate critical IDs missing image alt attribute invalid ARIA relationship page without H1 page without title non-keyboard-operable custom control known critical/serious axe issue

Other warnings reviewed consciously.


## 11.53 — ACCESSIBILITY STATEMENT

A public accessibility statement is worthwhile **only when we can honestly maintain it**.

Do not publish:

100% accessible.

Better, if adopted:

- target standard;
- known limitations;
- contact route for problems;
- review date.

Legal requirements depend on the organisation's applicable regulatory status and should be confirmed separately.

We do not invent compliance claims.


## 11.54 — ACCESSIBILITY HATER TEST

Can I:

- use the entire site with Tab/Enter/Space?
- understand selected state without red?
- reach every destination without SVG?
- refuse analytics without difficulty?
- complete contact form at 400% zoom?
- use planning without horizontal page scrolling?
- use the site without animations?
- identify errors without sound/color?
- still understand the page if photography fails?

If any answer is no:

not Baffled Bar.


## 11.55 — ACCESSIBILITY +1


### ACCESSIBILITY RESOLVES THE SAME ROUTE THROUGH DIFFERENT SENSES.

Visual user:

line → node → red route → Portet

Screen-reader user:

criterion → result → reason → Portet

Keyboard user:

focus → selection → confirmation → Portet

Reduced-motion user:

state → result → Portet

Different rendering.


### SAME DECISION.

That is our accessibility philosophy.


<a id="part-12-performance-technical-architecture"></a>

# PART 12 — Performance & Technical Architecture


## 12.0 — PERFORMANCE THESIS

Performance is not:

“get Lighthouse 100.”

Performance is:


### THE SITE RESPONDS AT THE SPEED OF THE USER'S DECISION.

The client brief explicitly requires fast loading and strong mobile behavior.

Our design requires more.

No user should pay a technical tax for:

- The Resolving Route;
- motion;
- photography;
- analytics;
- comparison;
- club selection.


## 12.1 — OFFICIAL CORE WEB VITAL BASELINES

Current good thresholds at the 75th percentile are:

LCP ≤ 2.5 s INP ≤ 200 ms CLS ≤ 0.1

measured separately across mobile/desktop where appropriate.

Those are **maximum good thresholds**.

Not our aspiration.


## 12.2 — INTERNAL BAFFLED BAR TARGETS

Target field performance where traffic/data permits:

LCP ≤ 2.0 s INP ≤ 150 ms CLS ≤ 0.05

These are internal quality budgets, not alternative Google definitions.

If field traffic is insufficient:

lab tests + real-device testing are used until enough field data exists.


## 12.3 — PERFORMANCE PRIORITY ORDER

1. HTML 2. typography 3. primary image 4. primary interaction 5. secondary images 6. enhanced motion 7. analytics

Never:

WebGL ↓ font ↓ tracker ↓ H1


## 12.4 — ARCHITECTURAL MODE

Recommended:


### ASTRO STATIC-FIRST

Canonical routes pre-render to HTML.

Dynamic server logic exists only where the product genuinely requires it.

Astro's islands architecture hydrates only explicitly interactive components while leaving the rest as static HTML.


## 12.5 — DEFAULT RENDERING MAP

/ STATIC /boxe-anglaise/ STATIC /mma/ STATIC

/boxing-fitness/ STATIC /boxe-enfants/ STATIC /premiere-seance/ STATIC /quel-club/ STATIC + CLIENT ISLAND /plannings/ STATIC + CLIENT FILTER /tarifs/ STATIC /contact/ STATIC /api/contact SERVER ENDPOINT

This is the recommended launch architecture.


## 12.6 — WHY CONTACT STAYS STATIC

Astro supports fully on-demand form pages and Actions with Zod validation.

However, this site does not need the **whole Contact page** to become request-rendered merely because one POST action exists.

Better:

static contact HTML ↓ POST /api/contact ↓ server validation ↓ mail adapter

This keeps:

- caching simple;
- failure surface small;
- public page fast;
- JavaScript optional.


## 12.7 — CONTACT ENDPOINT

Conceptual:

POST /api/contact

Accept only:

application/x-www-form-urlencoded multipart/form-data

where needed.

Reject oversized/unexpected requests.


## 12.8 — VALIDATION

Shared server schema:

```text
const ContactSchema = z.object({
firstName: z.string().trim().min(1).max(...),
lastName: ...,
phone: ...,
email: ...,
activity: ...,
preferredClub: ...,
message: ...
})
```

Client-side HTML constraints improve UX.

Server validation remains authoritative.


## 12.9 — CSRF

Astro currently enables same-origin checking by default for eligible on-demand mutation requests, using the `Origin` header as CSRF protection.

Locked:


### DO NOT DISABLE security.checkOrigin.

If infrastructure makes it fail:

fix host/proxy configuration.

Do not solve it with:

```text
checkOrigin: false
```

unless a security review explicitly replaces the protection.


## 12.10 — ALLOWED HOSTS

When SSR/on-demand deployment uses forwarded hosts:

configure allowed-domain validation where appropriate rather than trusting arbitrary incoming Host/X-Forwarded-Host values.


## 12.11 — MAIL DELIVERY ABSTRACTION

The web layer should not care whether email is delivered by:

- SMTP;
- Resend;
- Postmark;
- another transactional provider.

Create:

```text
interface LeadDeliveryAdapter {
deliver(lead: Lead): Promise<DeliveryResult>;
}
```

Recipient comes from server environment:

CONTACT_RECIPIENT=bc.combat31@gmail.com

subject to final pre-launch verification of the brief's contact data.


## 12.12 — NEVER SEND MAIL FROM CLIENT

No:

provider API key SMTP credential service secret

in browser JavaScript.

All secrets:

server environment only.


## 12.13 — FORM DELIVERY SUCCESS

A visitor receives “sent” only after the delivery provider accepts the request.

Not immediately after JavaScript fires.

If provider is unavailable:

say so.

Do not silently discard leads.


## 12.14 — LEAD RESILIENCE

Ideal:

validate ↓ delivery provider ↓ accepted ↓ success

If deployment stack supports a durable queue without disproportionate complexity:

a short retry queue can be added later.

At launch, success semantics must be honest.


## 12.15 — SPAM DEFENCE

Layered:


### 1

honeypot.


### 2

server rate limiting.


### 3

input-length limits.


### 4

minimum sensible interaction-time heuristic.


### 5

duplicate suppression over a very short window.


### 6

optional challenge only if real abuse appears.


## 12.16 — CAPTCHA POLICY


### NO CAPTCHA BY DEFAULT.

Do not make real users classify motorcycles because bots might someday arrive.

If attack volume justifies it:

add the least intrusive accessible challenge appropriate to the deployment.

Evidence before friction.


## 12.17 — FORM PII LOGGING

Never log:

- message;
- full name;
- phone;
- email.

Operational log:

request id timestamp delivery status error category duration

Maybe coarse anti-abuse data under retention policy.

No PII dump into server logs.


## 12.18 — STATIC DATA PIPELINE

Build starts from:

entities claims offers schedules media routes metadata

Then:

VALIDATE ↓ DERIVE ↓ BUILD

If critical data is invalid:

build fails.


## 12.19 — BUILD-TIME TRUTH VALIDATION

Before Astro renders:

no conflicted public critical claim no expired offer no missing canonical URL no fake Colomiers location no stale staging domain

no unowned media asset

This is faster and safer than discovering errors in production.


## 12.20 — SERVER ISLANDS

Astro server islands can defer slower personalized/server-rendered regions while allowing the main page to cache/render earlier.

But:


### NO SERVER ISLAND IS REQUIRED AT LAUNCH.

We do not use a feature because Astro provides it.

Potential future legitimate use:

live schedule backend too slow to block page generation.

Then:

static/current snapshot first live enhancement second.


## 12.21 — CLIENT ISLAND MAP

Likely:

Intent Selector Proximity Engine Schedule Explorer Mobile navigation form enhancement

Everything else:

zero hydration.


## 12.22 — ISLAND LOADING


### Above-fold required interaction

`client:load` only when genuinely needed immediately.


### Lower interactive system

`client:visible`.


### Low-priority enhancement

`client:idle`.

Astro explicitly supports selective component hydration rather than shipping all site JavaScript to every page.


## 12.23 — JAVASCRIPT BUDGET

Internal release budgets, compressed/gzip:


### Normal discipline page

initial client JS ≤ 35 KB

excluding browser-native platform behavior.


### Homepage

initial JS ≤ 70 KB

Target lower.

```text
/quel-club/
```

interactive JS ≤ 90 KB

only if the product logic genuinely needs it.


### Motion-specific

Part 06 remains:

≤ 15 KB gzip custom motion code


## 12.24 — FRAMEWORK COST

If one component requires 12KB business logic and importing a framework adds another substantial runtime:

challenge the framework choice.

Framework convenience is not free.


## 12.25 — CSS BUDGET

Target compressed:

shared/site CSS ≤ 70 KB

Ideally far lower.

No page ships an entire UI framework stylesheet.

No unused Tailwind-like universe if not actually needed.


## 12.26 — HTML

HTML can be rich.

Do not aggressively minimize semantics to save 3KB while shipping 100KB JavaScript.

Still:

avoid enormous duplicate SEO blocks and serialized unused application state.


## 12.27 — INITIAL NETWORK BUDGET

Typical mobile first load target:


### ≤ 900 KB TRANSFERRED

for normal route pages where photography permits.

Hard upper design review:

approximately:


### 1.2 MB

before an explicit exception is required.

This includes:

- HTML;
- CSS;
- JS;
- fonts;
- initial image.

Below-fold media is lazy.


## 12.28 — HERO IMAGE BUDGET

Typical mobile LCP candidate:

~120–250 KB

depending content complexity.

Desktop:

~200–400 KB

These are budgets, not fixed encoding rules.

Visual QA decides acceptable compression.


## 12.29 — RESPONSIVE IMAGES

Never serve a 2400px image to a 390px screen.

Use responsive:

srcset sizes

and next-gen formats.

web.dev explicitly recommends responsive images so browsers can download an appropriately sized resource rather than wasting mobile bandwidth.


## 12.30 — ASTRO IMAGE COMPONENT

`ProvenanceImage` should integrate Astro's image pipeline or an equally controlled optimization layer.

It owns:

- dimensions;
- formats;
- srcset;

- sizes;
- loading priority;
- alt;
- crop/focal point.

Page authors do not manually tune every `<img>`.


## 12.31 — LCP IMAGE

If an image is genuinely the LCP candidate:

```text
loading=eager
fetchpriority=high
```

only where appropriate.

web.dev warns that high fetch priority competes with other resources and should be reserved for truly important images.


## 12.32 — IMAGE PRELOAD

Prefer discoverability in initial HTML.

Only preload when browser discovery would otherwise be delayed.

Do not preload every hero candidate for every breakpoint.

Responsive preloading is possible, but should be targeted.


## 12.33 — BELOW-FOLD IMAGES

```text
loading="lazy"
```

where appropriate.

Dimensions reserved.

No CLS.


## 12.34 — CLS INTERNAL TARGET

Official good threshold:

`≤0.1`.

Our target:


### ≤0.05

Therefore every:

- image;
- video;
- route canvas;
- embedded tool;
- cookie banner;
- form state;

reserves predictable space or overlays intentionally.


## 12.35 — NEVER INSERT A COOKIE BANNER INTO FLOW AFTER LOAD

Cookie UI should be positioned without pushing the entire page down after first paint.

Either:

- reserved region;
- overlay/bottom sheet accessible implementation.

No CLS spike because consent script woke up.


## 12.36 — FONT SYSTEM

Prefer:


### ONE VARIABLE PRIMARY FAMILY.

Self-host WOFF2.

If secondary editorial family is later approved:

load only where actually used or carefully subset.


## 12.37 — FONT BUDGET

Target first-load font transfer:

≤ 120–150 KB total

Prefer lower.

No 12 weights.

No separate desktop/mobile font families.


## 12.38 — FONT PRELOAD

Preload only the primary above-fold font resource where justified.

Over-preloading blocks useful resources.


## 12.39 — FONT FALLBACK

System fallback tuned to reduce layout shift.

Use appropriate:

font-display size-adjust ascent/descent overrides

where testing justifies.

Do not let final font arrival cause hero line breaks to change violently.


## 12.40 — THIRD-PARTY SCRIPTS

Default:


### ZERO BEFORE CONSENT/INTERACTION EXCEPT STRICTLY ESSENTIAL INFRASTRUCTURE.

No:

- Facebook pixel;
- Hotjar;
- random review widget;

- live chat;
- YouTube iframe;
- Google Maps;
- GA4;

before their privacy/loading conditions are satisfied.


## 12.41 — PERFORMANCE = PRIVACY

Blocking unnecessary third parties improves:

- speed;
- INP;
- privacy;
- security;
- CSP simplicity.

This is why Parts 12 and 13 are one architecture.


## 12.42 — PREFETCH

Astro provides opt-in MPA prefetching for internal links.

Do **not** prefetch the entire site.

Recommended candidates:

- Trouver mon club
- selected discipline destination
- Première séance

High-confidence next actions only.


## 12.43 — PREFETCH AND DATA

Do not waste mobile data prefetching five possible club paths.

A route becomes more predictable as the user resolves their intent.

This gives us a unique optimization:


### PREFETCH CAN FOLLOW THE RESOLVING ROUTE.

Before intent:

little/no prefetch.

After MMA selected:

prefetch `/mma/` or relevant decision route.

After recommendation:

prefetch the next internal route.

Technical performance follows cognition.


## 12.44 — BROWSER CACHE

Fingerprinted static assets:

Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable

where deployment permits.

HTML:

short/shared caching with revalidation strategy appropriate to the host.


## 12.45 — SCHEDULE FRESHNESS VS CACHE

Schedule data should not be cached for a year merely because static assets are.

If built into HTML:

new build invalidates it.

Freshness label continues to show verification date.

If future live endpoint:

appropriate short cache/stale-revalidate.


## 12.46 — COMPRESSION

Host should serve:

- Brotli where available;
- gzip fallback.

Text resources compressed.

Already compressed images are not pointlessly recompressed at transport layer.


## 12.47 — CDN

Static assets/HTML should be edge/CDN served when hosting permits.

User in Colomiers should not wait for an origin server unnecessarily far away.

No vendor choice is locked yet.


## 12.48 — HERO ROUTE GRAPH

SVG first.

Do not make an 800KB WebGL bundle the site's identity.

If later prototype demonstrates true WebGL need:

advanced bundle loads after core content.


## 12.49 — RENDER LOOP

Part 06 remains:

one scheduler if needed.

No idle continuous animation.

Offscreen/hidden tab:

sleep.


## 12.50 — MAIN THREAD

Avoid:

- huge JSON hydration payload;
- runtime Markdown parser;
- large animation library;
- 50 IntersectionObservers where one architecture works;
- DOM measurement every scroll frame.


## 12.51 — INP

Official good:

`≤200ms`.

Internal:


### ≤150ms

for common interactions.

Selector selection must feel immediate.

Recommendation calculation should itself be effectively instantaneous because its rule set is local/deterministic.


## 12.52 — PROXIMITY ENGINE DATA

Serialize only facts required by matching.

Do not hydrate the entire corporate claim registry into the browser.

Create a minimized public projection:

publicClubMatchData

containing:

- allowed discipline IDs;
- audience;
- schedule compatibility data;
- display explanation IDs.

No private metadata.


## 12.53 — CLIENT STATE SIZE

Tiny.

No Redux.

No global state framework.

Potential:

sessionStorage + URL state + small local store

The site's product problem does not justify an application platform.


## 12.54 — SECURITY ARCHITECTURE

Security is part of Baffled Bar.

A visually elite website that leaks form data or permits basic XSS is not elite.


## 12.55 — CSP

Use a Content Security Policy.

Preferred delivery:

HTTP header where host allows.

OWASP recommends CSP as a significant defence against XSS/data injection and recommends header delivery.

Astro v6 has promoted its CSP support from experimental into stable configuration.

Implementation should use the stable version available at build time.


## 12.56 — CSP PHILOSOPHY

Aim toward:

default-src 'self' base-uri 'self' object-src 'none' frame-ancestors 'none' form-action 'self'

plus explicit:

- image origins;
- fonts;
- connections;
- consented analytics origins.

No:

script-src *

No:

unsafe-eval

without extraordinary justification.

Avoid `unsafe-inline` scripts.


## 12.57 — CSP ROLLOUT

First production-like environment:

Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only

inspect legitimate violations.

Then enforce.

Do not deploy a guessed CSP that breaks forms/analytics on launch.


## 12.58 — SECURITY HEADERS

Baseline to evaluate/deploy:

X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin Permissions-Policy: camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=() X-Frame-Options: DENY

with CSP `frame-ancestors` as modern frame control.

OWASP currently recommends these classes of controls.


## 12.59 — GEOLOCATION PERMISSION

The site does not need browser geolocation at launch.

Our starting context is:

Colomiers.

Therefore:

```text
geolocation=()
```

is appropriate.

If real user-origin routing is added in future:

this security/privacy decision must be revisited explicitly.


## 12.60 — HSTS

Deploy HSTS **carefully**.

Initial production validation:

shorter `max-age`.

Only after:

- HTTPS stable;
- all intended subdomains HTTPS;
- certificate automation reliable;

increase duration/include subdomains.

OWASP warns that an incorrectly long HSTS policy can make legitimate access difficult when HTTPS infrastructure fails.

Do not jump to preload blindly.


## 12.61 — SECRETS

Never commit:

- mail API key;
- analytics secret;
- IndexNow secret;
- deployment token.

Use environment secrets.

`.env.example` contains names.

Not values.


## 12.62 — DEPENDENCY POLICY

Every package answers:

What exact problem does this solve better than the platform?

No dependency for:

- class names;
- one disclosure;
- one animation;
- one debounce;
- one SVG line.

Small dependency graph means:

- faster;
- safer;
- easier upgrades;
- lower implementation drift.


## 12.63 — DEPENDENCY SECURITY

Lockfile committed.

Automated vulnerability update workflow.

Major dependency upgrades reviewed, not auto-deployed blind.

Astro/framework version pinned within an intentional update strategy.


## 12.64 — FAILURE MODE: JAVASCRIPT

JS fails:

site still works as informational orientation system.


## 12.65 — FAILURE MODE: PROXIMITY ENGINE

If engine errors:

show:

On n'a pas pu calculer la recommandation automatiquement.

Then:

**Comparer Minimes et Portet**

Manual decision survives.


## 12.66 — FAILURE MODE: SCHEDULE

If dynamic schedule data later fails:

show last safely known snapshot **only when clearly dated**.

Or:

Les données actuelles ne répondent pas.

Then:

**Consulter le planning officiel ↗**

Never pretend stale information is live.


## 12.67 — FAILURE MODE: IMAGE

Alt/caption and layout survive.

No giant blank zero-height space.


## 12.68 — FAILURE MODE: ANALYTICS

Nothing changes for visitor.

Analytics is observation.

Not infrastructure.


## 12.69 — FAILURE MODE: COOKIE CMP

If consent UI fails:

non-essential tracking defaults to **off**.

Privacy fails closed.

Website remains usable.


## 12.70 — FAILURE MODE: MAIL

Do not display success.

Show:

La demande n'a pas pu être envoyée.

Provide:

- phone;
- email;
- retry.

The visitor still has a route.


## 12.71 — OBSERVABILITY

Monitor:

5xx rate contact API errors mail delivery errors 404 volume CSP violations Core Web Vitals frontend JS errors

No full user-session recording required.


## 12.72 — ERROR MONITORING

If third-party error-monitoring is used:

- privacy reviewed;
- no form contents;
- no sensitive breadcrumbs;
- consent/legal basis evaluated;
- sampled appropriately.

Do not send `message` form content in an exception object.


## 12.73 — PERFORMANCE CI

Build should report/fail on:

JS budget regression CSS budget regression unexpected giant image font budget broken optimized asset

Large regression requires explicit review.


## 12.74 — LIGHTHOUSE

Useful as lab smoke test.

Not final truth.

Run mobile-like profiles for:

- home;
- MMA;
- /quel-club/;
- planning;
- contact.

Do not optimize one homepage score and ignore product pages.


## 12.75 — REAL DEVICE TEST

At minimum:

- mid-range Android;
- iPhone/Safari;
- ordinary Windows laptop;
- throttled network;
- low-power CPU profile.

Designer MacBook Pro is not the baseline.


## 12.76 — PERFORMANCE HATER TEST

Disable the interactive graphics.

Does design still feel complete?

Throttle network.

Can I read H1 immediately?

Slow CPU 4×.

Can I select MMA immediately?

Block analytics.

Does anything break?

Deny cookies.

Does navigation stay perfect?

Yes to all.

Otherwise fail.


## 12.77 — PERFORMANCE +1


### THE RESOLVING ROUTE CONTROLS RESOURCE PRIORITY.

As user certainty increases:

the browser may increasingly predict the next resource.

UNKNOWN → minimal speculation MMA SELECTED → MMA route becomes likely PORTET RECOMMENDED → first-session/planning route becomes likely

Our information architecture can inform:


### predictive-but-respectful prefetching.

The design concept becomes a network optimization mechanism.

That is a genuine +1.


<a id="part-13-analytics-measurement-privacy-conversion-intelligence"></a>

# PART 13 — Analytics, Measurement, Privacy & Conversion Intelligence


## 13.0 — ANALYTICS THESIS

Analytics must answer:

**Did the website help someone make a better decision?**

Not:

How much can we watch this person?

Therefore:


### MEASURE DECISIONS, NOT PEOPLE.

We want enough information to improve:

- acquisition;
- content;
- selector;
- recommendations;
- schedules;
- handoffs;
- lead flow.

We do not need behavioral surveillance.


## 13.1 — PRIVACY FIRST ARCHITECTURE

Three measurement layers.

LAYER 1 ESSENTIAL OPERATIONAL MEASUREMENT server/CDN aggregates errors performance security ↓ LAYER 2 PRIVACY-PRESERVING AUDIENCE MEASUREMENT only if configured to meet applicable CNIL exemption conditions ↓ LAYER 3 CONSENTED MARKETING/ATTRIBUTION ANALYTICS GA4 / Ads / other marketing tags only after appropriate consent

These layers must not be confused.


## 13.2 — CNIL MEASUREMENT EXEMPTION

The CNIL currently says some audience-measurement trackers **may** be exempt from consent when strict conditions are satisfied, including that measurement is limited to the publisher's own audience measurement and generates anonymous statistics without cross-site tracking or non-anonymous data sharing.

Crucial:


### AN ANALYTICS PRODUCT IS NOT AUTOMATICALLY EXEMPT BECAUSE ITS MARKETING SAYS “PRIVACY FRIENDLY.”

Configuration and actual data flows matter.

The publisher remains responsible for verifying the conditions.


## 13.3 — LAUNCH RECOMMENDATION

Recommended:


### Layer 1

First-party server/CDN operational analytics.

Always minimal.


### Layer 2

Optional privacy-preserving audience statistics only if the chosen setup has been reviewed against current CNIL exemption conditions.


### Layer 3


### GA4 CONSENT-GATED

if Boxing Center wants:

- marketing attribution;
- campaign reporting;
- Google Ads integration;
- richer behavioral funnels.

This is the safest clean separation.


## 13.4 — GA4 CONSENT MODE

Google currently supports:


### Basic Consent Mode

Google tags are blocked until consent.

No data is sent to Google before the visitor interacts with the consent mechanism.


### Advanced Consent Mode

tags load with denied defaults and may send cookieless pings when consent is denied.

Our default recommendation for launch:


### BASIC CONSENT MODE

because it produces the clearest privacy boundary.

No Google analytics call before consent.


## 13.5 — ADVANCED CONSENT MODE

Not forbidden forever.

But adopting it is:


### A SEPARATE PRIVACY/LEGAL/BUSINESS DECISION.

Do not enable it because:

better modeling.

If paid media requirements later justify it:

evaluate then.


## 13.6 — COOKIE BANNER

If consent-required tags exist, first level:

MESURE D'AUDIENCE / MARKETING [TOUT REFUSER] [TOUT ACCEPTER] [PERSONNALISER]

Accept and reject must be comparably easy.

The CNIL explicitly states refusal must be as easy as acceptance and currently recommends same-level controls.


## 13.7 — NO COOKIE DARK PATTERN

Forbidden:

Big red:

TOUT ACCEPTER

Tiny gray link:

continuer sans accepter.

The CNIL has specifically acted against deceptive banners that visually or procedurally privilege acceptance.

Our banner obeys the same:


### PRECISION UNDER PRESSURE

design system.

Clear choice.

No manipulation.


## 13.8 — CONSENT WITHDRAWAL

Footer always exposes:


### GÉRER MES COOKIES

Withdrawing consent must be straightforward.

The CNIL says withdrawing consent must be as simple as giving it.


## 13.9 — CONSENT PERSISTENCE

The CNIL currently describes around **six months** as generally appropriate for preserving a user's consent/refusal choice.

Use as launch default unless legal/privacy review sets a different justified policy.

Do not re-show the banner every session after refusal.


## 13.10 — CONSENT DENIAL EXPERIENCE

After refusal:


### THE FULL WEBSITE WORKS.

No:

- blocked selector;
- missing schedules;
- disabled contact;
- nag overlay;
- degraded speed;
- repeated prompts.

The consequence is only:

reduced tracking.


## 13.11 — CONSENT CATEGORIES

Keep minimal.

Potential:


### NÉCESSAIRES

No opt-out where genuinely required.


### MESURE D'AUDIENCE

For consent-required analytics.


### MARKETING

Only if actual ad/remarketing technology exists.

Do not create six categories because a CMP template has six.


## 13.12 — NO MARKETING CATEGORY IF NO MARKETING TAG EXISTS

Empty cookie-control theatre is bad UX.

Categories describe **actual technologies**.


## 13.13 — FIRST-PARTY OPERATIONAL MEASUREMENT

We can collect aggregate operational information necessary to run the website, subject to appropriate privacy/legal controls:

- request counts;
- response status;
- page route;
- performance;
- bot traffic;
- errors.

Do not build visitor profiles from it.


## 13.14 — IP ADDRESSES

Server/CDN infrastructure naturally may process IP addresses.

We do not:

- copy them into analytics dimensions;
- expose them in dashboards;
- retain them indefinitely;
- combine them with form data for marketing.

Logging and retention should be minimized according to operational/security need.


## 13.15 — SELECTOR STATE PRIVACY

Intent:

mma beginner evening

does not need server persistence.

Use:

URL low-risk state or sessionStorage

No cookie profile:

this user is an MMA beginner.

No persistent personal segmentation.


## 13.16 — CONTACT FORM DATA

The website collects only what the business needs to respond.

The formal brief currently requests:

- first/last name;
- phone;
- email;
- activity;
- message;
- club/zone.

Our UX already minimizes required status where operationally possible.


## 13.17 — FORM DATA MUST NEVER ENTER ANALYTICS

Google explicitly prohibits sending personally identifiable information such as personal email addresses and mobile phone numbers into Analytics.

Therefore analytics NEVER receives:

first_name last_name phone email message

Not as:

- event values;
- URL parameters;
- page titles;
- custom dimensions;
- error logs.


## 13.18 — FORM QUERY STRINGS

Never redirect to:

/contact/merci?email=user@example.com

PII cannot appear in URLs.

Google Analytics itself warns URLs/titles are commonly accidental PII leakage points.


## 13.19 — FORM EVENT

Analytics receives only:

contact_submitted discipline=mma club_context=recommend

No personal data.


## 13.20 — EVENT PHILOSOPHY

Events describe:


### A BUSINESS-MEANINGFUL DECISION.

Not DOM implementation.

Good:

intent_selected recommendation_generated club_destination_clicked

Bad:

button_red_click card_hover section_6_visible


## 13.21 — EVENT TAXONOMY

Locked initial custom events:

intent_selected recommendation_generated recommendation_changed club_destination_clicked

schedule_filter_applied schedule_source_clicked price_destination_clicked first_session_started phone_clicked email_clicked contact_started contact_submitted contact_failed

Normal page views come from the analytics platform's standard page-view model.


## 13.22 — EVENT NAMING

Use:

lowercase_snake_case

Stable English/internal taxonomy.

Why English internally?

Consistency with analytics tooling and code.

Public interface remains French.

GA4 currently requires event names to start with a letter, use letters/numbers/underscores, and stay within its naming constraints; event names are case-sensitive.


## 13.23 — DON'T COLLIDE WITH RESERVED GA EVENTS

GA4 already reserves/automatically uses names such as:

page_view scroll click form_start form_submit

so our custom taxonomy should not misuse them.

If GA's built-in `form_start`/`form_submit` measurement is enabled, ensure it doesn't conflict with our own preferred clean form instrumentation.


## 13.24 — RECOMMENDATION: DISABLE UNNEEDED ENHANCED MEASUREMENT

Do not enable every GA4 automatic feature blindly.

Review individually.

Particularly:

automatic form interaction tracking may create duplicate/less controlled events if we already implement our own.

Google advises understanding what each Enhanced Measurement option collects before enabling it.


## 13.25 — EVENT PARAMETERS

Allowed low-cardinality parameters:

page_type primary_intent intent discipline audience level club_id recommendation_type day time_window cta_context origin_route destination_type

Not every event needs every parameter.


## 13.26 — NO HIGH-CARDINALITY DUMP

Avoid custom dimensions such as:

- complete URL;
- timestamp;
- session ID;
- free-text message;
- unique recommendation hash.

GA4 warns high-cardinality dimensions degrade reporting and recommends predefined dimensions where possible.


## 13.27 — PARAMETER LIMIT

Keep events tiny.

GA4 currently allows up to 25 parameters per event and event names under 40 characters.

Our design target:


### ≤ 8 CUSTOM PARAMETERS PER EVENT

usually fewer.


## 13.28 — EVENT SCHEMA EXAMPLE

track('recommendation_generated', { discipline: 'mma', club_id: 'portet', level: 'beginner', time_window: 'evening', recommendation_type: 'single_match' });

Good.


## 13.29 — BAD EVENT

track('recommendation_generated', { email: 'eddy@...', phone: '...', message: '...', timestamp: '...', session_uuid: '...', visitor_fingerprint: '...' });

Forbidden.


## 13.30 — THE PRIMARY KPI

Part 01 locked:


### QUALIFIED DESTINATION RATE

Now formalize it.

A **decision-engaged session** is a session where the visitor performs at least one explicit decision action:

intent_selected recommendation_generated schedule_filter_applied

Potentially expanded later based on evidence.

A **qualified destination action** is:

club_destination_clicked OR contact_submitted OR phone_clicked

when it follows a relevant content/decision context.

Formula:

Qualified Destination Rate = decision-engaged sessions with ≥1 qualified destination action ÷ all decision-engaged sessions

This measures whether guidance actually turns into a real next step.


## 13.31 — WHY NOT RAW CONVERSION RATE ONLY?

Homepage-to-contact conversion ignores the site's job.

Someone may:

1. search MMA; 2. discover Portet; 3. click Portet; 4. register there.

Colomiers did its job brilliantly even if no Colomiers form was submitted.

Therefore outbound qualified destination matters.


## 13.32 — KPI TREE


### ACQUISITION

- organic sessions;
- priority-query impressions;
- SERP CTR;
- AI-search visibility/referrals;
- campaign sessions.


### UNDERSTANDING

- discipline route engagement;
- intent selection rate;
- first-session page use.


### DECISION

- recommendation completion;
- schedule filtering;
- comparison engagement.


### ACTION

- destination clicks;
- schedule-source clicks;
- phone clicks;
- contact submissions.


### QUALITY

- qualified destination rate;
- form failure rate;
- no-match rate;
- recommendation-change rate.


## 13.33 — PROXIMITY ENGINE ANALYTICS

The engine needs a product funnel.

engine_viewed ↓ intent_selected ↓ criteria_completed ↓ recommendation_generated ↓ club_destination_clicked

Do we need all these custom events?

Not necessarily at launch.

We can derive `engine_viewed` from page/visibility only if it becomes useful.


### DO NOT MEASURE STEPS WE WILL NOT USE.

Minimum useful set first.


## 13.34 — NO-MATCH RATE

Important product metric:

```text
recommendation_type =
no_exact_match
```

If high:

possible causes:

- programmes insufficient;
- criteria too restrictive;
- data stale;
- UX asks wrong questions.

This is far more actionable than “time on page.”


## 13.35 — RECOMMENDATION CHANGE RATE

If many users receive Portet then immediately switch to Minimes:

ask why.

Possibilities:

- explanation weak;
- result feels wrong;
- geography matters but engine doesn't model it;
- visitor had prior knowledge.

This becomes a product insight.


## 13.36 — SCHEDULE ZERO-RESULT RATE

Track aggregated:

discipline day time_window

when filter yields zero.

No person identifier needed.

This may reveal unmet demand:

Many MMA users from Colomiers want Saturday morning.

That is actual business intelligence.


## 13.37 — CONTACT FAILURE RATE

Track:

contact_failed reason_category = validation delivery network rate_limit

Never raw error message if it could contain form data.


## 13.38 — PHONE CLICKS

A mobile phone click is meaningful.

phone_clicked

But do not treat it automatically as confirmed sale.

It's a lead action.


## 13.39 — EXTERNAL DESTINATION ATTRIBUTION

When Colomiers sends a visitor to a Boxing Center site:

append controlled campaign parameters where appropriate.

Example:

?utm_source=boxingcenter-colomiers &utm_medium=referral &utm_campaign=orientation_colomiers &utm_content=mma

Parameter vocabulary must be canonicalized.

No free-text user-specific data.


## 13.40 — UTM SOURCE

Recommended consistent source:

boxingcenter-colomiers

Not alternating:

colomiers bc-colomiers boxing_colomiers website-colomiers

Data cleanliness matters.


## 13.41 — UTM MEDIUM

For sister-site handoff:

referral

unless broader network analytics adopts a different locked taxonomy.


## 13.42 — UTM CONTENT

Low-cardinality intent:

mma boxe enfants planning tarifs

No user identifier.


## 13.43 — CROSS-DOMAIN GA4

Possible future option if all Boxing Center sites use:

- coordinated GA4 property;
- coordinated consent;
- same privacy policy/data controller logic;
- correctly configured cross-domain measurement.

But not required for launch.

Google supports cross-domain measurement and referral handling, but it introduces a network-level governance dependency.

Launch recommendation:


### UTM HANDOFF FIRST.

Simpler.

More transparent.


## 13.44 — BOUTIQUE CONVERSION

If Boutique can preserve UTMs:

we can measure:

Colomiers referral → boutique → offer conversion

on the Boutique side.

Do not insert personal tracking IDs to make this possible.

Campaign attribution is enough initially.


## 13.45 — CHATGPT ATTRIBUTION

Part 10 already noted OpenAI Search referrals can expose:

utm_source=chatgpt.com

Use that in acquisition reporting.

No custom tracking integration with ChatGPT needed.


## 13.46 — SEARCH CONSOLE DATA

Report:

- queries;
- pages;
- countries/devices;
- traditional search performance;
- generative search performance where available.

Search Console data does not need the website's cookie consent because the measurement occurs in Google's search ecosystem rather than through a tracker installed on the site.


## 13.47 — BING DATA

Report separately:

- indexed URLs;
- queries/clicks where available;
- crawl issues;
- IndexNow activity.

No need to funnel everything into GA.


## 13.48 — ANALYTICS DASHBOARD

Recommended high-level dashboard:

01 ACQUISITION organic / AI / direct / campaign 02 INTENT boxe / MMA / fitness / child 03 DECISION selector / recommendation / planning 04 DESTINATION Minimes / Portet / contact / phone 05 QUALITY no-match / form failures / performance 06 SEARCH Google + Bing + AI visibility

One screen should answer:

Is the Colomiers orientation product working?


## 13.49 — NO VANITY DASHBOARD

Do not lead with:

- bounce rate;
- average session duration;
- total page views.

They may exist.

They are not strategy.

A visitor could spend 17 seconds, get the answer, click Portet, and represent a perfect session.


## 13.50 — SCROLL DEPTH

Not a primary KPI.

Use only when a specific content hypothesis needs it.

Do not measure:

25% 50% 75% 90%

on every page because GTM templates make it easy.


## 13.51 — HOVER ANALYTICS

Never.

Hover behavior is visual interaction, not business intent.

No event noise.


## 13.52 — SESSION REPLAY


### NO SESSION REPLAY AT LAUNCH.
