No Hotjar-style replay simply because we want UX insights.

Reasons:

- privacy;
- script cost;

- consent complexity;
- accidental form capture risk;
- enormous data for a small local site.

If later usability research requires it:

conduct a separate controlled, consented study.


## 13.53 — HEATMAPS

Same.

Not installed by default.

Direct usability testing is often higher quality.


## 13.54 — FINGERPRINTING

Forbidden.

No need.


## 13.55 — USER ID

No GA User-ID system.

There is no account.

No reason to manufacture persistent identity.


## 13.56 — PRECISE GEOLOCATION

No.

We already know the site context:

Colomiers.

We do not need browser GPS for analytics or selection at launch.


## 13.57 — FORM RETENTION

Website architecture should avoid creating its own long-lived lead database at launch if email delivery is sufficient.

This minimizes the web application's stored PII.

Email/business-system retention is an organisational privacy-policy issue and must be explicitly set by the data controller.

Do not let the implementation LLM invent a retention period.


## 13.58 — ANALYTICS RETENTION

For GA4, standard properties currently support 2- or 14-month user/event retention configurations for relevant retained data.

Recommended default:


### 2 MONTHS

unless Boxing Center can articulate a real need for 14-month user-level exploratory retention.

Long-term strategy reporting should rely primarily on aggregated reporting.

More retention is not automatically better intelligence.


## 13.59 — CONSENT RECORD

Consent/refusal preference:

approximately 6 months as launch default, consistent with current CNIL guidance.

Store:

- categories;
- policy/version;
- timestamp.

Not a profile.


## 13.60 — SESSIONSTORAGE

Selector preference disappears when the browsing session ends.

Good.

No need to remember for six months:

User prefers MMA.


## 13.61 — PRIVACY POLICY DATA MAP

The privacy documentation should explicitly describe:

CONTACT FORM what why recipient retention rights ANALYTICS provider purpose

consent basis/status data categories retention SERVER LOGS purpose security/operations retention COOKIE PREFERENCE purpose duration

No generic copy pasted from another website.


## 13.62 — COOKIE TABLE

Where cookies/local storage exist:

document:

NAME PROVIDER PURPOSE TYPE DURATION CONSENT CATEGORY

And keep the documentation synchronized with actual site technology.


## 13.63 — COOKIE SCANNER QA

Before launch and periodically:

scan production.

Compare discovered:

- cookies;
- localStorage;
- network calls;

to privacy declarations.

Unknown third party:

release issue.


## 13.64 — TAG MANAGER

Do we need GTM?

Not automatically.

If only GA4 exists:

direct Google tag may be simpler.

If later:

- Ads;
- multiple marketing tools;
- complex consent;

GTM becomes useful.

Google Tag Manager provides consent-initialization mechanisms and consent checks, but adding GTM is still an architectural decision, not a requirement.


## 13.65 — NO TAG MANAGER “WILD WEST”

If GTM is used:

production container changes require governance.

No marketer adds:

- Hotjar;
- Meta Pixel;
- random conversion script

without privacy/performance review.

Tag Manager can bypass the whole site's engineering discipline if uncontrolled.


## 13.66 — CSP + ANALYTICS

Analytics domains are explicitly allowed only after chosen stack is known.

Do not weaken CSP globally:

connect-src * script-src https:

to make tracking easy.

Tracking adapts to security.

Not reverse.


## 13.67 — CONSENT + PERFORMANCE

GA bundle doesn't download before consent under Basic Consent Mode.

Thus refusing analytics also produces:

- fewer requests;
- lower JS;
- better privacy.

The site does not punish rejection.

It gets slightly lighter.

Correct.


## 13.68 — ANALYTICS FAILS SILENTLY

If analytics request fails:

no UI error.

No retry loop blocking main thread.

No user impact.


## 13.69 — ANALYTICS DEVELOPMENT MODE

Development/staging:

no production analytics.

Use debug logging/local stub.

Do not pollute GA4 with:

localhost preview qa

traffic.


## 13.70 — PRODUCTION BOT FILTERING

Analytics reporting should exclude obvious internal/test/bot activity where tools support it, while server logs retain appropriate operational evidence.

No fake performance improvements by deleting legitimate users.


## 13.71 — INTERNAL STAFF TRAFFIC

If meaningful traffic volume later warrants:

filter staff/test traffic using maintainable methods.

Do not use invasive employee fingerprinting.


## 13.72 — ANALYTICS QA

Before launch verify:


### Consent denied

No Google Analytics requests.


### Consent accepted

expected tag loads once.


### Consent withdrawn

future tracking stops according to configuration.


### Form submit

no PII in event.


### Outbound club link

correct low-cardinality event/UTM.


### Selector

event only once per actual decision.


### Rapid choice changes

no event explosion.


### Staging

zero production analytics.


## 13.73 — NETWORK INSPECTION QA

Use browser Network panel.

Search for:

email phone message name

in analytics requests.

Any personal form content leaving to Analytics:


### CRITICAL FAILURE.


## 13.74 — ANALYTICS SCHEMA TESTS

Type event contracts in code.

Example:

```text
type AnalyticsEvent =
| {
name: 'intent_selected';
params: {
intent: IntentId;
origin_route: RouteId;
};
```

} | { name: 'club_destination_clicked'; params: { club_id: ClubId; discipline?: DisciplineId; origin_route: RouteId; }; };

This prevents random analytics strings from spreading through components.


## 13.75 — COMPONENTS EMIT INTENT, ANALYTICS ADAPTER TRANSLATES

Component:

emit('club-destination-selected', {...})

Analytics adapter decides:

- whether consent exists;
- which provider;
- exact event syntax.

UI components do not import GA APIs directly.

This is important.


## 13.76 — ANALYTICS PROVIDER ABSTRACTION

Conceptually:

```text
interface AnalyticsAdapter {
track(event: AnalyticsEvent): void;
}
```

Implementations:

NoopAnalytics GA4Analytics PrivacyAnalytics

Consent swaps availability.

The product logic does not care.


## 13.77 — PRIVACY FAIL-CLOSED

If consent state unknown:

NoopAnalytics

Not:

assume yes.


## 13.78 — REPORTING CADENCE

Early launch:

weekly operational review.

Then:

monthly strategic review.

Review:

- search query movement;
- no-match patterns;
- destination mix;
- contact quality;
- schedule-zero results;
- AI referrals.

Do not redesign daily.


## 13.79 — EXPERIMENTATION

No random A/B testing at launch.

First:

establish baseline.

If later testing:

hypothesis-driven.

Example:

Users do not understand why Portet is recommended.

Test:

visible explanation wording.

Not:

blue button vs red button because optimization.


## 13.80 — EXPERIMENT PRIVACY

Any experimentation tool is subject to:

- consent requirements;
- performance budget;

- privacy review.

Server-side deterministic test allocation may be preferable if true experimentation becomes necessary.


## 13.81 — ANALYTICS +1

Traditional analytics asks:

What did users click?

Our system asks:


### WHERE DID CERTAINTY BREAK?

Examples:

many intent selections few recommendations → criteria flow problem many recommendations many changes → trust/matching problem many schedule filters many zero results → programme/time mismatch many club clicks few downstream conversions → destination site problem

Analytics follows the **decision graph**.

That is materially more useful than funnel screenshots.


## 13.82 — PRIVACY +1


### THE LESS CERTAIN THE BUSINESS NEED, THE LESS DATA WE COLLECT.

We do not collect information:

just in case it becomes useful.

Each event/field answers:

Which decision will this improve?

No answer:

delete it.

The data architecture mirrors Red Is Earned:


### DATA IS EARNED TOO.


## 13.83 — PARTS 11–13 SYSTEM COLLISION

These three systems reinforce one another.

Example:

Cookie consent denied.


### Accessibility

site still fully operable.


### Performance

fewer scripts.


### Privacy

choice respected.


### Analytics

Noop adapter.


### UX

no penalty.

Another:

Proximity Engine recommendation.


### Accessibility

semantic textual result.


### Performance

deterministic local rules.


### Analytics

low-cardinality result event.


### Privacy

no identity required.


### GEO

verified claim source.


### Design

red route resolves.

One architecture.


## 13.84 — “NO THIRD-PARTY REQUIRED FOR CORE JOB” RULE

A visitor must be able to:

understand choose discipline compare clubs see core schedule information understand first session contact Boxing Center

without:

- Google Analytics;
- Google Maps;
- Facebook;
- YouTube;
- Hotjar;
- chat widget.

Third parties enhance.

They do not own the experience.


## 13.85 — COMBINED RELEASE GATE


### ACCESSIBILITY

WCAG 2.2 AA critical checks pass.

Keyboard journey complete.

Reduced motion designed.

320px/reflow works.

Major AT smoke tests pass.


### PERFORMANCE

LCP/INP/CLS budgets pass lab gate.

No large bundle regression.

Responsive images correct.

No unnecessary hydration.


### SECURITY

CSP tested.

CSRF protection active.

Secrets server-only.

Rate limiting/spam control active.


### PRIVACY

Non-essential trackers do not load before required consent.

Reject is as easy as accept.

Privacy documentation matches network behavior.

No PII in analytics.


### ANALYTICS

Events use semantic contract.

No duplicate events.

No staging traffic.

UTMs consistent.

Primary KPI computable.


### RESILIENCE

JS off works.

Analytics blocked works.

Mail failure has alternative.

Schedule failure has authoritative fallback.

Cookie denial changes no product function.


## 13.86 — LOCKED PART 11 DOCTRINES


### WCAG 2.2 AA IS THE FLOOR

Not aspiration.


### FOCUS IS PART OF ART DIRECTION

Not debug styling.


### NO POINTER-ONLY FUNCTION

Ever.


### MOBILE + 400% ZOOM ARE REAL SURFACES

Not edge cases.


### ROUTE GRAPH IS NEVER THE ONLY MEANING

Semantic decision always exists.


### ACCESSIBLE EXPERIENCE IS EQUALLY BRANDED

No second-class fallback.


## 13.87 — LOCKED PART 12 DOCTRINES


### STATIC FIRST

Astro HTML by default.


### HYDRATION IS EARNED

Only stateful product systems receive JS.


### CORE WEB VITALS HAVE INTERNAL BUDGETS

We aim beyond the good threshold.


### RESOURCE PRIORITY FOLLOWS USER CERTAINTY

Resolving Route can guide prefetch.


### FAILURE DEGRADES CAPABILITY, NOT MEANING

The site still solves the problem.


### SECURITY IS DESIGN QUALITY

No compromise.


## 13.88 — LOCKED PART 13 DOCTRINES


### MEASURE DECISIONS, NOT PEOPLE

The primary analytics law.


### DATA IS EARNED

No speculative collection.


### GA4 IS CONSENT-GATED AT LAUNCH

Basic Consent Mode recommended.


### NO SESSION REPLAY / FINGERPRINTING / USER ID

Not needed.


### PII NEVER ENTERS ANALYTICS

Absolute.


### QUALIFIED DESTINATION RATE IS PRIMARY

Because successful handoff is the site's job.


### ANALYTICS FINDS WHERE CERTAINTY BROKE

Not merely what got clicked.


## 13.89 — PARTS 11–13 IN ONE SENTENCE

**Boxing Center Colomiers must remain fast, usable and truthful regardless of** **device, motor ability, visual ability, motion preference, JavaScript availability** **or tracking consent; it should collect only the data necessary to understand** **whether that inclusive experience successfully turned uncertainty into a** **confident real-world Boxing Center destination.**

These three systems are now locked.


<a id="part-14-qa-testing-launch-maintenance-system"></a>

# PART 14 — QA, Testing, Launch & Maintenance System


## 14.0 — PURPOSE

A project at this level cannot define “done” as:

the pages look correct on my laptop.

The release definition is:

**every important claim, route, interaction, visual state, browser state,** **accessibility mode, crawler surface, consent state and failure mode has been** **tested against an explicit expected result.**

Part 14 converts everything established in Parts 01–13 into a release machine.

The governing principle is:


### NO CLAIM OF QUALITY WITHOUT EVIDENCE.

Not:

“It should work.”

But:

“Here is the test that proves this behavior.”


## 14.1 — RELEASE PHILOSOPHY

The deployment pipeline should progressively eliminate four classes of risk:

TRUTH FAILURE ↓ CODE FAILURE

↓ EXPERIENCE FAILURE ↓ PRODUCTION FAILURE

A beautiful page with false data fails before visual review matters.

A truthful page with inaccessible interaction fails.

A perfect staging build with production `noindex` fails.

A passing Lighthouse score with the wrong Portet schedule fails.

All layers matter.


## 14.2 — DEFINITION OF DONE

A page is not complete when:

- design is implemented;
- copy is inserted;
- animation works.

It is complete only when:

DATA VALIDATED + SEMANTIC HTML VALID + FUNCTIONAL TESTS PASS + ACCESSIBILITY PASS + VISUAL PASS + RESPONSIVE PASS + PERFORMANCE PASS + SEO/GEO PASS +

PRIVACY PASS + SECURITY PASS + CONTENT QA PASS + PRODUCTION SMOKE PASS

No individual discipline can unilaterally declare completion.


## 14.3 — THE RELEASE GATE STACK

There are **11 gates**.

GATE 00 — SOURCE TRUTH GATE 01 — STATIC VALIDATION GATE 02 — UNIT / DOMAIN LOGIC GATE 03 — FUNCTIONAL / E2E GATE 04 — ACCESSIBILITY GATE 05 — VISUAL / RESPONSIVE GATE 06 — PERFORMANCE GATE 07 — SEO / GEO / CRAWL GATE 08 — SECURITY / PRIVACY GATE 09 — CONTENT / EDITORIAL GATE 10 — PRODUCTION SMOKE

Failure at an earlier gate invalidates later approval where relevant.


### GATE 00 — SOURCE TRUTH


## 14.4 — WHY THIS COMES FIRST

The most dangerous failures on this project are not syntax errors.

They are:

- wrong club;
- wrong price;
- wrong schedule;
- wrong address;
- wrong age band;
- stale offer;
- fake Colomiers implication;
- wrong coach;
- wrong facility.

Therefore the first release test is the factual graph.


## 14.5 — CRITICAL CLAIM VALIDATION

Before production build:

all `public=true` critical claims must be:

verified OR time-sensitive but within review window

They may **not** be:

pending conflicted prohibited expired

unless the rendering intentionally omits that claim.


## 14.6 — CRITICAL BUILD BLOCKERS

At launch, unresolved values in any of these categories are blockers if the page attempts to display them:

physical address contact phone contact email opening hours discipline availability child age bands class schedule pricing offer validity equipment lending first-session contact policy coach qualification facility claim

No “temporary reasonable value.”


## 14.7 — THE NEGATIVE-CLAIM TEST

CI must explicitly assert:

```text
physicalClubInColomiers === false
```

Then scan generated output for forbidden implications.

Examples:

notre salle à Colomiers notre club de Colomiers Boxing Center situé à Colomiers adresse Boxing Center Colomiers

A prohibited phrase appearing in an indexable production page:


### CRITICAL FAILURE.

This makes the central truth architecture executable.


### GATE 01 — STATIC VALIDATION


## 14.8 — TYPE CHECK

No production build with TypeScript errors.

Use Astro's supported type/checking tooling as applicable to the pinned version.


## 14.9 — LINT

Static checks cover:

- JavaScript/TypeScript;
- Astro templates;
- CSS conventions where useful;
- accessibility mistakes detectable statically.

No warning graveyard.

If a rule is inappropriate:

configure it consciously.

Do not scatter:

// eslint-disable

everywhere.


## 14.10 — FORMAT

A single formatting configuration.

No formatting debates in implementation PRs.

Formatting is automated.


## 14.11 — GENERATED-OUTPUT GREP

Production build must contain zero occurrences of:

localhost 127.0.0.1 .vercel.app .netlify.app pages.dev TODO FIXME LOREM IPSUM [VERIFY] [SOURCE NEEDED] [CONFLICT] [DONNÉE DE TEST]

except where a legitimate external infrastructure hostname has been explicitly allowlisted.


## 14.12 — RAW PRICE SCAN

Search built public output for monetary amounts.

Every Boxing Center offer price must resolve to a known offer ID.

A raw unexplained price string is flagged.

This prevents:

tariff changed in component but not prose.


## 14.13 — RAW HOURS SCAN

Opening-hour strings should not be freehand repeated throughout Markdown.

They must originate through controlled fact components.


### GATE 02 — UNIT / DOMAIN LOGIC


## 14.14 — TEST FRAMEWORK

Use **Vitest** for pure TypeScript/domain logic.

Astro's current testing documentation explicitly supports Vitest for unit/integration work and Playwright for end-to-end browser testing.

Astro 6 also changed client-environment behavior for Astro component rendering: Astro components tested through Vitest should run in an appropriate server/Node environment rather than relying on old jsdom Container behavior.


## 14.15 — WHAT GETS UNIT-TESTED

Not buttons.

The important pure systems:

claim-state rules claim expiry club matching recommendation explanations URL registry canonical generation metadata generation robots policy schema builders sitemap filters analytics event validation UTM builder contact validation media provenance validation

public-data projection


## 14.16 — PROXIMITY ENGINE TEST MATRIX

Example:

INPUT MMA beginner evening DATA Portet MMA = verified Minimes MMA = unavailable EXPECTED Portet

Another:

INPUT English boxing DATA Minimes = verified Portet = verified EXPECTED two valid results or comparison required NOT arbitrary Portet/Minimes winner

Another:

INPUT MMA Saturday morning DATA no compatible verified schedule EXPECTED no_exact_match NOT forced recommendation


## 14.17 — CONFLICT TEST

If a matching criterion references a `conflicted` claim:

engine must not use it to generate certainty.

Expected:

criterion excluded OR recommendation downgraded

according to matching rules.

Never silently consume disputed facts.


## 14.18 — DETERMINISM

Same data + same criteria:

same result.

No randomization.

No LLM variability.

This makes the engine testable and trustworthy.


## 14.19 — METADATA TEST

Each indexable route:

has title has description has canonical canonical host correct one primary intent unique title unique description

Test uniqueness programmatically.


## 14.20 — SCHEMA BUILDER TEST

Given:

colomiers homepage

assert:

WebSite exists WebPage exists Organization exists where intended spatialCoverage = Colomiers LocalBusiness Colomiers DOES NOT EXIST


## 14.21 — OFFER EXPIRY TEST

Given:

validThrough < build date

expected:

offer not rendered/publicly exportable.

No expired campaign remains because marketing forgot a page.


### GATE 03 — FUNCTIONAL / E2E


## 14.22 — PLAYWRIGHT

Use Playwright as the canonical browser-level functional test runner.

Playwright currently supports Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, plus mobile device emulation through test projects.

This directly matches our cross-browser requirements.


## 14.23 — BROWSER PROJECTS

Recommended CI projects:

desktop-chromium desktop-firefox desktop-webkit mobile-chrome mobile-webkit

Optional periodic:

Edge stable Chrome stable channel


## 14.24 — CORE E2E JOURNEYS


### JOURNEY A — BOXE BEGINNER

Home → choose "Apprendre à boxer" → discipline context → compare club options → first-session content → physical club CTA


### JOURNEY B — MMA

/mma/ → understand no Colomiers location → club destination → schedule → Portet handoff if verified


### JOURNEY C — CHILD

/boxe-enfants/ → choose age → understand format → compatible location → child schedule → price


### JOURNEY D — PROXIMITY ENGINE

/quel-club/ → set criteria → recommendation → read reason

→ change criteria → new result


### JOURNEY E — NO MATCH

set unsupported combination → no exact match → alternative path offered


### JOURNEY F — CONTACT

/contact/ → select need → fill valid contact → submit → success

Provider delivery can be mocked in test environment.


### JOURNEY G — FORM FAILURE

mail provider failure → no false success → phone/email fallback visible


### JOURNEY H — 404

unknown URL → HTTP 404 → navigation works → no soft-404 redirect


## 14.25 — LINK TESTS

Every internal link in generated HTML resolves to:

200 or intentional redirect

No internal:

404 500 staging URL.


## 14.26 — EXTERNAL CLUB LINKS

Verify:

- canonical Minimes domain;
- canonical Portet domain;
- expected UTM format;
- no Vercel preview.

External site outage should generate warning rather than necessarily block every deploy, but stale or structurally incorrect URLs block release.


## 14.27 — BACK BUTTON TEST

Selector → route → Back:

browser returns predictably.

No broken state.

No accidental duplicate history records on every radio selection.


## 14.28 — REFRESH TEST

Refresh URL with valid query state:

```text
/quel-club/?discipline=mma
interface reconstructs meaningful state where intended.
```

Invalid parameters:

ignored safely.

No crash.


## 14.29 — RAPID INTERACTION TEST

Select:

MMA BOXE MMA BOXE

rapidly.

Expected:

last selection wins.

No stale animation/result.

This directly tests Part 06.


## 14.30 — NO JAVASCRIPT BROWSER TEST

At least one Playwright project/context disables JS.

Verify:

- nav;
- page content;
- physical destination links;
- tariff information;
- contact routes;
- manual club comparison;
- first session.

The Proximity Engine may degrade, but the site cannot become empty.


### GATE 04 — ACCESSIBILITY


## 14.31 — AXE IN PLAYWRIGHT

Playwright's current accessibility documentation explicitly supports `@axe-core/playwright`, while also stressing that automated tests detect only some accessibility failures and must be combined with manual assessment.

Use axe scans for:

homepage each page template family mobile menu open selector state recommendation state no-match state planning filter state form state form errors consent dialog


## 14.32 — AXE BLOCKING POLICY

`critical`:

block.

`serious`:

block unless documented false positive.

`moderate/minor`:

review.

Never suppress an axe rule merely to turn CI green.


## 14.33 — MANUAL KEYBOARD SCRIPT

Run manually before release:

open site Tab skip link nav disciplines disclosure Commencer selector recommendation planning form footer

At every step ask:

- where is focus?
- is it visible?
- can I activate?
- can I reverse?
- can I escape?


## 14.34 — SCREEN READER SCRIPT

Test representative flows rather than every decorative pixel.

NVDA/VoiceOver/TalkBack checks:

- page title announced;
- H1 logical;
- menu understandable;
- radio group understandable;
- recommendation final status announced once;
- comparison understandable;
- form errors associated;
- no decorative SVG chatter.


## 14.35 — ZOOM MATRIX

Manually test:

200% browser zoom 400% browser zoom 320px viewport 375px 390px 768px 1024px 1440px+

No release based solely on responsive DevTools presets.


## 14.36 — REDUCED MOTION TEST

Set OS/browser:

`prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`.

Verify:

- no route draw;
- no parallax;
- no large page transition;
- state changes immediate;
- identity remains complete.


## 14.37 — FORCED COLORS

Manual Windows forced-colors/high contrast pass.

Critical interactive state remains legible.


### GATE 05 — VISUAL & RESPONSIVE


## 14.38 — VISUAL REGRESSION

Playwright supports screenshot comparisons through `toHaveScreenshot()` and recommends stable execution environments because rendering varies across OS/browser conditions.

Therefore:

visual baselines are generated and compared in a **pinned CI environment**.

Not developer-to-developer local screenshots.


## 14.39 — VISUAL BASELINES

Capture at minimum:


### Home

desktop + mobile.


### Hero

default.


### Intent selector

unselected + selected.


### Proximity Engine

single result + two results + no match.


### MMA

desktop/mobile.


### Children

desktop/mobile.


### Planning

default/filter.


### Contact

default/errors/success.


### Menu

mobile open.


### 404.


## 14.40 — DO NOT MASK REAL CONTENT TO MAKE TESTS PASS

Dynamic timestamps may be normalized.

External video/iframe may be masked if unavoidable.

But we do not mask:

- headings;
- buttons;
- route graph;
- prices;
- club names;
- primary imagery.

Those are what visual regression is supposed to protect.


## 14.41 — SCREENSHOT REVIEW POLICY

A changed snapshot is not automatically accepted because:

--update-snapshots

made the tests green.

Snapshot updates receive visual review.

Playwright explicitly stores expected snapshots as reviewable baseline assets.


## 14.42 — HATER SCREENSHOT PASS

For each major viewport:

look for:

- generic card creep;
- weak hierarchy;
- too much red;
- unearned red;
- dead space;
- awkward French line break;
- image crop damage;

- misaligned route;
- accidental Colomiers-gym implication.

Automated pixel equality cannot judge those.


### GATE 06 — PERFORMANCE


## 14.43 — LIGHTHOUSE CI

Lighthouse CI is specifically designed to run Lighthouse continuously and can assert performance/resource budgets and catch regressions.

Use it as a lab regression gate.

Not field truth.


## 14.44 — LIGHTHOUSE ROUTES

Collect at least:

/ /mma/ /boxe-enfants/ /quel-club/ /plannings/ /contact/

These cover:

- immersive home;
- content-heavy page;
- parent page;
- interactive product;
- data interface;
- form.


## 14.45 — PERFORMANCE BUDGET ASSERTIONS

CI should enforce resource budgets from Part 12.

Examples:

JS count/size CSS size image size font transfer LCP regression CLS regression

Budgets can be stored in version control.

Lighthouse CI supports explicit budget assertions rather than relying only on an overall performance score.


## 14.46 — SCORE POLICY

Do not block solely because:

```text
Performance = 89 rather than 90
```

if actual metrics satisfy our defined budgets.

Prefer metric-level gates.

Suggested Lighthouse score floors may exist as smoke indicators:

Accessibility ≥ 95 Best Practices ≥ 95 SEO ≥ 95

but semantic/manual tests remain authoritative.


## 14.47 — BUNDLE REGRESSION

PR adds:

+45 KB JavaScript

for one tiny interaction?

Requires explicit review.

The default response is not:

still under arbitrary global budget, ship it.

Ask why.


### GATE 07 — SEO / GEO / CRAWL


## 14.48 — CRAWL THE PRODUCTION BUILD

Use a local/CI crawler to verify:

- status;
- title;
- description;
- canonical;
- H1;
- robots;
- internal links;
- JSON-LD;
- image alts.

No human manually opening ten source files as the only QA.


## 14.49 — CORE SEO ASSERTIONS

For every canonical route:

status = 200 indexable = expected canonical self title unique description unique one H1 canonical host = www structured data parses internal links valid


## 14.50 — ENTITY ASSERTIONS

Production HTML/JSON-LD must satisfy:

NO physical LocalBusiness Colomiers NO Colomiers business address real Minimes facts attributed to Minimes real Portet facts attributed to Portet


## 14.51 — SITEMAP ASSERTION

Sitemap contains expected indexables only.

No:

- query strings;
- redirects;
- preview;
- noindex;
- API;

404.


## 14.52 — ROBOTS ASSERTION

Production robots:

- public crawl allowed;
- sitemap correct;
- SearchBot policy correct;
- training bot policy matches chosen business setting.


## 14.53 — MACHINE ANSWER SMOKE TEST

Manually inspect rendered page content and test current answer/search engines using questions established in Part 10.

We are not demanding immediate rankings.

We are checking semantic confusion.


### GATE 08 — SECURITY & PRIVACY


## 14.54 — CONTACT SECURITY TESTS

Attempt:

- missing Origin where relevant;
- foreign Origin;
- huge body;
- unexpected field;
- malformed email;
- rate-limit burst;
- honeypot;
- HTML/script payload;
- header injection in form fields.

Expected:

safe rejection/sanitization.

No mail-header injection.

No script execution.


## 14.55 — CSP TEST

Start in report-only during production-like QA.

Audit violations.

Then enforce.

No “fix” consisting of:

script-src *


## 14.56 — CONSENT NETWORK TEST

Browser network inspector automated/manual:


### Before decision

no consent-required GA/Ads request.


### Reject

still none.


### Accept

expected analytics requests appear.


### Withdraw

future tracking ceases according to implementation.


## 14.57 — PII NETWORK TEST

Submit form containing unique marker strings.

Inspect:

- GA request;
- analytics payload;
- error monitoring;
- logs;
- URLs.

Marker must appear only where necessary for contact delivery.

Never analytics.


### GATE 09 — CONTENT / EDITORIAL


## 14.58 — HUMAN FRENCH PASS

Final content is proofread by a fluent human reader.

Check:

- spelling;
- accents;
- punctuation;
- brand names;
- discipline terminology;
- line breaks;
- tone;
- factual precision.

AI spellcheck assists.

It does not replace editorial review.


## 14.59 — COPY DUPLICATION PASS

Compare route pages.

Look for paragraph/section cloning.

If pages have become:

same template + different discipline noun

return to Part 08.


## 14.60 — NON-CLAIM PASS

Search for:

meilleur n°1 unique leader le plus grand premium

Each occurrence reviewed.

Not automatically banned if truly supported, but default burden is high.


## 14.61 — MEDIA PASS

Every meaningful photo:

- provenance known;
- correct club;
- crop correct;
- alt correct;
- caption correct where needed;
- rights/use status accepted.

No photo is accepted because:

it looks like Portet.


### GATE 10 — PRODUCTION SMOKE


## 14.62 — AFTER DEPLOY

Do not assume CI equals production.

Immediately verify live:

HTTPS redirects canonical host robots sitemap homepage major route 404 contact endpoint images fonts CSP analytics consent external links


## 14.63 — SEARCH-ENGINE SETUP

Production:

- Google Search Console property;
- sitemap submission;
- Bing Webmaster;
- IndexNow;
- priority URL inspection.

No staging submission.


## 14.64 — PRODUCTION CONTACT TEST

Send one clearly marked real QA message.

Confirm:

- frontend success;
- recipient receives it;
- subject/context correct;
- no encoding corruption;
- no PII analytics leak.

Then delete test message according to operational practice.


## 14.65 — PRODUCTION MOBILE TEST

Physical phone.

Not only DevTools.

Check:

- network;
- scroll;
- sticky UI;
- navigation;
- selector;
- recommendation;
- form.


## 14.66 — RELEASE EVIDENCE PACK

Every production release should be able to produce:

CI result Playwright result axe result Lighthouse result bundle report claim validation result SEO crawl result visual diff review production smoke result

For major launches, retain this as release evidence.

That is what “Baffled Bar approved” means operationally.


## 14.67 — TEST FAILURE TRACES

Playwright's Trace Viewer can retain DOM snapshots, screenshots, network activity and action history for failed tests, making it useful for reproducible debugging.

Recommended:

```text
trace: on-first-retry
```

in CI.

No need to save huge traces for every successful run indefinitely.


## 14.68 — RELEASE BRANCH POLICY

Recommended:

```text
main = production-ready
```

Feature work:

short-lived branches/PRs.

No:

main contains known broken work

with someone remembering not to deploy.


## 14.69 — PR REQUIREMENTS

Before merge:

typecheck lint unit build claim validation E2E smoke axe visual where affected bundle budgets

Full matrix may be optimized for CI cost, but release runs complete suite.


## 14.70 — VISUAL-CHANGE PR

If component appearance changes:

requires screenshot diff.

If factual data changes:

requires claim/source diff.

If route changes:

requires SEO/canonical test.

Different changes require different evidence.


## 14.71 — CHANGE CLASSIFICATION

Every change belongs to one category:

CONTENT FACT DESIGN CODE SEO SECURITY INFRASTRUCTURE

A change may have several tags.

This determines which tests become mandatory.


## 14.72 — FACT CHANGE

Examples:

price hours discipline availability.

Triggers:

claim validation affected pages machine docs recommendation logic schema visual check IndexNow


## 14.73 — DESIGN CHANGE

Triggers:

visual regression accessibility mobile performance


## 14.74 — ROUTE/SEO CHANGE

Triggers:

redirect canonical sitemap internal links Search Console migration check


## 14.75 — DEPENDENCY CHANGE

Triggers:

- build;
- unit;
- E2E;
- performance;
- bundle size;
- security audit.

A major Astro upgrade receives dedicated upgrade review.


## 14.76 — ROLLBACK STRATEGY

The previous production deployment remains quickly restorable through the hosting platform/release system.

Rollback is appropriate for:

- broken form;
- critical visual regression;
- incorrect data;
- SEO indexation accident;
- security defect.

Do not debug for hours in production while a known-good version exists.


## 14.77 — DATA CORRECTION VS CODE ROLLBACK

If:

price is wrong

but code is fine:

fix claim + redeploy.

If:

renderer corrupts all prices:

rollback code.

Separate truth incident from software incident.


## 14.78 — INCIDENT SEVERITY


### SEV 0 — CRITICAL TRUTH / SECURITY

Examples:

- fake Colomiers address;
- exposed secret;
- form data leak;
- wrong widespread price;
- site compromised.

Immediate removal/rollback.


### SEV 1 — CORE FUNCTION FAILURE

- contact broken;
- selector unusable;
- site inaccessible mobile;
- production noindex.

Immediate correction.


### SEV 2 — MAJOR QUALITY

- main animation broken;
- schedule stale;
- serious accessibility issue.

High priority.


### SEV 3 — POLISH

- tiny spacing;
- non-critical visual mismatch.

Normal queue.


## 14.79 — MAINTENANCE MODEL

A website is not “finished forever.”

Maintenance follows volatility.


## 14.80 — WEEKLY / HIGH-FREQUENCY CHECK

Operationally, for highly volatile periods:

- offer status;
- schedule data if manually maintained;
- contact delivery errors;
- uptime/errors.

Not every page needs rewriting.


## 14.81 — MONTHLY REVIEW

Review:

- claim-expiry warnings;
- broken external links;
- search visibility;
- no-match rate;
- contact conversion;
- accessibility automated scan;
- performance field data;
- dependencies/security advisories.


## 14.82 — QUARTERLY REVIEW

Fuller:

- physical club facts;

- coach/facility claims;
- structured data;
- crawler policies;
- privacy/cookie scan;
- visual regression baseline relevance;
- content duplication;
- competitor/local SERP shifts.


## 14.83 — ANNUAL STRATEGIC REVIEW

Ask:

Does Colomiers still need to exist in this form?

If real Colomiers club opened:

architecture changes fundamentally.

If network destinations changed:

orientation product changes.

Do not keep a strategy alive because a repository exists.


## 14.84 — MAINTENANCE DOES NOT MEAN “UPDATE DATE”

Do not alter page timestamps without material re-verification.

Part 09/10 remains authoritative.


## 14.85 — PART 14 +1


### QUALITY GATES FOLLOW CLAIM IMPACT.

Most QA systems treat every change equally.

Ours understands:

changing padding ≠ changing MMA availability ≠ changing Colomiers physical-location truth

The more consequential the claim, the more gates it triggers.

That is a much more intelligent release system.


## 14.86 — PART 14 LOCKED PRINCIPLE

**Nothing is called Baffled Bar because it looked impressive in staging. It earns** **that label only after truth, interaction, accessibility, performance, machine** **understanding, security and production behavior all survive their own hostile** **test.**


<a id="part-15-astro-implementation-contract-repository-architecture"></a>

# PART 15 — Astro Implementation Contract & Repository Architecture


## 15.0 — PURPOSE

Part 15 is written for the implementation LLM/developer.

Its purpose is to eliminate re-decisions.

The implementation agent is **not** being asked:

design a Boxing Center Colomiers website.

That decision has already been made.

Its task is:


### IMPLEMENT THIS SYSTEM FAITHFULLY.

The blueprint is the authority.


## 15.1 — IMPLEMENTATION ORDER OF AUTHORITY

When instructions conflict:

1. VERIFIED REAL-WORLD TRUTH / CLAIM REGISTRY 2. MASTER BLUEPRINT 3. OFFICIAL BOXING CENTER BRAND ASSETS 4. COMPONENT/DATA CONTRACTS 5. PAGE-SPECIFIC SPEC 6. IMPLEMENTATION CONVENIENCE

Convenience never outranks truth/design-system decisions.


## 15.2 — TECHNICAL BASELINE

As of 7 September 2026, Node **24.20.0** is the latest LTS release, while Node 26 is Current; production apps should use LTS branches.

Therefore baseline:

Node 24 LTS Astro 6 stable TypeScript strict pnpm

Exact patch versions are pinned in:

package.json pnpm-lock.yaml .nvmrc


## 15.3 — WHY ASTRO 6

Current Astro 6 supports:

- static-by-default rendering;
- individual on-demand routes;
- typed environment variables;
- content-loader/data validation;
- modern View Transition support;
- testing integration.

This matches our architecture almost exactly.


## 15.4 — OUTPUT MODE

Locked:

```text
output: 'static'
```

All pages pre-render by default.

Only the contact submission endpoint:

```text
export const prerender = false;
```

Astro explicitly supports combining static pages with selected on-demand routes once a server adapter is installed.


## 15.5 — DEPLOYMENT ADAPTER

This is one of the few intentionally provider-dependent implementation decisions.

The architecture must use a **first-party Astro adapter appropriate to the actual production** **host**.

Astro currently supports provider adapters including Cloudflare, Netlify, Node and Vercel for on-demand rendering.

The implementation agent must **not** restructure application logic around proprietary provider APIs unless essential.

Use Astro abstractions for:

- request;
- environment;
- endpoint;
- redirects

where possible.

This preserves portability.


## 15.6 — ENVIRONMENT ABSTRACTION

Use:

```text
astro:env
```

for typed environment variables.

Astro's current env API differentiates client/public/server/secret variables and ensures server secrets are not exposed to browser bundles.

No scattered:

process.env.X

through application code.


## 15.7 — REPOSITORY ROOT

Locked proposed repository:

```text
boxingcenter-colomiers/
│
├── .github/
│ └── workflows/
│ ├── ci.yml
│ └── production-smoke.yml
│
├── docs/
│ ├── MASTER-BLUEPRINT.md
│ ├── DECISIONS.md
│ ├── CLAIM-SOURCES.md
│ ├── RELEASE-CHECKLIST.md
│ └── OPEN-QUESTIONS.md
│
├── public/
│ ├── favicon.ico
│ ├── favicon.svg
│ ├── apple-touch-icon.png
│ └── og/
│ ├── home.jpg
│ ├── mma.jpg
│ ├── boxe-anglaise.jpg
│ ├── boxe-enfants.jpg
│ └── ...
│
├── src/
│ ├── assets/
│ ├── components/
│ ├── content/
│ ├── data/
│ ├── layouts/
│ ├── lib/
│ ├── pages/
│ ├── scripts/
│ ├── styles/
│ ├── content.config.ts
│ └── env.d.ts
│
├── tests/
│ ├── e2e/
│ ├── unit/
│ ├── accessibility/
│ ├── visual/
│ └── fixtures/
│
├── scripts/
│ ├── validate-data.ts
│ ├── validate-build.ts
│ ├── check-links.ts
│ ├── check-copy.ts
│ ├── check-bundles.ts
│ └── submit-indexnow.ts
│
├── astro.config.mjs
├── playwright.config.ts
├── vitest.config.ts
├── tsconfig.json
├── package.json
├── pnpm-lock.yaml
├── .nvmrc
├── .env.example
├── .gitignore
└── README.md
```


## 15.8 — docs/ IS PART OF THE PRODUCT

The final master blueprint belongs in the repository.

The implementation agent should not repeatedly reopen dozens of research/reference sources.

It reads:

MASTER-BLUEPRINT.md DECISIONS.md OPEN-QUESTIONS.md

and works from locked conclusions.

This is exactly the context-efficiency objective we identified at the beginning.


## 15.9 — DECISIONS.md

A compact, implementation-facing extraction of immutable choices.

Example:

NO physical Colomiers club. NO LocalBusiness schema for Colomiers. Astro static first. No React by default. No animation library by default. Route visual = SVG first. Red = resolved decision. No SaaS card system. No generic dark/red gym look. No promo modal. No fake live routing.

This file prevents repeated strategic interpretation.


## 15.10 — OPEN-QUESTIONS.md

Only genuinely unresolved implementation blockers.

Not an idea backlog.

When resolved:

move decision into appropriate registry/doc and remove question.


## 15.11 — ASSET TREE

```text
src/assets/
├── brand/
│ ├── boxing-center-logo.svg
│ └── approved-variants/
│
├── minimes/
│ ├── environment/
│ ├── boxing/
│ ├── coaches/
│ └── children/
│
├── portet/
│ ├── environment/
│ ├── mma/
│ ├── boxing/
│ └── children/
│
└── editorial/
└── non-documentary/
```

Documentary assets stay physically grouped by origin.

This alone helps prevent location leakage.


## 15.12 — MEDIA MANIFEST

Every used asset has a registry entry.

```text
interface MediaAsset {
id: string;
sourcePath: string;
locationId?: ClubId;
alt: string;
caption?: string;
role: MediaRole;
focalPoint?: {
x: number;
y: number;
```

}; rightsStatus: 'approved' | 'pending'; }

Build fails if:

```text
rightsStatus = pending
```

for public production use.


## 15.13 — DATA DIRECTORY

```text
src/data/
├── entities/
│ ├── organization.ts
│ ├── colomiers.ts
│ ├── minimes.ts
│ └── portet.ts
│
├── claims/
│ ├── network.ts
│ ├── minimes.ts
│ ├── portet.ts
│ └── colomiers.ts
│
├── disciplines/
│ └── index.ts
│
├── offers/
│ └── index.ts
│
├── schedules/
│ ├── minimes.ts
│ └── portet.ts
│
├── media/
│ └── index.ts
│
├── routes/
│ ├── metadata.ts
│ ├── internal-links.ts
│ └── redirects.ts
│
└── analytics/
└── events.ts
```


## 15.14 — WHY TYPESCRIPT FOR FACT REGISTRIES

These are not editorial documents.

They are executable truth.

Use:

```text
export const portet = {
...
} satisfies Club;
```

This gives:

- type checking;
- references;
- IDE safety;
- controlled enum values.


## 15.15 — ZOD RUNTIME VALIDATION

TypeScript checks authoring.

Zod/build validation checks actual loaded data.

The two are complementary.

Astro's content loader/content layer likewise supports schema-based validation of loaded content.


## 15.16 — CLAIM TYPE

```text
export type ClaimState =
| 'verified'
| 'time-sensitive'
| 'pending'
| 'conflicted'
| 'prohibited';
export interface Claim<T> {
id: string;
subjectId: string;
predicate: string;
value: T | null;
state: ClaimState;
sources: ClaimSource[];
verifiedAt?: string;
reviewAfter?: string;
public: boolean;
allowedSurfaces?: ClaimSurface[];
forbiddenInferences?: string[];
}
```


## 15.17 — CLAIM SOURCE

```text
export interface ClaimSource {
id: string;
type:
| 'client-brief'
| 'official-site'
| 'direct-confirmation'
| 'official-network'
| 'federation'
| 'internal-verified';
reference: string;
checkedAt: string;
}
```

No source:

`ChatGPT`.

No source:

`Google result snippet`.


## 15.18 — CLAIM ACCESS API

UI does not rummage through raw arrays.

Provide:

getPublicClaim() getVerifiedClaim() getClubClaims() getClaimState() assertPublishableClaim()

Centralize behavior.


## 15.19 — PROHIBITED-CLAIM API

Example:

assertNoPhysicalColomiersInference()

may sound excessive.

It is not.

It protects the single most important project constraint.


## 15.20 — CONTENT LAYER

Long-form editorial sections can live in:

```text
src/content/
├── boxe-anglaise/
├── mma/
├── boxing-fitness/
├── boxe-enfants/
└── premiere-seance/
```

or a single route-oriented collection.

Use Astro's content collection/loading system with a defined schema.

Astro 6's loader API supports local Markdown/MDX/JSON/YAML/TOML and schema validation.


## 15.21 — CONTENT FRONTMATTER

Example:

title: "..." description: "..." route: "/mma/" primaryIntent: "mma-colomiers" reviewedAt: "2026-09-07" claimDependencies: - "portet.discipline.mma" - "portet.facility.cage"


## 15.22 — NO VOLATILE FACTS IN MARKDOWN

Bad:

Portet est ouvert jusqu'à 21h30.

Better:

<OpeningHours clubId="portet" />

or dynamic factual interpolation.

Editorial content says:

Consulte les horaires vérifiés ci-dessous.


## 15.23 — CONTENT ESCAPE HATCH

Some static historical explanation is fine in Markdown.

Anything likely to change:

data registry.

This distinction is mandatory.


## 15.24 — PAGES

Locked route files:

```text
src/pages/
├── index.astro
├── boxe-anglaise.astro
├── mma.astro
├── boxing-fitness.astro
├── boxe-enfants.astro
├── premiere-seance.astro
├── quel-club.astro
├── plannings.astro
├── tarifs.astro
├── contact.astro
├── mentions-legales.astro
├── confidentialite.astro
├── contact/
│ └── merci.astro
├── api/
│ └── contact.ts
├── robots.txt.ts
├── llms.txt.ts
├── llms-full.txt.ts
└── 404.astro
```

Use trailing-slash configuration consistently.


## 15.25 — STATIC MACHINE ENDPOINTS

```text
robots.txt.ts
llms.txt.ts
llms-full.txt.ts
```

can be generated at build time from configuration/registry.

No hand-edited duplicate files.


## 15.26 — CONTACT ENDPOINT

```text
export const prerender = false;
```

Astro supports server endpoints in an otherwise static project when an adapter is installed.


## 15.27 — CONTACT ZERO-JS CONTRACT

Form:

```text
<form
method="POST"
action="/api/contact"
>
```

works without client JavaScript.

Server:


### Valid

send → `303` → /contact/merci/


### Invalid

return accessible error response or controlled redirect/fallback.

Enhanced JS may provide inline validation/AJAX-like feedback, but server behavior remains complete.


## 15.28 — WHY NOT ASTRO ACTIONS FOR THE LAUNCH FORM?

Astro Actions are excellent and currently provide type-safe form/JSON validation through Zod.

However, direct zero-JS form actions require an on-demand-rendered page when used through Astro's Action form mechanism.

Because we deliberately keep `/contact/` static, a dedicated server endpoint is the cleaner launch architecture.

Actions remain a valid future refactor if contact becomes a more dynamic application surface.

## 15.30 — NO REACT AT LAUNCH

Locked default:


### ZERO REACT.

Astro + semantic HTML + scoped/global CSS + vanilla TypeScript.

Reason:

none of the launch product systems inherently requires React.


## 15.31 — INTERACTION CONTROLLERS

```text
src/scripts/
├── intent-selector.ts
├── proximity-engine.ts
├── schedule-explorer.ts
├── mobile-navigation.ts
├── contact-form.ts
├── consent.ts
└── analytics.ts
```

Each enhances existing HTML.

No client application root.


## 15.32 — CUSTOM ELEMENTS

For state-heavy isolated controls, custom elements are permitted.

Example:

<bc-proximity-engine>

if that materially encapsulates:

- state;
- setup;
- cleanup.

But custom element usage is not mandatory everywhere.


## 15.33 — PROXIMITY ENGINE CORE

Pure logic:

```text
src/lib/recommendation/
├── match.ts
├── explain.ts
├── normalize.ts
├── types.ts
└── public-data.ts
```

Browser controller calls pure `match()`.

Unit tests call same function.

No duplicated engine logic in UI.


## 15.34 — PUBLIC MATCH DATA

Generate at build:

```text
const publicMatchData = projectVerifiedClubFacts(...)
```

Only browser-safe facts.

Never serialize:

- source internal notes;
- private contact;
- draft claims;
- disputed claims.


## 15.35 — SEO LIBRARY

```text
src/lib/seo/
├── metadata.ts
├── canonicals.ts
├── structured-data.ts
├── breadcrumbs.ts
├── robots.ts
├── sitemap.ts
└── machine-docs.ts
```


## 15.36 — NO PAGE-LOCAL JSON-LD

Forbidden:

```text
<script type="application/ld+json">
{
"@type": ...
}
</script>
```

manually created on individual pages.

Pages request a schema profile.

Builder constructs graph.


## 15.37 — ANALYTICS LIBRARY

```text
src/lib/analytics/
├── events.ts
├── adapter.ts
├── noop.ts
├── ga4.ts
├── consent.ts
└── utm.ts
```

UI does not import Google directly.


## 15.38 — FORM LIBRARY

```text
src/lib/forms/
├── contact-schema.ts
├── sanitize.ts
├── rate-limit.ts
├── delivery/
│ ├── index.ts
│ └── provider.ts
└── errors.ts
```


## 15.39 — STYLE ARCHITECTURE

```text
src/styles/
├── reset.css
├── tokens.css
├── typography.css
├── layout.css
├── utilities.css
├── focus.css
├── forms.css
│
├── route/
│ ├── graph.css

│
├── motion/
│ ├── tokens.css
│ ├── transitions.css
│ └── reduced-motion.css
│
└── global.css
```


## 15.40 — CSS LAYERS

Recommended:

```text
@layer reset, tokens, base, components, utilities;
```

Avoid specificity warfare.


## 15.41 — NO TAILWIND BY DEFAULT

The visual system is custom and relatively small.

CSS tokens + component styles are enough.

Do not introduce a utility framework merely because implementation agents know it quickly.

Baffled Bar should not trade maintainability for rapid templating.


## 15.42 — NO CSS-IN-JS

No reason.

Astro CSS is sufficient.


## 15.43 — DESIGN TOKEN AUTHORITY

`tokens.css` contains:

- palette;
- typography;
- spacing;
- radii;
- route;

- focus;
- motion.

Component CSS does not invent independent design systems.


## 15.44 — COMPONENT STYLES

Local component styling permitted.

But values reference tokens.

Bad:

border-radius: 28px; color: #e31e30;

without semantic reason.


## 15.45 — VIEW TRANSITIONS

Global cross-document View Transition enhancement in CSS/platform APIs.

No client router by default.

Part 06 remains authoritative.


## 15.46 — MOTION IMPLEMENTATION

Priority:

CSS WAAPI native View Transition SVG

No GSAP.

No Motion.

No Lenis.

Unless a later proven requirement passes the dependency gate.


## 15.47 — PACKAGE BASELINE

Production dependencies should remain intentionally small.

Expected core:

astro @astrojs/sitemap deployment adapter

Potential direct dependencies only where needed:

zod

if not satisfied through preferred Astro exports/workflow.

Image processor/adapters according to Astro deployment.


## 15.48 — DEVELOPMENT DEPENDENCIES

Likely:

typescript @astrojs/check vitest @playwright/test @axe-core/playwright

@lhci/cli eslint eslint-plugin-astro prettier prettier-plugin-astro

Version compatibility validated against current Astro version.

No dependency list is sacred if official tooling changes before implementation.

The architectural jobs are sacred.


## 15.49 — EXPLICITLY FORBIDDEN DEFAULT DEPENDENCIES

react react-dom framer-motion / motion gsap lenis three @react-three/fiber swiper slick-carousel a generic component framework a giant icon library jquery hotjar

They may enter only after a written justification.


## 15.50 — ICONS

Prefer:

- arrows as text/SVG primitives;

- a tiny curated local SVG set.

Do not import 500 icons to display six.


## 15.51 — ENV SCHEMA

Conceptual:

SITE_ENV= development | preview | production CONTACT_RECIPIENT= CONTACT_DELIVERY_PROVIDER= CONTACT_API_KEY= PUBLIC_ANALYTICS_MODE= off | consented PUBLIC_GA_MEASUREMENT_ID= ALLOW_GPTBOT= true | false INDEXNOW_KEY=

Only `PUBLIC_*` values are browser-safe.

Secrets server-only.


## 15.52 — ENV RULE

No “optional secret that fails only when a user submits.”

Production boot/deploy should validate required production secrets.


## 15.53 — .env.example

Contains:

KEY=

and documentation.

Never real values.


## 15.54 — CONFIGURATION

`astro.config.mjs` owns:

site output adapter trailingSlash sitemap security env schema build integration

No environment-specific hack scattered elsewhere.


## 15.55 — SITE ORIGIN

Locked production:

https://www.boxingcenter-colomiers.fr

from brief, unless explicit infrastructure decision changes before launch.


## 15.56 — astro.config HIGH-LEVEL SHAPE

Conceptually:

```text
export default defineConfig({
site: SITE_URL,
output: 'static',
adapter: deploymentAdapter(),
trailingSlash: 'always',
integrations: [
sitemap(...)
],
security: {
checkOrigin: true
},
env: {
schema: ...
}
});
```

Astro currently keeps `security.checkOrigin` enabled by default for on-demand mutation protection.

Do not disable it for convenience.


## 15.57 — ROUTE METADATA REGISTRY

```text
export const routeMetadata = {
home: {...},
mma: {...},
...
```

} satisfies RouteMetadataRegistry;

One location.

No `<title>` string typed in route page.


## 15.58 — ROUTE IDS

Stable internal enum:

home boxe_anglaise mma boxing_fitness boxe_enfants premiere_seance quel_club plannings tarifs contact

Analytics/internal links reference IDs.

URLs resolved through registry.


## 15.59 — NO MAGIC URL STRINGS

Bad:

navigate('/premiere-seance/');

Good:

routeUrl('premiere_seance');

for application logic where appropriate.

Astro templates can still use known route helper output.

This makes route migration safe.


## 15.60 — NETWORK SITE REGISTRY

```text
export const networkSites = {
minimes: {
canonicalUrl: ...
},
portet: {
canonicalUrl: ...
}
};
```

No external Boxing Center URL elsewhere.


## 15.61 — UTM BUILDER

Only sanctioned helper may append campaign attribution.

buildNetworkHandoffUrl({ clubId: 'portet', context: 'mma' });

Prevents malformed analytics taxonomy.


## 15.62 — TEST TREE

```text
tests/
├── unit/
│ ├── claims.test.ts
│ ├── recommendation.test.ts
│ ├── metadata.test.ts
│ ├── schema.test.ts
│ ├── urls.test.ts

│

│
└── visual/
├── home.spec.ts
├── decision.spec.ts
├── pages.spec.ts
└── states.spec.ts
```


## 15.63 — PLAYWRIGHT CONFIG

Use projects for:

Chromium Firefox WebKit mobile Chrome mobile Safari.

Playwright explicitly supports this project model.

Traces:

`on-first-retry`.

Screenshots:

on failure + deliberate visual snapshots.


## 15.64 — VISUAL BASELINES

Generated in the same pinned CI container/OS.

Playwright warns rendering can differ based on OS/hardware/browser environment, so visual baselines must not be casually generated across arbitrary local machines.


## 15.65 — CI PIPELINE

Concept:

INSTALL ↓ TYPECHECK ↓ LINT ↓ UNIT ↓ DATA VALIDATION ↓ BUILD ↓ BUILD OUTPUT VALIDATION ↓ PLAYWRIGHT ↓ AXE ↓ VISUAL ↓ LIGHTHOUSE BUDGET

Production deploy occurs only after required checks.


## 15.66 — PACKAGE SCRIPTS

Recommended:

{ "scripts": { "dev": "astro dev", "build": "...", "preview": "astro preview", "typecheck": "...", "lint": "...", "format": "...", "validate:data": "...", "validate:build": "...", "test": "...", "test:unit": "...", "test:e2e": "...", "test:a11y": "...", "test:visual": "...", "test:lighthouse": "...", "check": "..." } }

`check` runs the full local pre-PR suite that is sensible in one command.


## 15.67 — BUILD SCRIPT

Recommended logical sequence:

```text
validate:data
astro build
validate:build
```

No generation after validation that could introduce unchecked output.


## 15.68 — MACHINE DOC GENERATION

Build-time endpoint generators call:

buildLlmsTxt() buildLlmsFull() buildRobots()

from the same data sources.

No manual second truth.


## 15.69 — SITEMAP

`@astrojs/sitemap`.

Filter from route indexability registry.

Not separate hand-maintained URL list.


## 15.70 — REDIRECTS

Preferred infrastructure/platform redirects generated from:

```text
src/data/routes/redirects.ts
```

If adapter/provider format differs:

create build adapter function.

Do not manually duplicate redirect maps in platform UI and repo if avoidable.


## 15.71 — CONTENT SECURITY POLICY

Generate/deploy according to actual host.

No component adds unreviewed remote script domain.

Adding a third party requires:

privacy review performance review CSP update consent update docs update


## 15.72 — ANALYTICS

Analytics adapter initializes only after consent state permits.

Default:

NoopAnalytics

No hidden global `gtag()` calls.


## 15.73 — COMPONENT ANALYTICS

Components emit domain intent.

Example:

dispatchEvent( new CustomEvent('bc:intent-selected', { detail: { intent: 'mma' } }) );

A central analytics listener/adaptor converts to provider event if permitted.

UI remains provider-independent.


## 15.74 — CONSENT

Consent system state:

unknown necessary_only analytics_allowed marketing_allowed

At launch, if only GA measurement exists:

do not create marketing state unnecessarily.


## 15.75 — CONSENT STORAGE

First-party consent-preference storage.

No analytics script required to remember a refusal.


## 15.76 — OG ASSETS

Recommendation:

design actual OG assets intentionally and store under:

```text
public/og/
```

rather than adding a runtime OG-image generation service at launch.

Reason:

10-ish pages.

Manual art direction is stronger and simpler.


## 15.77 — NO HEADLESS CMS AT LAUNCH

There are:

- few canonical pages;
- controlled facts;
- strong Git workflow;
- highly structured truth requirements.

A CMS would add:

- auth;
- API;
- webhook;
- schema mapping;
- runtime/build dependency.

Do not add one before a real editorial operations need appears.


## 15.78 — FUTURE CMS GATE

A CMS becomes justified if:

- non-technical team needs frequent publishing;
- editorial page volume materially increases;
- schedule/offer operations require UI.

Even then:

claim registry semantics must survive.

The CMS does not become permission to free-type volatile facts everywhere.


## 15.79 — NO DATABASE AT LAUNCH

No user accounts.

No persistent recommendation profiles.

No internal lead database required.

Therefore:

no database.

The contact mail provider is enough.

Again:

complexity must be earned.


## 15.80 — NO AI API AT LAUNCH

No OpenAI/LLM runtime API.

The site itself is intelligent through:

- deterministic data;
- clear content;
- decision logic.

Adding conversational AI would increase:

- cost;
- latency;
- hallucination risk;
- privacy complexity.

No need.


## 15.81 — CODE STYLE

Prefer:

- small pure functions;
- explicit names;
- immutable data;
- narrow interfaces;
- no meta-framework abstraction layer over Astro.

No:

`utils.ts` with 90 unrelated functions.


## 15.82 — DIRECTORY RULE

If a concern grows beyond roughly several coherent files:

give it a folder.

But do not create enterprise architecture for ten routes.

The tree should remain understandable in one glance.


## 15.83 — COMMENTS

Comments explain:


### WHY.

Not:

// increment i i++;

Important architectural constraints deserve comments.

Example:

// Colomiers is a spatial content context, never a physical club entity.

Useful.


## 15.84 — IMPLEMENTATION LLM MAY IMPROVISE

Allowed:

- optical spacing refinement;
- exact SVG route geometry;
- small responsive decisions within locked principles;
- efficient TypeScript implementation;
- helper names;
- refactoring;
- browser API fallbacks;
- test implementation details.


## 15.85 — IMPLEMENTATION LLM MAY NOT IMPROVISE

Forbidden without explicit redesign decision:

new site positioning new color semantics new font strategy new navigation IA new SEO routes new city pages new promotions new business facts new prices new schedules new club claims new LocalBusiness Colomiers new animation library React app conversion fake geolocation new analytics trackers new third-party embeds new CTA hierarchy new rounded SaaS visual system


## 15.86 — IF SOMETHING IS MISSING

The implementation agent follows:

CAN I DERIVE IT FROM LOCKED SYSTEM? YES → implement. IS IT A VOLATILE BUSINESS FACT? YES → mark unresolved; do not invent. IS IT A VISUAL MICRO-DETAIL? YES → choose within design tokens. WOULD IT CHANGE STRATEGY/ENTITY/TRUTH? YES → stop that sub-decision and preserve safe fallback.

Do not hallucinate to keep momentum.


## 15.87 — VERTICAL-SLICE IMPLEMENTATION ORDER

Recommended implementation sequence:


### SLICE 1 — FOUNDATION

Astro tokens layout data registry SEO head header/footer home skeleton


### SLICE 2 — TRUTH SYSTEM

claims media club components structured data machine docs


### SLICE 3 — HOME CORE

hero truth disciplines destinations


### SLICE 4 — DECISION PRODUCT

intent proximity engine recommendation


### SLICE 5 — INNER ROUTES

discipline pages.


### SLICE 6 — PRACTICAL

planning pricing first session contact.


### SLICE 7 — MOTION

Only after static layouts work.


### SLICE 8 — ANALYTICS / CONSENT

After functional product exists.


### SLICE 9 — HARDENING

accessibility performance SEO security.


## 15.88 — WHY MOTION COMES AFTER STATIC

If motion begins before:

- hierarchy;
- content;
- spacing;
- semantic state

are correct, it hides structural weaknesses.

Static screenshot must already pass Baffled Bar.

Then movement intensifies it.


## 15.89 — IMPLEMENTATION +1


### THE REPOSITORY MIRRORS THE KNOWLEDGE GRAPH.

Traditional site architecture:

components pages assets

Ours:

entities claims evidence routes decision logic components pages machine outputs tests

The file system itself expresses:

what is true → how it is interpreted → how it is presented.

That makes implementation drift far harder.


## 15.90 — PART 15 LOCKED PRINCIPLE

**The implementation must be simple in runtime architecture and sophisticated** **in information architecture: mostly static Astro, minimal JavaScript, no** **unnecessary framework, no database, no AI API, no CMS, but an unusually** **rigorous typed truth graph powering every human and machine surface.**


<a id="part-16-final-reference-fusion-rejections-immutable-decisions-ultimate-baffled-bar-gate"></a>

# PART 16 — Final Reference Fusion, Rejections, Immutable Decisions & Ultimate Baffled-Bar Gate


## 16.0 — PURPOSE

Part 16 does four things:

1. prove the concept is not self-referential; 2. prove it is not a clone of external references; 3. record what has deliberately been rejected; 4. establish what implementation may never silently change.

This is the final strategic gate before consolidation.


## 16.1 — PROJECT THESIS, FINAL FORM

The project started with:

rank `boxingcenter-colomiers.fr` for boxing/MMA searches around Colomiers and redirect prospects toward nearby Boxing Center clubs.

That objective remains commercially valid.

But the correct product became:


### A LOCAL DECISION ENGINE FOR COMBAT SPORTS FROM COLOMIERS.

Not a virtual club.

Not a doorway.

Not an SEO shell.


## 16.2 — FINAL POSITIONING

**Boxingcenter-colomiers.fr helps someone starting from Colomiers** **understand what kind of combat-sport practice suits them, determine which** **real Boxing Center location fits that need, see when and how they can train,** **understand the first-session experience, and move confidently toward the** **correct physical club.**

The physical truth stays visible:


### COLOMIERS IS THE STARTING POINT. NOT THE FAKE DESTINATION.


## 16.3 — PROJECT +1 STACK

We now have multiple project-level innovations.


### +1.1 — PROXIMITY AS PRODUCT

The absence of a physical Colomiers club becomes the reason for the product to exist.


### +1.2 — THE RESOLVING ROUTE

A shared visual/information primitive begins as uncertainty and progressively converges toward a real decision.


### +1.3 — RED IS EARNED

Boxing Center red is not constant decorative branding.

It appears as decisions become supported.


### +1.4 — MOTION HAS MEMORY

The same route changes semantic role:

possibility preference recommendation time commitment.


### +1.5 — ENERGY CONSERVATION

As certainty increases:

the interface moves less.


### +1.6 — COMPONENTS KNOW WHY THEY ARE TRUE

Components consume verified claim IDs/entities, not free-written facts.


### +1.7 — NON-CLAIMS ARE DATA

The system stores facts that must **not** be inferred.


### +1.8 — QUESTIONS GET SHARPER

Editorial language progressively narrows the decision.


### +1.9 — RESOURCE PRIORITY FOLLOWS CERTAINTY

Prefetching can become more confident only as the user's path becomes more predictable.


### +1.10 — DATA IS EARNED

Analytics collection mirrors our visual philosophy:

collect only information required to improve a real decision.


## 16.4 — LIVE OUTSIDE-BANK REFERENCE REQUIREMENT

Fusion Baffled requires:


### AT LEAST 10 LIVE SOURCES OUTSIDE THE EXISTING REFERENCE BANK.

The following were live-checked for this project on 7 September 2026.

They are used for mechanisms.

Never for wholesale styling.


## 16.5 — LIVE EXTERNAL SOURCE 01 — ACTIVE THEORY

Active Theory describes its digital work as meticulously crafted in-house with emphasis on performance, efficiency and attention to detail. Its live site also currently presents a hard WebGL dependency message when graphics support is unavailable.


### BORROW

- integration of creative development and performance;
- confidence in advanced browser experiences.


### REJECT

- making the primary experience unavailable when WebGL support fails.


### BECOMES HERE

Our signature route can become advanced when capable, but semantic experience remains complete without it.


### +1

**Capability degrades; meaning does not.**


## 16.6 — LIVE EXTERNAL SOURCE 02 — MAKEMEPULSE

makemepulse currently describes its goal as creating immersive experiences with “tech that's light as air” and explicitly rejects technology merely because it is trendy.


### BORROW

- complexity that disappears behind usability;
- immersive craft with explicit purpose.


### BECOMES HERE

Proximity Engine looks simple despite a rigorous data/decision system underneath.


### +1

Recommendation explains why it is true rather than exposing technology.


## 16.7 — LIVE EXTERNAL SOURCE 03 — PHANTOM STUDIOS

Phantom presents itself as a technology-led creative agency combining immersive technology with user-centred design and measurable impact.


### BORROW

technology + user purpose rather than technology as a separate spectacle layer.


### REJECT

“AI/WebGL because agency capability.”


### BECOMES HERE

No AI runtime. No WebGL requirement. Technology only where the decision problem benefits.


## 16.8 — LIVE EXTERNAL SOURCE 04 — BUILD IN AMSTERDAM

Build in Amsterdam currently frames its work as emotionally driven but logically built, describes mobile-first design, and explicitly states that technologies such as 3D/AR should be used when they are a fitting addition to the brand.


### BORROW

- emotion + logic;
- mobile-first craft;
- technology must earn fit;
- physical/digital continuity.


### BECOMES HERE

Boxing photography carries emotion.

Decision architecture carries logic.

Mobile is independently composed.


### +1

The physical/digital bridge is not ecommerce—it is **orientation toward a real sports** **destination**.


## 16.9 — LIVE EXTERNAL SOURCE 05 — STINK STUDIOS

Stink Studios currently emphasizes that creative thinking and execution happen in-house and describes its digital roots as part of a unified brand/customer-experience practice.


### BORROW

the idea that concept and execution should not separate.


### BECOMES HERE

“The Route Is The Interface” survives:

strategy copy motion data SEO code.

It is not handed off as a visual motif after UX.


## 16.10 — LIVE EXTERNAL SOURCE 06 — MONOPO LONDON

Monopo London currently describes itself as design-driven, emphasizes trying a different starting point, injecting personality into interactions and challenging category codes rather than following template conventions.


### BORROW

challenge the category's default visual codes.


### BECOMES HERE

We deliberately reject:

black/red/fighter/smoke/gym-template language.


### +1

The category's geographic weakness—no Colomiers venue—becomes the central design mechanism.


## 16.11 — LIVE EXTERNAL SOURCE 07 — HELLO MONDAY

Hello Monday's live portfolio spans digital experiences, products, platforms and branding while maintaining a stated goal of making digital ideas that connect with audiences.


### BORROW

interactive character can coexist with product utility.


### REJECT

playfulness for its own sake.


### BECOMES HERE

The selector can feel alive without becoming a game.

The user's decision remains primary.


## 16.12 — LIVE EXTERNAL SOURCE 08 — DOGSTUDIO

Dogstudio currently positions its work at the intersection of art, design and technology and explicitly frames emotional experiences as a way to improve results rather than an end in themselves.


### BORROW

emotion tied to outcome.


### BECOMES HERE

First-session photography is not cinematic filler.

It reduces beginner uncertainty.

Visual emotion earns conversion value.


## 16.13 — LIVE EXTERNAL SOURCE 09 — ITOM

ITom's current 2026 portfolio uses a walkable, room-based 3D corridor and interactive doors as navigation, with the navigation metaphor forming the actual portfolio experience.


### BORROW

the strongest lesson:


### METAPHOR CAN BECOME NAVIGATION.


### REJECT

requiring visitors to inhabit a virtual world to reach ordinary information.


### BECOMES HERE

The route metaphor structures:

- hierarchy;
- state;
- transition;
- comparison;

while normal document navigation remains available.


### +1

Metaphor augments semantic HTML rather than replacing it.


## 16.14 — LIVE EXTERNAL SOURCE 10 — FILIP VAN HARREVELD

Filip van Harreveld's live site explicitly foregrounds performant interactive web experiences and combines practical commercial work with small interactive experiments.


### BORROW

creative interaction can remain performance-conscious and modular.


### BECOMES HERE

The Resolving Route is composed from small platform-native pieces rather than forcing one giant application runtime.


## 16.15 — EXTRA LIVE SOURCE — STUDIO DUMBAR

Studio Dumbar's live site continues to operate as a highly distilled portfolio/index surface across identity, culture, sports and technology projects.


### BORROW

confidence in reduction.


### BECOMES HERE

Not every section needs illustrative chrome.

Strong identity can live through:

```text
type
spacing
system behavior.
```


## 16.16 — EXTERNAL REFERENCE VERDICT

No external reference supplies:


### THE PRODUCT.

They contribute mechanisms such as:

- immersive focus;
- category-code rejection;
- integrated craft;
- tech restraint;
- metaphor/navigation;
- emotional utility;
- mobile-first thinking.

Our final mechanism remains specific to this business problem:


### COLOMIERS → INTENT → VERIFIED REAL CLUB.


## 16.17 — OWN-WORK FUSION

Internal references remain equally important.


### SAINT-CYPRIEN

Borrow:

- discipline choice;
- physical-space storytelling;
- beginner anxiety;
- transport/proximity thinking.

Do not copy:

six-station visual structure.


### MINIMES

Borrow:

- route ownership;
- planning utility;
- SEO/GEO factual architecture;
- non-claim discipline.

Correct:

- any stale machine facts;
- address/hours conflicts;
- duplicated source-of-truth risks.


### PORTET

Borrow:

- first-session storytelling;
- indexed coach/facility clarity;

round-based page framework.

## 16.25 — IMMUTABLE STRATEGIC DECISIONS

Unless real-world circumstances change:

1. No physical Boxing Center is represented in Colomiers. 2. Colomiers is origin/context. 3. Minimes/Portet are real destinations. 4. Site provides standalone decision value. 5. No doorway architecture. 6. Homepage owns broad local intent. 7. Each canonical route owns a distinct problem. 8. Proximity Engine is transparent/deterministic. 9. No fake routing precision. 10.No opaque AI scoring.


## 16.26 — IMMUTABLE EXPERIENCE DECISIONS

11.The Route Is The Interface. 12.The Resolving Route is the project's signature mechanism. 13.Red Is Earned. 14.More certainty means less movement. 15.Native scrolling. 16.No pointer-only function. 17.Mobile transforms rather than hides. 18.First-session anxiety is a first-class product problem. 19.Page progression goes uncertainty → certainty. 20.Destination handoff is explicit.


## 16.27 — IMMUTABLE VISUAL DECISIONS

21.Official Boxing Center logo stays official. 22.Boxing Center navy anchors identity. 23.Signal red represents active resolution. 24.No red saturation across whole UI. 25.Physical intensity comes through real photography. 26.Interface supplies precision. 27.No generic fight-night aesthetic. 28.No SaaS-card aesthetic. 29.Low-radius/linear geometry.

30.Route line only appears when encoding meaning. 31.Documentary imagery cannot be fabricated. 32.Location-specific imagery is labeled appropriately.


## 16.28 — IMMUTABLE CONTENT DECISIONS

33.Main experience uses respectful tutoiement. 34.Facts before adjectives. 35.Certainty cannot exceed evidence. 36.No fake localism. 37.Answer first. 38.No keyword walls. 39.No generic AI gym voice. 40.No unsupported superlatives. 41.Parent safety facts precede inspiration. 42.Terms are explained for beginners. 43.Experienced practitioners receive precise information. 44.AI may transform verified truth, never source it.


## 16.29 — IMMUTABLE SEO/GEO DECISIONS

45.No LocalBusiness for Colomiers. 46.`spatialCoverage`/content context handles Colomiers relationship. 47.Physical club structured data belongs only to real clubs. 48.One canonical URL per intent. 49.Filter states are not SEO page factories. 50.Visible HTML is canonical information. 51.JSON-LD confirms visible truth. 52.Machine docs derive from same truth. 53.OAI-SearchBot remains allowed for desired search discoverability. 54.GPTBot is a separate business-policy choice. 55.Sitemap contains canonical indexables only. 56.Staging cannot index.

57.No stale Vercel/preview domains.


## 16.30 — IMMUTABLE TECHNICAL DECISIONS

58.Astro static-first. 59.Only genuinely dynamic endpoints run on demand. 60.No React at launch. 61.No animation framework by default. 62.SVG route first. 63.CSS/platform APIs before library. 64.No CMS at launch. 65.No database at launch. 66.No AI API at launch. 67.Claim registry is central. 68.Fact components do not accept arbitrary free-written business claims. 69.TypeScript strict. 70.Runtime data validation. 71.Tests are part of release definition.


## 16.31 — IMMUTABLE ACCESSIBILITY/PERFORMANCE/PRIVAC Y DECISIONS

72.WCAG 2.2 AA is release floor. 73.Focus is deliberately designed. 74.Reduced motion is first-class. 75.320px and 400% zoom are supported. 76.Core meaning survives JavaScript failure. 77.Performance budgets are enforced. 78.Third parties are non-essential. 79.GA4 is consent-gated under recommended launch model. 80.Reject is as easy as accept. 81.No PII enters analytics.

82.No session replay. 83.No fingerprinting. 84.No persistent recommendation profile. 85.Analytics measures decisions, not identities.
